paired targets share no root-free witness; in the half-window language, a
primitive denominator common to both paired targets is an endpoint-pair charge.
More generally, an \((L+1)\)-block of consecutive shifted paired residuals at
window \(t+r-1\) intersects exactly in the ordinary paired residual at depth
\(r+L\).  Thus consecutive paired-residual overlap is paid at the deepest pair,
not once per adjacent shift.
If a finite ladder begins before the half-window range, there is also a sharp
cutoff: once \(h\le t+r\) first holds, every later root-free residual is already
contained in one of the four residual families at that first half-window rung.
The primitive-denominator charge for the half-window tail is therefore paid
once at the cutoff; only the earlier pre-half-window rungs remain longer-Pade
targets.
This gives a mixed-ladder closure ledger: after fixed-root/root-slice charges,
charge the pre-half-window longer-Pade residual families, charge the four
primitive denominator targets at the first half-window rung when it exists, and
then charge only the bottom finite parameter systems, of total size at most
\(2h\) on the remaining ledger.  The consecutive common-image part introduces
neither a half-window-depth multiplier nor a projective image-line multiplier.
The cutoff control is also denominator-theoretic: for any one of the four
residual families, every deeper half-window primitive denominator is divisible
by the primitive denominator at the first half-window rung.  Thus deeper tail
denominators are refinements inside the cutoff multiplier ledger, not new
primitive bases.  Over \(\F_q\), if the cutoff primitive denominator has
degree \(\delta\), the whole half-window tail for that family has at most
\((q^{h-\delta}-1)/(q-1)\) ambient projective certificate-denominator
classes, and hence at most that many primitive denominator classes, before the
deeper truncation equations are imposed.  This is independent of the number of
half-window depths.  Summed over the four residual families, the
family-labelled tail budget is
\[
  \sum_F {q^{h-\delta_F}-1\over q-1}
  \le 4{q^h-1\over q-1},
\]
where absent cutoff families contribute zero.  This is a depth-independent
denominator-class budget for the half-window tail.  The ordinary and shifted
paired tails also have controlled overlap: any projective
certificate-denominator class appearing in both is already an endpoint-pair
certificate at the first half-window rung, so after the endpoint-pair residual
is charged their intersection contributes no new tail budget.  Scalar-paired
tail intersections are likewise explicit: they are one-sided cutoff endpoint
residuals, with one endpoint in the longer \(t+r\) window and the other in the
paired \(t+r-1\) window.  After these one-sided residuals are charged,
scalar-paired intersections also contribute no new unlabelled tail
certificate-denominator classes.  Finally, the two scalar tails
intersect only through the same endpoint-pair residual.  Hence the four-family
tail budget now has an explicit pairwise-overlap classification: scalar-scalar
and paired-paired overlaps are endpoint-pair charges, while scalar-paired
overlaps are one-sided endpoint charges.  Once those named overlap residuals
and their multiplier ledgers are charged, the remaining half-window tail is
family-disjoint: every uncharged projective certificate-denominator class, and
hence every primitive denominator class, has a unique residual-family label, so
the depth-independent family sum is an ordinary disjoint denominator ledger.
The named cutoff overlap charges are themselves half-window Pade ledgers.  At
the cutoff depth \(r_{\rm hw}\), the endpoint-pair residual uses the paired
parent \((u,v)\) at window \(t+r_{\rm hw}\), and the four one-sided residuals
use either the paired parent \((u,v)\) or the shifted paired parent \((Su,Sv)\)
at window \(t+r_{\rm hw}-1\).  Thus each active overlap system has one parent
primitive vector denominator and an explicit projective multiplier ledger of
size at most
\((q^{h-\delta}-1)/(q-1)\); the four one-sided systems may impose one extra
linear row on that multiplier ledger.  Crude summation gives at most
\(5(q^h-1)/(q-1)\) ambient projective certificate-denominator classes for all
cutoff overlap charges before those extra rows are imposed.
This overlap budget is not independent of the family budget.  Each overlap
denominator is divisible by the least common multiple of the primitive
denominators for the cutoff families it intersects: the endpoint-pair overlap
uses \(\operatorname{lcm}(D_u,D_v,D_{uv},D_S)\), while the four one-sided
overlaps use \(\operatorname{lcm}(D_u,D_{uv})\),
\(\operatorname{lcm}(D_v,D_{uv})\), \(\operatorname{lcm}(D_u,D_S)\), and
\(\operatorname{lcm}(D_v,D_S)\).  Thus the overlap ledgers are intersection
ledgers of the existing family primitives, and their multiplier counts improve
with the lcm degree.
There is also a locator-space row-cut dichotomy for the four one-sided
overlaps.  Inside the ordinary paired parent kernel
\(\ker H_s(u)\cap\ker H_s(v)\), the systems \((u\mid u,v)\) and
\((v\mid u,v)\) add respectively the missing rows \(\operatorname{row}_s(u)\)
and \(\operatorname{row}_s(v)\).  Inside the shifted paired parent
\(\ker H_s(Su)\cap\ker H_s(Sv)\), the systems \((u\mid Su,Sv)\) and
\((v\mid Su,Sv)\) add the missing rows \(\operatorname{row}_0(u)\) and
\(\operatorname{row}_0(v)\).  If the added row is nonzero on the parent
kernel, the one-sided overlap loses one projective dimension; otherwise the
parent paired kernel is already endpoint-persistent in that direction.
This persistence alternative is itself a concrete stacked-Hankel row-span
condition: the missing row must lie in the row span of the paired parent
matrix.  The endpoint-pair overlap has the two-row version: its codimension
inside \(\ker H_s(u)\cap\ker H_s(v)\) is the rank of the two restricted
endpoint rows \(\operatorname{row}_s(u)\) and \(\operatorname{row}_s(v)\).
Hence the full endpoint-pair ledger loses two dimensions unless those two rows
are dependent modulo the ordinary paired parent row span.
Writing \(d_{uv}=\dim(\ker H_s(u)\cap\ker H_s(v))\), \(d_S=\dim(\ker H_s(Su)\cap
\ker H_s(Sv))\), and letting \(r_{EP}\) and the four \(\epsilon\)'s denote
these restricted endpoint-row ranks, the whole cutoff-overlap projective
locator budget is bounded by
\[
  \Phi(d_{uv}-r_{EP})+\Phi(d_{uv}-\epsilon_u)+\Phi(d_{uv}-\epsilon_v)
  +\Phi(d_S-\epsilon_{uS})+\Phi(d_S-\epsilon_{vS}),
  \qquad \Phi(m)={q^m-1\over q-1}.
\]
Root-free certificate-denominator classes are bounded by the same expression.
Combining this with the lcm multiplier ledgers gives a termwise hybrid
overlap budget: for each overlap system one may take the minimum of the
family-lcm multiplier count and the endpoint-row rank count.  For example, the
endpoint-pair summand is
\[
  \min\!\left\{\Phi(h-\deg \operatorname{lcm}(D_u,D_v,D_{uv},D_S)),
               \Phi(d_{uv}-r_{EP})\right\},
\]
and the four one-sided summands have the analogous lcm/rank minima.  Thus a
large lcm degree and a genuine endpoint-row cut are independent ways to shrink
the same cutoff-overlap charge.
On the rank side, the endpoint-pair residual is not a fifth independent
summand: it is contained in each one-sided overlap space.  Thus the projective
rank budget for the union of cutoff overlap systems can be sharpened by
inclusion-exclusion to
\[
  \Phi(d_{uv}-\epsilon_u)+\Phi(d_{uv}-\epsilon_v)-\Phi(d_{uv}-r_{EP})
 +\Phi(d_S-\epsilon_{uS})+\Phi(d_S-\epsilon_{vS})-\Phi(d_S-r_S),
\]
where \(r_S\) is the rank of the two missing lower endpoint rows on the shifted
paired parent kernel.
In refined mixed-ladder form, after the pre-half-window residuals and the raw
half-window tail divisor-arrangement ledger are charged, the only remaining
finite frontier charge is the bottom finite parameter set
\(E_{r_0,>b}\cup\Theta_{r_0,>b}\), of size at most \(2h\) on the remaining
ledger.  Equivalently, apart from the pre-half-window longer-Pade residual
families, the current direct raw-denominator upper ledger is
\[
  \mathrm{ArrBudget}_{hw}+2h.
\]
The tail term is the divisor arrangement of the active cutoff primitive
denominators:
\[
  \mathrm{ArrBudget}_{hw}
  =\sum_{\emptyset\ne I\subseteq A}(-1)^{|I|+1}
     \Phi_+\!\left(h-\deg \operatorname{lcm}_{F\in I}D_F\right),
  \qquad
  \Phi_+(m)=\Phi(\max\{m,0\}),
\]
and
\[
  |\mathrm{TailUnion}_{hw}|
  \le \mathrm{ArrBudget}_{hw}
  \le \mathrm{FamilyBudget}_{hw}.
\]
The same arrangement may be computed after deleting every active family whose
cutoff primitive denominator is a multiple of another active family's
primitive denominator and after identifying duplicate primitive denominators.
Thus only the distinct divisibility-minimal antichain of active primitive
denominators contributes to the raw tail union; if the active denominators
form a chain, then
\[
  \mathrm{ArrBudget}_{hw}=\Phi(h-\delta_{\min})
\]
for the smallest active primitive denominator \(D_{\min}\), with the convention
that the right-hand side is zero when \(\delta_{\min}\ge h\).
More generally, if the surviving antichain has common core
\[
  C=\gcd(D:D\in M_{\min}), \qquad \gamma=\deg C,
\]
then multiplication by \(C\) identifies the raw tail arrangement with the
quotient arrangement for the denominators \(D/C\) in the shorter window
\(h-\gamma\).  In particular,
\[
  \mathrm{ArrBudget}_{hw}\le \Phi_+(h-\gamma),
  \qquad
  \mathrm{ArrBudget}_{hw}+2h\le \Phi_+(h-\gamma)+2h,
\]
so the half-window tail vanishes from the raw denominator ledger when
\(\gamma\ge h\).  If those quotient denominators are pairwise coprime, the exact
tail term is
\[
  \mathrm{ArrBudget}_{hw}
  =\sum_{\emptyset\ne I\subseteq M_{\min}}(-1)^{|I|+1}
     \Phi_+\!\left(h-\gamma-\sum_{D\in I}\deg(D/C)\right).
\]
Without coprimality, if \(s=|M_{\min}|\) and
\(e_{\min}=\min_{D\in M_{\min}}\deg(D/C)\), then
\[
  \mathrm{ArrBudget}_{hw}\le s\,\Phi_+(h-\gamma-e_{\min}).
\]
When \(s\ge2\), one has \(e_{\min}\ge1\), so every genuinely multi-family
antichain gains at least one quotient degree beyond the common-core ambient
bound.  Hence if \(\gamma+e_{\min}\ge h-L\), then
\[
  \mathrm{ArrBudget}_{hw}+2h \le 4\Phi_+(L)+2h.
\]
For \(q\le n^a\), \(h\le n\), and fixed \(L\ge1\), this is at most
\[
  4L n^{a(L-1)}+2n.
\]
There is also a bottom residual route: charging the four root-free residual
families at \(r_0=\min R\) removes every residual at every depth in the ladder,
so the pre-half part has no \(|R_{\mathrm{pre}}|\) factor.  This route closes
with only the same bottom finite frontier charge \(2h\), while the half-window
arrangement route is useful when the bottom longer-Pade residuals are left as
the named obstruction.
Equivalently, the residual bottleneck can be recorded as a two-route minimum.
If \(B_0\) is the union of the four bottom root-free residual families and
\(P_{\mathrm{pre}}\) is the union of the four pre-half residual families over
all \(r\in R_{\mathrm{pre}}\), then any admissible charges for these sets give
the consumable ledger
\[
  \min\{\mathrm{Charge}(B_0),
          \mathrm{Charge}(P_{\mathrm{pre}})
          +\mathrm{ArrBudget}_{hw}\}+2h.
\]
This does not bound \(B_0\) or \(P_{\mathrm{pre}}\) by itself; it makes the
current M1 bottleneck explicit: either prove the bottom longer-Pade family is
small enough, or leave the pre-half family as the named obstruction and prove
the half-window arrangement term is small.
In the multiplicative-domain case \(0\notin D\), the raw arrangement can also
use the exact root-free multiplier count.  If \(n=|D|\), define
\[
  \mathrm{RFPhi}_D(m)
  ={1\over q-1}\sum_{j=0}^{\min(n,m-1)}
      (-1)^j\binom{n}{j}(q^{m-j}-1)
\]
for \(m\ge1\), and set it to zero for \(m\le0\).  Replacing each
\(\Phi_+(h-\ell_I)\) in \(\mathrm{ArrBudget}_{hw}\) by
\(\mathrm{RFPhi}_D(h-\ell_I)\) gives a sharper arrangement
\(\mathrm{RFArrBudget}_{hw}\le \mathrm{ArrBudget}_{hw}\), because any allowed
multiplier must avoid the reciprocal evaluation domain.  Thus the direct
raw-tail ledger may be sharpened to
\[
  \mathrm{RFArrBudget}_{hw}+2h
\]
whenever this root-free multiplier count is being used.
Accordingly, the residual-tail criterion also improves: if
\(r_{\mathrm{tail}}=\max(0,h-\gamma-e_{\min})\le L\), then
\[
  \mathrm{RFArrBudget}_{hw}\le 4\,\mathrm{RFPhi}_D(L),
  \qquad
  \mathrm{RFArrBudget}_{hw}+2h\le 4\,\mathrm{RFPhi}_D(L)+2h.
\]
For instance \(\mathrm{RFPhi}_D(1)=1\) and
\(\mathrm{RFPhi}_D(2)=q+1-n\).  Thus residual dimension one gives
\(4+2h\), while residual dimension two gives \(4(q+1-n)+2h\), and only
\(8+2h\) on the full multiplicative domain \(n=q-1\).
The denominator threshold has an exact cutoff Hankel test.  At the first
half-window depth \(r_{hw}\), define the four truncated maps on degree-\(<m\)
directions
\[
\begin{aligned}
A_u(m)&=H_{t+r_{hw},m-1}(u),&
A_v(m)&=H_{t+r_{hw},m-1}(v),\\
A_{uv}(m)&=(H_{t+r_{hw}-1,m-1}(u),
            H_{t+r_{hw}-1,m-1}(v)),&
A_{Suv}(m)&=(H_{t+r_{hw}-1,m-1}(Su),
              H_{t+r_{hw}-1,m-1}(Sv)).
\end{aligned}
\]
If \(D_F\) is the cutoff primitive denominator for family \(F\), with
\(\delta_F=\deg D_F\), then \(\delta_F<m\) if and only if
\(\ker A_F(m)\) contains a nonzero root-free polynomial.  Indeed the
primitive denominator itself is a root-free cutoff certificate, and conversely
any root-free degree-\(<m\) certificate has the same reduced Pade denominator,
which divides its reversed denominator.  Thus, for \(0\le L<h\),
\(\delta_F\ge h-L\) for all active families is exactly the absence of
nonzero root-free elements in the kernels \(A_F(h-L)\).  The stronger linear
condition \(\ker A_F(h-L)=0\) for all four families is therefore a concrete
sufficient Hankel-rank target implying
\[
  \mathrm{RFArrBudget}_{hw}\le 4\,\mathrm{RFPhi}_D(L),
\]
and hence the residual ledger
\[
  \min\{BCF_0,\mathrm{Charge}(P_{\rm pre})+
  4\,\mathrm{RFPhi}_D(L)\}+2h.
\]
The exact low-degree root-free kernel obstruction also has a root-slice
formula.  If \(K_F(m)=\ker A_F(m)\), then
\[
  |K_F(m)^{\rm rf}|
  =\sum_{J\subseteq D}(-1)^{|J|}
      \Phi(\dim K_F(m)(-J)),
\]
where \(K_F(m)(-J)\) denotes the subspace vanishing on \(J\).  Multiplication
by \(L_J=\prod_{\alpha\in J}(X-\alpha)\) identifies this slice with the
stripped cutoff kernel obtained by replacing \(u,v\) by
\(\Delta_Ju,\Delta_Jv\) and reducing the denominator degree by \(|J|\);
for the shifted pair one uses \(S\Delta_Ju,S\Delta_Jv\).  Thus
\(\delta_F\ge h-L\) is equivalent to the vanishing of this finite
inclusion-exclusion count at \(m=h-L\).  The half-window denominator target is
therefore an exact root-slice rank identity, with plain injectivity as a
stronger sufficient shortcut.
Over the multiplicative-domain range this shortcut is exact after fixed-root
pieces are separated.  Let \(K\subset F_q[X]_{<m}\) and assume \(|D|<q\).  If
\(K\ne0\) and no point of \(D\) is a common root of all elements of \(K\), then
the hyperplanes \(\ker(\operatorname{ev}_\alpha|_K)\), \(\alpha\in D\), cover
at most \(|D|q^{\dim K-1}<q^{\dim K}\) vectors.  Hence some nonzero
\(Q\in K\) avoids all domain roots.  Therefore \(K^{\rm rf}\) is empty iff
\(K=0\) or \(K\) has a common domain root.  Applying this to
\(K_F(h-L)\), after the fixed-root/root-slice charges from the recurrence
stripping have been removed, the condition \(\delta_F\ge h-L\) is equivalent
to the ordinary injectivity \(\ker A_F(h-L)=0\) on the remaining ledger.
Equivalently, the half-window route is reduced to four explicit full-column
minor tests at the single cutoff depth.  With \(m=h-L\), the half-window
inequality \(h\le t+r_{hw}\) and \(t\ge2\) ensure that the scalar matrices have
at least \(m\) rows and the paired matrices have \(2(t+r_{hw}-1)\ge m\) rows.
If one \(m\times m\) minor of each of
\[
  A_u(m),\quad A_v(m),\quad A_{uv}(m),\quad A_{Suv}(m)
\]
is nonzero, then all active primitive denominators have degree at least
\(h-L\), so
\[
  \mathrm{RFArrBudget}_{hw}\le4\,\mathrm{RFPhi}_D(L)
\]
and the residual ledger is
\[
  \min\{BCF_0,\mathrm{Charge}(P_{\rm pre})+
  4\,\mathrm{RFPhi}_D(L)\}+2h.
\]
Conversely, after common domain-root slices are charged, vanishing of all
full-column minors for one of these four maps is exactly a remaining
low-degree root-free half-window recurrence for that family.
These cutoff minors are genuine M1 structure, not a formal consequence of
having local syndrome moments.  If \(|D|=n\ge s+1\), there is a nonzero
weight vector \((\mu_x)\) whose first \(s\) moments vanish.  For
\(w_a=\sum_{x\in D}\mu_xx^a\), the constant polynomial \(Q=1\) is root-free
and lies in \(\ker H_{s,m-1}(w)\) for every \(m\ge1\).  Taking \(u=v=w\) and
\(s=s_c=t+r_{hw}\) makes \(Q=1\) lie in all four cutoff kernels
\[
  A_u(m),\quad A_v(m),\quad A_{uv}(m),\quad A_{Suv}(m).
\]
By absorbing the nonzero parity-check scalars, these moment data are local
Reed-Solomon syndrome data.  Hence a proof of the four cutoff minors must use
active M1 geometry or quotient/aperiodic structure; row counts and local
syndrome normalization alone cannot supply it.
The positive structural form of the same kernel condition is a
rational-supercode stratum.  Let \(C^{(s)}=\RS[F,D,n-s]\), the supercode cut
out by the first \(s\) syndrome checks.  For \(w=\operatorname{Syn}(y)\) and
\(Q\in F[X]_{<m}\),
\[
  H_{s,m-1}(w)Q=0
  \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
  Qy\in C^{(s)}.
\]
Indeed the \(a\)-th entry is
\(\operatorname{Syn}(Qy)_a=\sum_iq_iw_{a+i}\).  If \(Q\) is root-free on \(D\),
this says \(y=P/Q\) on \(D\) with \(\deg P<n-s\).  Therefore, at the cutoff,
the scalar kernels put \(Qf\) or \(Qg\) in \(C^{(t+r_{hw})}\), the ordinary
paired kernel puts both \(Qf,Qg\) in \(C^{(t+r_{hw}-1)}\), and the shifted
paired kernel puts \(XQf,XQg\) in the same supercode.  Thus minor failure
after fixed-root stripping is exactly a low-degree root-free rational
representation of one or both endpoints into a larger RS supercode.
The paired rational-supercode strata have a direct interpolation charge.  If
\(Qf,Qg\in\RS[F,D,n-s]\), \(Q\) is root-free of degree \(e\), and an explained
slope \(f+zg\) agrees with a codeword on \(a\) points with
\[
  a\ge\max(n-s,k+e),
\]
then \(P_f+zP_g\) and \(QC_z\) are two polynomials of degrees \(<n-s\) and
\(<k+e\) agreeing on \(a\) points, hence are identical.  Thus \(f+zg\) is a
global codeword.  Two distinct global-codeword slopes force both \(f\) and
\(g\) to be codewords, so a noncontained line has at most one slope in such a
paired stratum.  For the shifted paired cutoff kernel, replace \(Q\) by
\(XQ\), so the degree term is \(k+\deg Q+1\) when \(0\notin D\).
There is also a one-sided scalar charge in the higher-agreement range.  Suppose
\(Qf\in\RS[F,D,n-s]\) or \(Qg\in\RS[F,D,n-s]\), \(Q\) is root-free of degree
e, and put \(d=\max(n-s,k+e)\).  If
\[
  2a-n\ge d,
\]
then two explained slopes have supports intersecting in at least \(d\) points.
For the \(Qg\)-stratum, subtracting the two identities
\[
  QC_i=Qf+z_iP_g
\]
on the intersection forces \(Q(C_2-C_1)=(z_2-z_1)P_g\) as a polynomial
identity, hence \(g\) is a global codeword and all explained slopes are
contained.  The \(Qf\)-stratum is the same after comparing
\((QC_i-P_f)/z_i\) for two nonzero slopes, while the zero slope alone forces
\(f\) global once \(a\ge d\).  Therefore each scalar rational-supercode
stratum contributes at most one noncontained finite slope under this
pairwise-intersection threshold.
Below this threshold the same proof still gives a support-packing ledger.  If
\(1\le d\le a\), then two noncontained slopes in the same scalar stratum cannot
have explaining supports intersecting in \(d\) points: otherwise the preceding
pairwise interpolation forces the relevant endpoint to be a global codeword.
Choosing one \(a\)-subset of each support therefore gives a family with no
shared \(d\)-subset, and hence
\[
  |\operatorname{Bad}_Q|
  \le \left\lfloor{\binom n d\over \binom a d}\right\rfloor .
\]
Thus scalar cutoff-kernel failures below the one-slope range are at least
converted into an explicit \(q\)-free support-packing charge, rather than an
unstructured field-size slope count.
This packing ledger has a sharp size warning.  Since
\[
  {\binom n d\over\binom a d}
  =\prod_{i=0}^{d-1}{n-i\over a-i},
\]
if \(a\le\alpha n\) with fixed \(\alpha<1\), then the ledger is at least
\(\alpha^{-d}\).  Thus a polynomial packing closure forces
\(d=O(\log n)\), while fixed-rate scalar strata have
\[
  d=\max(n-s,k+\deg Q)\ge k=\rho n.
\]
Consequently pure support packing cannot close the scalar rational-supercode
branch in the corrected-reserve fixed-rate window; the branch still needs
active M1 structure, quotient/aperiodic collapse, paired/global endpoint
collapse, or exclusion of the root-free scalar cutoff kernels.
There is nevertheless a structural list reduction for the scalar branch.  If
\(d=\max(n-s,k+\deg Q)\le n\), define
\[
  \operatorname{List}_d(Y,a)
  =\{R\in F[X]_{<d}: |\{x\in D:R(x)=Y(x)\}|\ge a\}.
\]
When \(Qg\in\RS[F,D,n-s]\), every noncontained scalar slope \(z\) gives
\[
  R_z=QC_z-zP_g\in \operatorname{List}_d(Qf,a),
\]
and two distinct noncontained slopes cannot give the same \(R_z\), since that
would force \(g\) to be a global codeword and make the slopes contained.
Similarly, when \(Qf\in\RS[F,D,n-s]\), the nonzero noncontained slopes inject
into \(\operatorname{List}_d(Qg,a)\) by
\[
  R_z=(QC_z-P_f)/z,
\]
with only the zero slope left as a possible extra element.  Thus scalar
cutoff-kernel failures can be attacked by ordinary RS list bounds for the
multiplied opposite endpoint in the dimension-\(d\) supercode.
The reduction is more constrained than a full supercode list.  In the
\(Qg\)-stratum the injected polynomials all lie in
\[
  V_g(Q)=QF[X]_{<k}+F P_g,
\]
and in the \(Qf\)-stratum the nonzero injected polynomials lie in
\[
  V_f(Q)=QF[X]_{<k}+F P_f.
\]
These are one-generator extensions of the multiplier code \(Q\RS[F,D,k]\),
of dimension at most \(k+1\).  Equivalently, the injected list polynomials
have residues modulo \(Q\) lying on the line spanned by \(P_g\bmod Q\) or
\(P_f\bmod Q\).  The scalar branch is therefore a constrained subcode-list
problem, not an arbitrary dimension-\(d\) RS list problem.
This constrained list parametrization is exact.  If \(P_g\in QF[X]_{<k}\),
then \(g\) is already global and the \(Qg\)-scalar branch has no noncontained
slope; otherwise each constrained list entry \(R=QC-zP_g\) has a unique
coefficient \(z\), and \(R=Qf\) on \(a\) points is equivalent to
\(f+zg=C\) on those points.  Dually, if \(P_f\notin QF[X]_{<k}\), the
nonzero \(Qf\)-scalar slopes are parametrized exactly by entries
\(R=QC+\mu P_f\) with \(\mu\ne0\), via \(z=-1/\mu\); if
\(P_f\in QF[X]_{<k}\), there are no nonzero noncontained slopes, while the
original zero slope remains separate.
In the standard-degree range \(n-s\le k+\deg Q\), this is exactly an ordinary
lower-degree residue-line datum.  Writing \(P_g=QH_g+R_g\) with
\(\deg H_g<k\) and \(\deg R_g<\deg Q\), the \(Qg\)-scalar branch is the
degree-\(\deg Q\) datum
\[
  E=Q,\qquad B=-R_g,\qquad w=Qf
\]
for the line \(f+z(g-H_g)\), unless \(R_g=0\), in which case \(g\) is global.
Dually, writing \(P_f=QH_f+R_f\), the nonzero \(Qf\)-scalar slopes are the
reciprocal parameters \(y=1/z\) of the datum
\[
  E=Q,\qquad B=-R_f,\qquad w=Qg
\]
for the line \(g+y(f-H_f)\); if \(R_f=0\), there are no nonzero
noncontained slopes, with the original zero slope still left separate.
In the complementary non-standard range \(n-s>k+\deg Q\), put
\(K=n-s-\deg Q>k\).  The same division \(P=QH+R\) gives an enlarged-code
residue-line datum over \(\RS[F,D,K]\), but the explaining polynomial must
return to the original code.  In the \(Qg\)-case, \(z\) is explained iff
there is \(C'_z\in F[X]_{<K}\) with
\[
  QC'_z+z(-R_g)=Qf
\]
on the support and \(C'_z+zH_g\in F[X]_{<k}\).  In the \(Qf\)-case, the
reciprocal parameter \(y=1/z\) satisfies the analogous conditions
\[
  QC'_y+y(-R_f)=Qg,\qquad C'_y+yH_f\in F[X]_{<k}.
\]
Thus the non-standard scalar branch is an enlarged residue-line problem cut by
an explicit affine return-to-\(RS_k\) slice.
Equivalently, if \(\tau_K\) denotes projection to coefficients of degrees
\(k,\ldots,K-1\), the return condition is the high-tail line equation
\[
  \tau_K(C'_z)=-z\,\tau_K(H_g)
\]
in the \(Qg\)-case, and
\[
  \tau_K(C'_y)=-y\,\tau_K(H_f)
\]
in the reciprocal \(Qf\)-case.  If the relevant quotient high tail vanishes,
the non-standard case collapses to the base-dimension residue-line datum; if
it is nonzero, the high tail determines the slope parameter.
At the scalar half-window cutoff this split has a simple parameter form.  Since
\(s=t+r_{hw}\), \(t=r-j\), and \(r=n-k\),
\[
  n-s=k+j-r_{hw}.
\]
Therefore the standard scalar range is \(\deg Q\ge j-r_{hw}\).  The only
positive-degree non-standard scalar cutoff witnesses have
\(1\le\deg Q<j-r_{hw}\), and their high-tail window has length
\[
  K-k=j-r_{hw}-\deg Q.
\]
In particular, if \(r_{hw}\ge j\), there is no non-standard scalar cutoff
range at all.
The constant case \(\deg Q=0\), when \(r_{hw}<j\), is separate: it says that
one endpoint itself lies in the enlarged code \(\RS[F,D,k+j-r_{hw}]\).  If
the endpoint has zero high tail it is global; otherwise the branch is a
one-direction supercode list whose high tail is constrained by
\(\tau_K(R)=-z\tau_K(P)\), with the reciprocal \(Qf\)-case again leaving the
original zero slope separate.
Uniformly across the positive short and constant scalar cases, if the
controlling high tail \(h\ne0\), the enlarged witness polynomial lies in
\[
  W_h=\{A\in F[X]_{<K}:\tau_K(A)\in Fh\}.
\]
Since \(\ker\tau_K=F[X]_{<k}\), this subspace has dimension \(k+1\) and
codimension \(L-1\) where \(L=K-k\).  Each fixed slope parameter cuts an
affine \(k\)-dimensional fiber.  Hence only the one-row tail case \(L=1\)
has no high-tail codimension saving.
In that one-row case the slope parameter can be eliminated.  Let
\(\operatorname{top}(A)\) be the coefficient of \(X^k\), and write
\(h=\tau_K(H)\ne0\).  The equations
\[
  QA+\lambda B=w,\qquad \operatorname{top}(A)=-\lambda h
\]
are equivalent to
\[
  T_h(A)=w,\qquad T_h(A)=QA-(\operatorname{top}(A)/h)B,
\]
with \(\lambda=-\operatorname{top}(A)/h\).  The map \(T_h\) is injective:
in the positive-degree case \(\deg B<\deg Q\), while in the constant case
\(B=0\).  Thus the one-row short scalar layer is a \(k+1\)-dimensional
linear-image list, with the reciprocal \(Qf\)-zero-slope exception still
separate.
Equivalently, after dividing by the root-free \(Q\), write
\[
  A=A_0+\mu hX^k,\qquad A_0\in F[X]_{<k},\qquad
  \mu=\operatorname{top}(A)/h .
\]
Then
\[
  T_h(A)/Q=A_0+\mu\bigl(hX^k-B/Q\bigr).
\]
Thus the one-row layer is exactly a list for the opposite endpoint word
\(w/Q\) against the rank-one rational extension
\[
  \RS[F,D,k]+F\bigl(hX^k-B/Q\bigr),
\]
with the original scalar parameter \(\lambda=-\mu\).  The new generator has
residue \(-B\bmod Q\), and in the constant case it reduces to the polynomial
generator \(hX^k\).
This rational generator can be primitive-compressed.  If
\[
  G=\gcd(Q,B),\qquad Q_{\rm prim}=Q/G,\qquad B_{\rm prim}=B/G,
\]
with \(G=Q\) when \(B=0\), then
\[
  hX^k-B/Q=hX^k-B_{\rm prim}/Q_{\rm prim},\qquad
  \gcd(Q_{\rm prim},B_{\rm prim})=1.
\]
Equivalently, the numerator \(hX^kQ-B\) has gcd \(G\) with \(Q\), since it is
congruent to \(-B\) modulo \(Q\).  Thus nonprimitive one-row presentations do
not create new packing objects; \(Q_{\rm prim}=1\) is the polynomial
\(\RS[F,D,k+1]\) endpoint.
This gives a primitive-degree packing bound.  Put
\[
  e_{\rm prim}=\deg Q_{\rm prim},\qquad d_{\rm prim}=k+e_{\rm prim}+1.
\]
For any word \(Y\), two distinct coefficients \(\mu_1\ne\mu_2\) in lists
against
\[
  \RS[F,D,k]+F(hX^k-B_{\rm prim}/Q_{\rm prim})
\]
cannot have agreement supports intersecting in \(d_{\rm prim}\) points:
after subtracting and multiplying by \(Q_{\rm prim}\), one gets a polynomial
of degree \(<d_{\rm prim}\), and a zero identity would force either the
nonzero \(X^k\)-coefficient \((\mu_1-\mu_2)h\) to vanish
(\(Q_{\rm prim}=1\)) or \(Q_{\rm prim}\mid B_{\rm prim}\)
(\(e_{\rm prim}>0\)).  Hence, when \(d_{\rm prim}\le a\), the coefficient set
has size at most
\[
  \left\lfloor{\binom n {d_{\rm prim}}\over\binom a {d_{\rm prim}}}\right\rfloor,
\]
and in the range \(2a-n\ge d_{\rm prim}\) it has size at most one.  Thus the
one-row scalar packing dimension depends on the primitive denominator degree,
not the unreduced presentation degree.
At the actual scalar cutoff, the MCA agreement threshold is
\(a=n-j=k+t\).  Hence \(d_{\rm prim}\le a\) is exactly
\[
  e_{\rm prim}\le t-1,
\]
and the pairwise one-coefficient range \(2a-n\ge d_{\rm prim}\) is exactly
\[
  e_{\rm prim}\le t-j-1.
\]
In the positive one-row branch the unreduced degree is
\(e=j-r_{hw}-1\), so primitive compression gives
\[
  e_{\rm prim}\le j-r_{hw}-1.
\]
Thus \(j-r_{hw}\le t\) is an unreduced sufficient condition for the packing
charge, and \(2j-r_{hw}\le t\) is an unreduced sufficient condition for the
one-coefficient charge.  Common factors between \(Q\) and \(B\) only improve
these criteria.
Equivalently, in a positive one-row branch with
\[
  e=\deg Q=j-r_{hw}-1,\qquad G=\gcd(Q,B),\qquad g=\deg G,
\]
the packing charge applies iff \(g\ge j-r_{hw}-t\), and the one-coefficient
charge applies iff \(g\ge 2j-r_{hw}-t\).  Therefore any positive one-row
branch left after the primitive packing charge has
\[
  t\le e_{\rm prim}\le j-r_{hw}-1,\qquad
  g\le j-r_{hw}-t-1.
\]
The constant branch has \(e_{\rm prim}=0\), so it is always in the packing
range for \(t\ge1\).  Thus the remaining scalar one-row problem is precisely
the primitive high-denominator range \(e_{\rm prim}\ge t\).
The same subtraction also identifies the residual collision object.  If
coefficients \(\mu_1\ne\mu_2\) are witnessed on supports \(S_1,S_2\), put
\(I=S_1\cap S_2\), \(m=|I|\), and \(L_I=\prod_{\alpha\in I}(X-\alpha)\).
Then \(m<d_{\rm prim}\), and there is a nonzero polynomial \(M\) with
\[
  \deg M<d_{\rm prim}-m,\qquad
  L_I M\equiv-(\mu_1-\mu_2)B_{\rm prim}\pmod {Q_{\rm prim}} .
\]
Thus collisions beyond the packing range are short quotient-residue landing
certificates for the line \(F B_{\rm prim}\bmod Q_{\rm prim}\).  At the cutoff,
if \(m=a-u\), then \(\deg M<e_{\rm prim}+1-t+u\); in the first unpacked layer
\(e_{\rm prim}=t\), one-exchange collisions have \(\deg M<2\).  For the
polynomial endpoint \(Q_{\rm prim}=1\), the congruence is vacuous and the
obstruction is the top-coefficient obstruction from the packing proof.
Thus, in the first unpacked layer, a one-exchange collision is either a core
landing \(L_I\in F B_{\rm prim}\bmod Q_{\rm prim}\), or after scaling
\[
  L_I(X-\beta)\in F B_{\rm prim}\pmod {Q_{\rm prim}}
\]
for a unique \(\beta\in F\).  If \(\beta\in D\setminus I\), this is an
all-domain one-exchange locator landing; if \(\beta\notin D\), it is exactly
an external-anchor boundary landing.
More generally, write \(e_{\rm prim}=t+\ell\).  For a fixed one-exchange core
\(I\), let
\[
  V_I^\ell=\{M\in F[X]_{\le \ell+1}:L_IM\in F B_{\rm prim}
      \bmod Q_{\rm prim}\}.
\]
Since \(t\ge2\), \(\ell+1<e_{\rm prim}\), so reduction modulo
\(Q_{\rm prim}\) is injective on this multiplier space.  As
\(\gcd(L_I,Q_{\rm prim})=1\), any two nonzero elements of \(V_I^\ell\) are
proportional.  Thus each fixed one-exchange core has at most one projective
multiplier certificate; if it splits, it is a bounded-anchor landing, and if
not it is an irreducible bounded-degree multiplier landing.
Let \(\operatorname{Exc}_\ell\) be the set of \((a-1)\)-cores for which this
multiplier space is nonzero.  Choosing one \(a\)-subset \(T_\mu\) for each
coefficient \(\mu\), every nonexceptional \((a-1)\)-core lies in at most one
chosen support.  If \(r_I=|\{\mu:I\subset T_\mu\}|\), the correct residual is
the exceptional incidence mass
\[
  R_{\rm exc}(Y,a)=\sum_{I\in\operatorname{Exc}_\ell}r_I .
\]
Counting pairs \((\mu,I)\) with \(I\subset T_\mu\) and \(|I|=a-1\) gives
\[
  |\operatorname{Mu}_h(Y,a)|\le
  { \binom n {a-1}-|\operatorname{Exc}_\ell|+R_{\rm exc}(Y,a) \over a } .
\]
Thus the one-row coefficient set is an \((a-1)\)-packing apart from the
weighted exceptional multiplier-core incidence ledger; the unweighted number
of exceptional cores alone does not control same-support coefficient
multiplicity.
Every nonzero exceptional multiplier is automatically primitive modulo
\(Q_{\rm prim}\).  Indeed, if
\[
  L_I M\equiv cB_{\rm prim}\pmod {Q_{\rm prim}},
\]
then \(c\ne0\), and any common factor of \(M\) and \(Q_{\rm prim}\) would
divide \(B_{\rm prim}\), contradicting
\(\gcd(B_{\rm prim},Q_{\rm prim})=1\).  Hence \(M\) is invertible modulo
\(Q_{\rm prim}\), and exceptional cores satisfy
\[
  L_I\in F B_{\rm prim}M^{-1}\pmod {Q_{\rm prim}}.
\]
There is therefore no additional shared-factor multiplier branch in the
one-exchange residual.
The exceptional core ledger consequently decomposes without multiplier
multiplicity.  For each projective low-degree class \([M]\) with
\(\gcd(M,Q_{\rm prim})=1\), define
\[
  R_{[M]}=F(B_{\rm prim}M^{-1})\subset F[X]/(Q_{\rm prim})
\]
and
\[
  \operatorname{Core}_{[M]}
  =\{I\subset D:|I|=a-1,\ L_I\bmod Q_{\rm prim}\in R_{[M]}\}.
\]
Then
\[
  \operatorname{Exc}_\ell
  =\bigsqcup_{[M]}\operatorname{Core}_{[M]}.
\]
Indeed, if a core lay in two multiplier fibers, then
\(L_I(dM-cN)\equiv0\bmod Q_{\rm prim}\); since \(L_I\) is coprime to
\(Q_{\rm prim}\) and \(\deg(dM-cN)<\deg Q_{\rm prim}\), the multipliers are
projectively equal.  Thus the remaining exceptional ledger is a disjoint sum
of ordinary split-locator residue-line fibers.
These fibers can be written on small complements.  Let
\(L_D=\prod_{\alpha\in D}(X-\alpha)\).  If \(I\) has size \(a-1=n-j-1\) and
\(C=D\setminus I\), then \(|C|=j+1\) and \(L_D=L_IL_C\).  Since
\(Q_{\rm prim}\) is root-free on \(D\), both \(L_D\) and \(L_C\) are
invertible modulo \(Q_{\rm prim}\).  Therefore
\[
  L_I\in F B_{\rm prim}M^{-1}\pmod {Q_{\rm prim}}
\]
is equivalent to
\[
  L_C\in F L_D M B_{\rm prim}^{-1}\pmod {Q_{\rm prim}}.
\]
Thus every exceptional agreement-core fiber is equivalently a
\((j+1)\)-point split-complement residue-line fiber modulo \(Q_{\rm prim}\).
Substituting this into the weighted packing-with-exceptions bound gives the
one-exchange one-row ledger in complement form.  With
\[
  R_{\rm comp}(Y,a)=\sum_{[M]}\sum_{C\in\operatorname{Comp}_{[M]}}r_{D\setminus C},
\]
one has
\[
  |\operatorname{Mu}_h(Y,a)|
  \le { \binom n {j+1}+R_{\rm comp}(Y,a)\over n-j } .
\]
Therefore any bound
\[
  R_{\rm comp}(Y,a)\le B_{\rm comp}
\]
closes this one-exchange scalar one-row contribution at
\[
  |\operatorname{Mu}_h(Y,a)|\le { \binom n {j+1}+B_{\rm comp}\over n-j } .
\]
The missing same-support multiplicity input has a matching complement form.
For a fixed \(a\)-support \(T\), the coefficient set
\(\{\mu:\exists A_0\in F[X]_{<k}, A_0+\mu\phi_h=Y\text{ on }T\}\) has size
at most one unless \(\phi_h|_T\in \RS[F,T,k]\); in the exceptional case it is
either empty or all of \(F\).  This generator-collapse condition is equivalent
to a \(j\)-point complement certificate.  Writing \(C=D\setminus T\), there is
a nonzero \(N\) with \(\deg N<\ell+1\) such that
\[
  L_C\in F L_D N B_{\rm prim}^{-1}\pmod {Q_{\rm prim}}.
\]
Thus same-support multiplicity is itself another small-complement
residue-line fiber, now on \(j\)-point complements and with multiplier degree
at most \(\ell\), together with the normalization that after scaling
\(L_TN\equiv-B_{\rm prim}\bmod Q_{\rm prim}\), the quotient
\((L_TN+B_{\rm prim})/Q_{\rm prim}\) has \(X^k\)-coefficient \(h\).
Moreover, if this generator-collapse support is adjacent to a one-exchange
exceptional complement, then it is root-anchored by the one-exchange
multiplier.  If \(C\in\operatorname{Comp}_{[M]}\), \(\beta\in C\), and
\(C_\beta=C\setminus\{\beta\}\) is a generator-collapse complement with
multiplier \(N\), then
\[
  [M]=[(X-\beta)N].
\]
Indeed the two complement congruences differ by the factor
\(L_C=(X-\beta)L_{C_\beta}\), and the degree bound
\(\ell+1<\deg Q_{\rm prim}\) turns the resulting congruence into a polynomial
identity.  Hence weighted incidence can exceed the trivial \(j+1\) adjacent
supports per exceptional complement only at roots in \(D\) of the low-degree
one-exchange multiplier; root-free multiplier fibers carry no such excess.
After a root is fixed, the corresponding fiber descends exactly.  If
\([M]=[(X-\beta)N]\), then deletion of \(\beta\) is a bijection from
\[
  \{C\in\operatorname{Comp}_{[M]}:\beta\in C\}
\]
to the \(j\)-point complement fiber
\[
  \{C_0\subset D: |C_0|=j,\ \beta\notin C_0,
      L_{C_0}\in F L_DN B_{\rm prim}^{-1}\pmod {Q_{\rm prim}}\}.
\]
This uses only that \(X-\beta\) is invertible modulo \(Q_{\rm prim}\).  Thus
the root-anchor excess is a degree-dropped complement-packing target after
the actual domain root has been charged.
The fixed-support multiplicity itself has no further hidden branch.  For an
\(a\)-support \(T\),
\[
  |\operatorname{Mu}_T(Y)|>1
  \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
  \phi_h|_T\in \RS[F,T,k]\ \text{and}\ Y|_T\in\RS[F,T,k],
\]
and then \(\operatorname{Mu}_T(Y)=F\).  Hence, with
\[
  \operatorname{DoubleColl}_h(Y,a)
  =\{T: |T|=a,\ \phi_h|_T\in\RS[F,T,k],\
              Y|_T\in\RS[F,T,k]\},
\]
the root-anchor excess ledger satisfies
\[
  E_{\rm root}(Y,a)=a\sum_{|T|=a}(m_T(Y)-1)_+
  \le a(q-1)|\operatorname{DoubleColl}_h(Y,a)|.
\]
Consequently
\[
  R_{\rm comp}(Y,a)\le (j+1)U_{\rm comp}
    +a(q-1)|\operatorname{DoubleColl}_h(Y,a)|.
\]
The weighted one-exchange ledger is therefore reduced to ordinary
small-complement packing plus a double-collapse support count where both the
one-row generator and the received word are base-code on the same support.
Equivalently, on \(j\)-complements this double-collapse count is an
intersection ledger.  Let
\[
  \operatorname{YLoc}_j(Y)=
  \{C\subset D: |C|=j,\ Y|_{D\setminus C}\in \RS[F,D\setminus C,k]\}.
\]
For projective \([N]\) with \(\deg N\le\ell\), let
\(G\operatorname{Drop}_{h,[N]}\) be the \(j\)-complement residue fiber
\[
  L_C\in F L_DN B_{\rm prim}^{-1}\pmod {Q_{\rm prim}},
\]
together with the same top-coefficient normalization defining the fixed
generator \(hX^k-B_{\rm prim}/Q_{\rm prim}\).  The complement map gives a
disjoint decomposition
\[
  \operatorname{DoubleColl}_h(Y,a)
  =\bigsqcup_{[N]}
    \bigl(\operatorname{YLoc}_j(Y)\cap G\operatorname{Drop}_{h,[N]}\bigr).
\]
Thus the one-exchange one-row residual has the closure criterion
\[
  |\operatorname{Mu}_h(Y,a)|\le
  { \binom n {j+1}+(j+1)U_{\rm comp}
       +a(q-1)D_{\rm int}(Y,a)\over n-j },
\]
where \(D_{\rm int}\) is the displayed double-collapse intersection count.
In particular, after the local-contained double-collapse supports are charged
away, \(D_{\rm int}=0\), and the one-row residual becomes q-free:
\[
  |\operatorname{Mu}_h(Y,a)|\le
  { \binom n {j+1}+(j+1)U_{\rm comp}\over n-j}.
\]
The complement fibers \(\operatorname{Comp}_{[M]}\) are disjoint subsets of
all \((j+1)\)-complements, so \(U_{\rm comp}\le\binom n {j+1}\).  Hence
\[
  |\operatorname{Mu}_h(Y,a)|
  \le {j+2\over j+1}\binom n j
  \le 2\binom n j.
\]
Thus the only field-size multiplicity in this one-exchange one-row residual
is the explicitly named local-contained double-collapse locus.
For the actual support-wise noncontained one-row residual this locus can be
removed at the choice-of-support stage.  Let \(\operatorname{Mu}_h^{\rm nc}(Y,a)\)
be the coefficients admitting a witness support \(T\) for
\(A_0+\mu\phi_h=Y\) on which not both \(\phi_h\) and \(Y\) restrict to
\(\RS_k\).  Choosing such supports, every nonexceptional \((a-1)\)-core is
used at most once, while an exceptional core has only its \(j+1\) adjacent
supports and each noncontained adjacent support carries at most one
coefficient.  Hence
\[
  |\operatorname{Mu}_h^{\rm nc}(Y,a)|
  \le { \binom n {j+1}+jU_{\rm comp}\over n-j }.
\]
Since the exceptional complement fibers are disjoint,
\(U_{\rm comp}\le\binom n {j+1}\), so
\[
  |\operatorname{Mu}_h^{\rm nc}(Y,a)|\le \binom n j.
\]
Thus the support-wise noncontained one-exchange one-row branch is q-free
without assuming the local-contained double-collapse locus is empty.
Equivalently, in a positive primitive one-row residual chart after the
primitive packing charge, the one-exchange support-wise noncontained finite
parameters contribute at most
\[
  {\binom n j\over q}
\]
to the line-MCA density in that chart.  The only field-size family in the
unrestricted coefficient ledger is the local-contained double-collapse locus,
which is not a noncontained witness and must be charged to the
contained/tangent ledger.
The exact-support formulation loses nothing for the threshold predicate.
If a coefficient has a noncontained witness on a support \(S\) with
\(|S|\ge a\), then some \(a\)-subset \(T\subset S\) is still noncontained:
otherwise both \(\phi_h\) and \(Y\) would restrict to \(\RS_k\) on every
\(a\)-subset, hence on \(S\) itself by the \((k+1)\)-subset test for
degree-\(<k\) interpolation.  Thus the same \(\binom n j/q\) density bound
applies to agreement at least \(a\).
Equivalently, every rank-one noncontained branch has a direct support
injection.  For any extension direction \(\phi\), choose one noncontained
\(a\)-support \(T_\mu\) for each coefficient \(\mu\).  Two coefficients
cannot choose the same \(T\), because subtracting the two equations forces
\(\phi|_T\in\RS_k\), and then either equation forces \(Y|_T\in\RS_k\), making
the support contained.  Hence
\[
  |\operatorname{Mu}_{\phi}^{\rm nc,\ge a}(Y)|\le \binom n a=\binom n j,
\]
without using the one-exchange exceptional-core ledger.  The residue-line
weighted ledgers above are therefore bookkeeping for the unrestricted
one-row coefficient/list object; the actual support-wise noncontained
one-row contribution is already q-free.
The same support injection applies to every short scalar high-tail-line
branch from the enlarged-code return slice.  If \(W_h=\{A:\tau_K(A)\in Fh\}\)
with \(h\ne0\), choose \(\Phi\) with \(\tau_K(\Phi)=h\).  Then
\[
  W_h=F[X]_{<k}+F\Phi,
\]
and the common scalar chart equations \(QA+\lambda B=w\) and
\(\tau_K(A)=-\lambda h\) become, after setting \(\mu=-\lambda\),
\[
  A_0+\mu(\Phi-B/Q)=w/Q
\]
on the support.  Thus the noncontained parameters in that branch are a
rank-one coefficient set for the normalized direction \(\Phi-B/Q\).  Hence
every such high-tail-line scalar branch contributes at most \(\binom n j/q\)
to line-MCA density.  The extra high-tail codimension for \(L\ge2\) remains
useful for unrestricted list bookkeeping, but is not needed for the
noncontained finite-parameter count.
Combining this with the scalar cutoff split removes the enlarged-code scalar
residual altogether.  Standard positive denominators, and short positive
denominators whose controlling high tail vanishes, are ordinary
base-dimension residue-line data.  Constant endpoint branches with no
high-tail window, or with zero high tail, are global/contained.  Every nonzero
high-tail-line branch is q-free by the rank-one support injection above.
A fixed base-dimension residue-line datum is also rank-one after division by
its root-free denominator: \(QA+\lambda B=w\) becomes
\[
  A+\lambda(B/Q)=w/Q.
\]
Therefore its support-wise noncontained coefficients also inject into
\(a\)-supports and contribute at most \(\binom n j/q\) to line-MCA density.
Thus no fixed scalar cutoff datum carries a field-size noncontained parameter
family; the remaining M1 packing issue is the active denominator/residue-line
class ledger after tangent, contained, and quotient-periodic branches are
charged.
This active ledger may be primitive-compressed.  If \(G=\gcd(Q,B)\), then
\[
  QA+\lambda B=w
  \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
  (Q/G)A+\lambda(B/G)=w/G
\]
on \(D\), and the divided direction \(B/Q\) is unchanged.  Hence
nonprimitive residue-line presentations add no new noncontained coefficients;
only primitive root-free residue pairs, plus the polynomial endpoint, need be
counted in the class ledger.
For a primitive positive class with \(e_0=\deg Q_0\), the fixed-class packing
dimension is \(d_0=k+e_0\).  Two distinct coefficients cannot have witness
supports meeting in \(d_0\) points, since subtracting the two equations gives
a degree-\(<d_0\) polynomial whose reduction modulo \(Q_0\) would force
\((\lambda_1-\lambda_2)B_0\equiv0\), contradicting
\(\gcd(Q_0,B_0)=1\).  Hence, when \(d_0\le a\),
\[
  |\Lambda_{Q_0,B_0}^{\rm nc,\ge a}(w_0)|
  \le \binom n {d_0}/\binom a {d_0}.
\]
At \(a=k+t\), this applies exactly for \(e_0\le t\), with a one-coefficient
charge for \(e_0\le t-j\).  The genuinely unpacked fixed-class range is
therefore primitive degree \(e_0>t\).
In that high-degree range, coefficient collisions force short residue
landings.  If two exact threshold supports meet in \(a-u\) points, then their
subtraction gives
\[
  L_I M\in F B_0 \pmod {Q_0},\qquad
  \deg M<e_0-t+u.
\]
Thus the first unpacked base layer \(e_0=t+1\) has one-exchange collisions
only through constant or linear multiplier landings, i.e. core or finite
anchor residue certificates.  The hard residual is therefore a bounded-degree
primitive residue-landing ledger, not raw coefficient multiplicity.
Dualizing by \(L_D=L_I L_C\), such a certificate is equivalently a
small-complement residue fiber
\[
  L_C\in F\,L_D M B_0^{-1}\pmod {Q_0},\qquad |C|=j+u.
\]
For \(u\le t\), these fibers are disjoint in the projective multiplier
\([M]\), because \(\deg M<e_0-t+u\le e_0\).  In the first unpacked
one-exchange layer this splits the collision ledger into core landings,
domain-anchor landings, repeated-domain-root landings, and external-anchor
landings.
The in-domain anchor part descends.  For \(M=X-\beta\) with \(\beta\in C\),
deleting \(\beta\) gives a bijection to the \(j\)-complement core fiber
\[
  L_{C_0}\in F\,L_D B_0^{-1}\pmod {Q_0},\qquad |C_0|=j.
\]
Thus domain-anchor incidences form exactly the support star over this
descended core fiber.  The remaining first-unpacked ledgers are the constant
core complement fiber itself, together with repeated-domain-root and
external-anchor linear landings.
For a fixed primitive first-unpacked base class, however, the whole
unweighted one-exchange complement ledger is disjoint across all projective
linear multipliers \(M\).  Hence the union of the core, in-domain-anchor,
repeated-root, and external-anchor complement fibers has size at most
\(\binom n {j+1}\).  In particular the descended domain-anchor core fiber has
size at most \(\binom n j/(j+1)\).
The same disjoint complement ledger controls coefficient-core incidence.
Choose one noncontained \(a\)-support for each coefficient and count its
\((a-1)\)-cores.  A core repeated by two coefficients must have complement in
the first-unpacked ledger above; otherwise its multiplicity is one.  On an
exceptional core, at most \(j+1\) supports can contain it.  Hence
\[
  |\Lambda^{\rm nc,\ge a}_{Q_0,B_0}(w_0)|
  \le { \binom n {j+1} + j |U_1(Q_0,B_0)| \over n-j}
  \le \binom n j.
\]
Thus the first-unpacked fixed base class has no weighted one-exchange
obstruction on the support-wise noncontained side.
The same complement-disjointness works at every exact exchange depth
\(u\le t\).  With \(r_u=e_0-t+u\), if \(r_u>0\), the fibers
\[
  L_C\in F\,L_D M B_0^{-1}\pmod {Q_0},\qquad
  |C|=j+u,\quad \deg M<r_u,
\]
are disjoint as the projective multiplier \([M]\) varies, so their union has
size at most \(\binom n {j+u}\).  If \(M=(X-\beta)N\) and the actual domain
root \(\beta\) lies in \(C\), deleting \(\beta\) bijects that subfiber with
the exchange-\((u-1)\) fiber for \(N\).  Thus in-domain complement roots
descend through the exchange ledger; only root-free-in-the-complement
multiplier pieces remain genuinely new at depth \(u\).
More generally, if \(M=L_RN\), \(|R|\le u\), and all roots in the split
locator \(L_R\) belong to the complement \(C\), deleting the whole root set
\(R\) bijects the fiber at depth \(u\) with the fiber for \(N\) at depth
\(u-|R|\).  Hence split complement-root factors are already lower-depth data
until core depth is reached; after this descent, the new pieces are
core-depth complement roots, roots in \(D\setminus C\), external roots, and
irreducible bounded-degree multiplier factors.
The same \(U_u\) ledger also controls weighted coefficient-core incidence at
exchange depth \(u\).  If one noncontained \(a\)-support is chosen for each
coefficient, any repeated \((a-u)\)-core has complement in \(U_u\).  Counting
these cores gives
\[
  |\Lambda^{\rm nc,\ge a}_{Q_0,B_0}(w_0)|
  \le { \binom n {j+u}
        +(\binom {j+u}u-1)|U_u(Q_0,B_0)| \over \binom a u}
  \le \binom n j.
\]
Thus for a fixed primitive base class, \(U_u\) controls both unweighted
complement landings and weighted repeated-core coefficient mass.
Finally, split in-domain multiplier roots admit an exact drop-or-slice
stratification.  If \(M=L_RN\), \(S=C\cap R\), and \(T=R\setminus S\), then
the roots in \(S\) delete from the complement and lower the exchange depth,
while the roots in \(T\) remain forced support roots:
\[
  C\setminus S\in \mathrm{Comp}_{u-|S|,[L_TN]}(Q_0,B_0),
  \qquad T\subset D\setminus (C\setminus S).
\]
If more complement roots occur than the exchange depth permits, deleting any
\(u\) of them lands at the core-depth ledger.  Thus the split domain-root
part of \(U_u\) is reduced to lower-depth data plus explicit root slices.
The remaining root-free multiplier factors can be moved into the primitive
base class.  If \([HN]\in P_v^x(Q_0)\) and
\[
  B_H\equiv B_0H^{-1}\pmod {Q_0},
\]
then
\[
  \mathrm{Comp}_{v,[HN]}(Q_0,B_0)
  =\mathrm{Comp}_{v,[N]}(Q_0,B_H).
\]
Thus split external roots and irreducible bounded-degree factors are moving
base-class twists, not additional coefficient multiplicity inside one fixed
primitive class.
Moreover these low-degree twists do not projectively collapse.  For
\(1\le h\le e_0\), the map
\[
  [H]\longmapsto [B_H],\qquad B_H\equiv B_0H^{-1}\pmod {Q_0},
  \quad \deg H<h,
\]
is injective on projective classes coprime to \(Q_0\).  Hence root-free
factors of degree \(<h\) produce at most \((q^h-1)/(q-1)\) primitive twists,
and distinct finite linear external factors give distinct twists when
\(e_0\ge2\).
For fixed residual \(N\), the whole root-free twist packet is also disjoint:
if \([HN]\in P_v^x(Q_0)\), then
\[
  \sum_{[H]} |\mathrm{Comp}_{v,[N]}(Q_0,B_H)|
  = \sum_{[H]} |\mathrm{Comp}_{v,[HN]}(Q_0,B_0)|
  \le \binom n {j+v}.
\]
Thus moving root-free twists do not create unweighted complement
multiplicity for a fixed residual multiplier.
The two reductions commute.  If \([L_SHN]\in P_u^x(Q_0)\), \(S\subset D\),
\(|S|=s\le u\), and \(H\) has no roots on \(D\), then with
\(B_H\equiv B_0H^{-1}\pmod {Q_0}\), deletion of \(S\) gives a bijection
\[
 \{C\in\mathrm{Comp}_{u,[L_SHN]}(Q_0,B_0):S\subset C\}
 \leftrightarrow
 \{C_0\in\mathrm{Comp}_{u-s,[N]}(Q_0,B_H):C_0\cap S=\emptyset\}.
\]
Thus complement-side domain roots and root-free multiplier factors can be
removed in one normal-form step.
If \(d=\deg H\), this also spends residual multiplier degree:
\[
  \deg N<r_{u-s}-d.
\]
Hence a split external linear factor lowers the remaining multiplier budget by
one, and an irreducible root-free factor of degree \(d\) lowers it by \(d\);
if \(d\ge r_{u-s}\), no such source fiber exists.
Thus the smallest residual budgets are explicit: at budget \(R\le1\) no
nonconstant root-free factor survives, at \(R=2\) the only nonconstant
root-free factor is one external linear anchor with constant residual, and at
\(R=3\) the only nonconstant root-free pieces are linear or quadratic twists.
At budget \(R=2\), the external-anchor packet is already disjoint.  For fixed
\(S\subset D\), \(v=u-|S|\), and \(r_v=2\),
\[
  \sum_{[H]\ {\rm linear}}
    |\{C\in \mathrm{Comp}_{u,[L_SH]}(Q_0,B_0):S\subset C\}|
  \le \binom n {j+v}.
\]
Thus the root-free part of the budget-two branch is charged by one
small-complement packet.
The reduced budget-two residual is also one packet.  For any fixed twisted
base numerator \(B_\ast\), the core fiber and the support-side domain-root
slices
\[
  \mathrm{Comp}_{v,[1]}(Q_0,B_\ast),\qquad
  \{C\in\mathrm{Comp}_{v,[X-\beta]}(Q_0,B_\ast):\beta\notin C\}
\]
are disjoint as \(\beta\in D\) varies, and their union has size at most
\(\binom n {j+v}\).  Hence after external anchors and complement roots are
charged, the remaining budget-two ledger is core-or-root-slice.
The same product-ledger mechanism controls budget \(R=3\).  After deleting a
fixed complement-root set \(S\), all residual multipliers of degree \(<3\)
with a nonconstant root-free factor form one disjoint packet:
\[
  \sum_{[M]\ {\rm root\mbox{-}free\ factor}}
    |\{C\in\mathrm{Comp}_{u,[L_SM]}(Q_0,B_0):S\subset C\}|
  \le \binom n {j+v}.
\]
This covers both linear root-free factors with residual degree \(<2\) and
quadratic root-free factors with constant residual.  For any fixed twisted
base numerator \(B_\ast\), the complementary reduced budget-three ledger is
also one packet: the slices with multiplier
\(\prod_{\beta\in D}(X-\beta)^{m_\beta}\), \(\sum m_\beta\le2\), and
\(\operatorname{supp}(m)\cap C=\emptyset\), are pairwise disjoint and have
total size at most \(\binom n {j+v}\).  Thus budget three has no extra
linear/quadratic multiplier multiplicity after complement roots are descended.
More generally, fixed root deletion is a lossless packet shift.  For any
residual multiplier subfamily \(T\subset P_v^x(Q_0)\) and fixed
\(S\subset D\), \(|S|=s\), \(v=u-s\), deletion gives a bijection
\[
 \coprod_{[N]\in T}\{C\in\mathrm{Comp}_{u,[L_SN]}(Q_0,B_0):S\subset C\}
 \leftrightarrow
 \{C_0\in\coprod_{[N]\in T}\mathrm{Comp}_{v,[N]}(Q_0,B_0):C_0\cap S=\emptyset\}.
\]
Hence the fixed frontier shift contributes at most \(\binom n {j+v}\); any
remaining cost comes from charging \(S\) or the residual family \(T\), not
from the shift itself.
The pure support-side domain-root residual has the same exact packet form at
every budget.  For fixed \(B_\ast\), \(R=r_v>0\), and multiplicity vectors
\(m:D\to\mathbb Z_{\ge0}\) with \(|m|<R\), the slices
\[
  \{C\in\mathrm{Comp}_{v,[L_m]}(Q_0,B_\ast):
        \operatorname{supp}(m)\cap C=\emptyset\},
  \qquad
  L_m=\prod_{\beta\in D}(X-\beta)^{m_\beta},
\]
are pairwise disjoint and have total size at most \(\binom n {j+v}\).  Thus,
after root-free factors and complement roots are charged, arbitrary
support-side root multiplicities do not create a new multiplier ledger.
The separated root-free/support-root form also remains disjoint when both
pieces vary.  For fixed \(S\subset D\), \(v=u-|S|\), and any set of pairs
\(([H],[N])\) with \(H\) root-free on \(D\), \([HN]\in P_v^x(Q_0)\), and
pairwise distinct products \([HN]\),
\[
  \sum_{([H],[N])}
    |\{C\in\mathrm{Comp}_{u,[L_SHN]}(Q_0,B_0):S\subset C\}|
  \le \binom n {j+v}.
\]
Indeed deleting \(S\) and twisting by \(H\) identifies these fibers with the
fixed-base product fibers \(\mathrm{Comp}_{v,[HN]}(Q_0,B_0)\).  Thus the
post-deletion total multiplier class, not the separate root-free and
support-root coordinates, is the packet index.
Consequently a labeled root-set stratification has only root-choice cost.  If
\(\mathcal S\) is any collection of \(S\subset D\), \(|S|\le u\), and
\(T_S\subset P_{u-|S|}^x(Q_0)\) is any residual product-class family, then
\[
 \left|
 \coprod_{S\in\mathcal S}\coprod_{[P]\in T_S}
   \{C\in\mathrm{Comp}_{u,[L_SP]}(Q_0,B_0):S\subset C\}
 \right|
 \le
 \sum_{S\in\mathcal S}\binom{n-|S|}{j+u-|S|}.
\]
If all root sets of sizes \(s\in I\) are allowed, this is
\[
  \binom n {j+u}\sum_{s\in I}\binom{j+u}{s}.
\]
Thus fixed-class complement-root bookkeeping pays for choosing charged roots
inside the complement, but not for an additional residual-multiplier family.
This yields an exact fixed-class root normal form.  Every exchange-\(u\)
landing whose multiplier has at most \(u\) roots in the current complement is
equivalent to a unique triple
\[
  (S,[P],C_0),\qquad
  S\subset D,\quad |S|\le u,\quad
  C_0\in\mathrm{Comp}_{u-|S|,[P]}(Q_0,B_0),\quad
  C_0\cap S=\emptyset,
\]
with \(P\) nonvanishing on \(C_0\), via
\((S,[P],C_0)\mapsto([L_SP],C_0\sqcup S)\).  Hence the non-core-descending
fixed-class root residual is bounded by
\[
  \binom n {j+u}\sum_{s=0}^u\binom{j+u}{s},
\]
and the omitted case is exactly the core-depth descent when more than \(u\)
multiplier roots lie in the complement.
The root-heavy case also has only root-choice cost.  If more than \(u\)
multiplier roots lie in \(C\), choose any \(u\) of them.  Deleting those roots
injects the landing into the labeled depth-zero core ledger, hence
\[
  |\mathrm{HC}_u(Q_0,B_0)|
  \le \binom n u\binom{n-u}j
  = \binom n {j+u}\binom{j+u}u.
\]
So both sides of the complement-root dichotomy are fixed-class packet charges:
the non-core branch is the reduced normal form, and the root-heavy branch is a
depth-zero core packet.
Putting the two branches together, every fixed primitive exchange-\(u\)
landing is accounted for by the complement-root dichotomy
\[
  \binom n {j+u}
  \left(\sum_{s=0}^u\binom{j+u}{s}+\binom{j+u}{u}\right).
\]
This is not meant to improve the direct fixed-class disjointness bound; it is
the explicit decomposition showing that complement roots contribute only
root-choice charges before the residual is reduced to a product packet or a
core-depth packet.
The residual product packet itself has a canonical \(D\)-root split.  For
\(0\le v\le t\), \(r_v>0\), the map
\[
  ([H],m)\longmapsto[H L_m],\qquad
  H(\alpha)\ne0\ (\alpha\in D),\quad
  L_m=\prod_{\beta\in D}(X-\beta)^{m_\beta},\quad
  \deg H+|m|<r_v,
\]
is a bijection onto \(P_v^x(Q_0)\).  Hence root-free factors and
support-side domain-root multiplicities are not independent coordinates:
their total product class uniquely determines both pieces.
Over \(\mathbb F_q\) this gives an exact product-count ledger.  Let
\(\mathrm{RFPhi}_{D,Q_0}(h)\) count projective degree-\(<h\) polynomials that
avoid all roots in \(D\) and are coprime to \(Q_0\).  Then for \(R=r_v>0\),
\[
  |P_v^x(Q_0)|
  =\sum_{s=0}^{R-1}\binom{n+s-1}{s}\,
       \mathrm{RFPhi}_{D,Q_0}(R-s).
\]
Equivalently, \(\mathrm{RFPhi}_{D,Q_0}\) is obtained by inclusion-exclusion
over the linear factors \(X-\alpha\), \(\alpha\in D\), together with the
distinct irreducible factors of \(Q_0\).  Thus fixed-denominator residual
counting is the usual root-free multiplier count with the denominator factors
also excluded.
The same count also has the direct denominator-only form
\[
  |P_v^x(Q_0)|
  ={1\over q-1}\sum_{J\subseteq\operatorname{Irr}(Q_0)}
      (-1)^{|J|}(q^{\max(r_v-d_J,0)}-1),
\]
where \(d_J=\sum_{f\in J}\deg f\).  Hence the \(D\)-root split is an exact
accounting decomposition of this denominator-coprime set: domain-root
multiplicities do not change the raw fixed-denominator multiplier count.
Therefore the remaining all-line residue-line ledger is an active primitive
class count.  For any finite family \(\mathcal A_{\rm prim}\) of primitive
base-dimension data \((Q_0,B_0,w_0)\),
\[
  |\Lambda(\mathcal A_{\rm prim})|
  \le \sum_{(Q_0,B_0,w_0)\in\mathcal A_{\rm prim}} W(\deg Q_0),
\]
where \(W(0)=0\),
\[
  W(e)=\min\left\{\binom n j,
       \left\lfloor{\binom n {k+e}\over \binom a {k+e}}\right\rfloor\right\}
       \quad(1\le e\le t),
  \qquad
  W(e)=\binom n j\quad(e>t).
\]
If \(e\le t-j\), the weight sharpens to \(W(e)\le1\).  Thus, after primitive
compression and the fixed-class packet normal forms, no coefficient,
exchange-multiplier, complement-root, or domain-root/root-free multiplicity is
left inside a single primitive class; the remaining M1 task is to bound the
active primitive classes themselves.
There is also a low-total-degree injectivity check for these active classes.
If two primitive data \((Q_i,B_i,w_i)\) have denominator degrees \(e_i\) with
\(e_1+e_2\le n\), and if both divided functions \(B_i/Q_i\) and \(w_i/Q_i\)
agree on \(D\), then the triples are projectively equal.  Indeed
\((B_1Q_2-B_2Q_1)|_D=0\) has degree \(<e_1+e_2\le n\), so it vanishes
identically and coprimality forces \(Q_2=cQ_1\), \(B_2=cB_1\), and
\(w_2=cw_1\) on \(D\).
Thus any nontrivial primitive evaluation alias has denominator degrees
satisfying \(e+e'>n\).  In particular, a support-packing-range class
\(e\le t\) is isolated from every class of degree at most \(n-t\), and if
\(2t\le n\) the whole support-packing subfamily injects into divided
evaluation data.
The remaining high-degree direction aliases have an explicit domain-defect
form.  With \(L_D=\prod_{\alpha\in D}(X-\alpha)\), equality
\((B_1/Q_1)|_D=(B_2/Q_2)|_D\) is equivalent to
\[
  B_1Q_2-B_2Q_1=L_D R
\]
for a unique defect \(R\), either \(R=0\) and the primitive directions are
projectively equal, or \(\deg R<e_1+e_2-n\).  For fixed
\((Q_1,B_1)\) with \(e_1>0\), aliases of denominator degree \(e_2\) are
therefore controlled by
\[
  Q_2\equiv L_D R B_1^{-1}\pmod {Q_1},
  \qquad \deg R<e_1+e_2-n,
\]
plus reduced-numerator, root-freeness, and coprimality admissibility.  In the
first alias layer \(e_1+e_2=n+1\), \(R\) is constant, so the denominators lie
in one projective residue class modulo \(Q_1\).
This first layer is one-sided rigid: if \(e_2=n+1-e_1<e_1\), then for fixed
\((Q_1,B_1)\) there is at most one projective primitive direction of degree
\(e_2\) with the same divided evaluation on \(D\), and a matching divided
target then determines the lower-degree target values as well.
More explicitly, for \(1\le h=n+1-e_1<e_1\), let
\(S_Q\equiv L_D B_1^{-1}\pmod {Q_1}\) with \(\deg S_Q<e_1\).  A lower
degree-\(h\) alias exists exactly when
\[
  \deg S_Q=h,\qquad \gcd(S_Q,L_D)=1,\qquad
  B_Q=(B_1S_Q-L_D)/Q_1,\qquad \gcd(S_Q,B_Q)=1.
\]
Then the unique lower alias is \((S_Q,B_Q)\).
More generally, fixed-direction aliases of degree \(e_2\) have a
defect-quotient chart.  Put \(d=e_1+e_2-n\); if \(d\le0\) there is no
nontrivial alias.  For \(d>0\), let
\[
  A_R=\operatorname{rem}_{Q_1}(L_D R B_1^{-1}),\qquad \deg R<d.
\]
If \(e_2<e_1\), aliases are retained candidates
\[
  Q_2=A_R,\qquad B_2=(B_1A_R-L_D R)/Q_1.
\]
If \(e_2\ge e_1\), they are retained candidates
\[
  Q_2=A_R+Q_1H,\qquad B_2=(B_1Q_2-L_D R)/Q_1,\qquad
  \deg H\le e_2-e_1.
\]
Over \(\F_q\), this gives at most \((q^d-1)/(q-1)\) projective alias
directions when \(e_2<e_1\), and at most
\((q^{2e_2-n+1}-1)/(q-1)\) when \(e_2\ge e_1\).
Summing the lower side, a fixed degree-\(e_1\) direction has no lower-degree
alias unless \(2e_1>n+1\).  When \(2e_1>n+1\), all lower aliases are
controlled by retained projective defects
\[
  [R]\in {\bf P}(\F[X]_{<2e_1-n-1}),
\]
with the same defect-degree, root-free, and primitive admissibility conditions
as in the fixed-degree chart.  Thus over \(\F_q\) their total number is at most
\((q^{2e_1-n-1}-1)/(q-1)\).
These direction-alias bounds transfer directly to full primitive
residue-line data: once a primitive direction alias \((Q_2,B_2)\) of
\((Q_1,B_1)\) is fixed, equality \(w_2/Q_2=w_1/Q_1\) on \(D\) uniquely
forces \(w_2(\alpha)=Q_2(\alpha)w_1(\alpha)/Q_1(\alpha)\).  Hence target
vectors add no extra multiplicity inside a primitive evaluation fiber.
Consequently cutoff fibers have two explicit defect budgets.  For a fixed
positive-degree primitive datum of degree \(e\), let
\(\operatorname{Alias}_{\le E}^*\) be the nontrivial full primitive aliases of
degree at most \(E\), and put
\(\Pi_q(m)=0\) for \(m\le0\), \(\Pi_q(m)=(q^m-1)/(q-1)\) for \(m>0\).  With
\[
  D_-(e,E)=e+\min(E,e-1)-n,
\]
one has
\[
  |\operatorname{Alias}_{\le E}^*|
  \le \Pi_q(D_-(e,E)) + U_q(e,E),
\]
where \(U_q(e,E)=0\) if \(E<e\) or \(e+E\le n\), and otherwise
\[
  U_q(e,E)=\Pi_q(2E-n+1).
\]
Uniformly over all positive-degree primitive classes with \(\deg Q\le E\),
this gives a divided-evaluation fiber multiplier
\[
  M_q(E)=
  \begin{cases}
    1, & 2E\le n,\\
    1+\Pi_q(2E-n+1), & 2E>n.
  \end{cases}
\]
Thus for any finite cutoff family \(\mathcal A_{\le E}\),
\[
  |\mathcal A_{\le E}|
  \le M_q(E)\,|\operatorname{Ev}(\mathcal A_{\le E})|.
\]
Combining this with the active ledger weights gives
\[
  |\Lambda(\mathcal A_{\le E})|
  \le W_E M_q(E)\,|\operatorname{Ev}(\mathcal A_{\le E})|,
  \qquad W_E=\max_{1\le e\le E} W(e).
\]
The lower-defect budget drops out here because each divided evaluation fiber
is counted from a minimal-degree representative.
In the low one-coefficient range \(1\le E\le t-j\) with \(2E\le n\), both
multipliers disappear:
\[
  |\Lambda(\mathcal A_{\le E})|
  \le |\operatorname{Ev}(\mathcal A_{\le E})|.
\]
Thus every divided evaluation datum accounts for at most one support-wise
noncontained coefficient contribution in this low primitive-denominator
range.
More generally, degree slabs pay only local multipliers.  If
\(\mathcal A_r\) is the subfamily with \(E_{r-1}<\deg Q\le E_r\), then
\[
  |\Lambda(\mathcal A_{\rm prim})|
  \le \sum_r W_r M_q(E_r)\,|\operatorname{Ev}(\mathcal A_r)|,
  \qquad
  W_r=\max_{E_{r-1}<e\le E_r} W(e).
\]
Thus low alias-free slabs and high alias-prone slabs can be charged
separately instead of using a single worst cutoff.
There is also a degree-sensitive skeleton form.  Choose one minimal-degree
representative \(s\) in each divided-evaluation fiber of
\(\mathcal A_{\le E}\), with \(e_s=\deg Q_s\).  Then
\[
  |\mathcal A_{\le E}|
  \le \sum_s (1+U_q(e_s,E)),
\]
and
\[
  |\Lambda(\mathcal A_{\le E})|
  \le \sum_s \bigl(W(e_s)+W_{s,E} U_q(e_s,E)\bigr),
  \qquad W_{s,E}=\max_{e_s\le e\le E}W(e).
\]
Thus fibers whose minimal degree satisfies \(e_s+E\le n\) are singleton inside
the cutoff family and pay only \(W(e_s)\).
Equivalently, all nontrivial cutoff fibers live on the high-minimal-degree
tail \(e_s>n-E\).  Splitting the minimal representatives into
\(S_{\rm low}\) and \(S_{\rm tail}\) according as \(e_s+E\le n\) or \(>n\),
\[
  |\Lambda(\mathcal A_{\le E})|
  \le \sum_{s\in S_{\rm low}} W(e_s)
     +\sum_{s\in S_{\rm tail}}
        \bigl(W(e_s)+W_{s,E}\Pi_q(2E-n+1)\bigr).
\]
In the whole one-coefficient cutoff range \(1\le E\le t-j\), this becomes
\[
  |\Lambda(\mathcal A_{\le E})|
  \le |\operatorname{Ev}(\mathcal A_{\le E})|
     +\Pi_q(2E-n+1)|S_{\rm tail}|.
\]
Thus above the half-domain threshold the only remaining loss in this range is
finite-domain aliasing over high-minimal-degree representatives.
For a fixed minimal representative \(s=(Q_s,B_s,w_s)\), that tail is exactly
an upper defect-quotient chart.  Every nontrivial cutoff alias of degree
\(e'\) has \(e_s\le e'\le E\), \(e_s+e'>n\), and is obtained from
\[
  R\in\F[X]_{<e_s+e'-n},\qquad H\in\F[X]_{\le e'-e_s},
\]
by
\[
  Q'=\operatorname{rem}_{Q_s}(L_D R B_s^{-1})+Q_sH,\qquad
  B'=(B_sQ'-L_D R)/Q_s,
\]
with the target then forced on \(D\).  Hence all aliases over \(s\) lie in a
projective parameter space of dimension \(2E-n+1\).
For the actual noncontained coefficient ledger, however, divided-evaluation
aliasing is lossless.  If \(\eta=(\phi,Y)\) is a divided evaluation datum and
\(\Lambda_\eta^{\rm nc,\ge a}\) is the rank-one coefficient set
\[
  A+\lambda\phi=Y\quad\hbox{on }S,\qquad |S|\ge a,
\]
with support-wise noncontainment, then every primitive presentation
\((Q,B,w)\) mapping to \(\eta=((B/Q)|_D,(w/Q)|_D)\) has
\[
  \Lambda_{Q,B}^{\rm nc,\ge a}(w)=\Lambda_\eta^{\rm nc,\ge a}.
\]
Thus, after choosing one representative \(s\) in each divided-evaluation
fiber,
\[
  |\Lambda(\mathcal A_{\le E})|\le \sum_s W(e_s).
\]
In particular, throughout the one-coefficient range \(E\le t-j\),
\[
  |\Lambda(\mathcal A_{\le E})|\le |\operatorname{Ev}(\mathcal A_{\le E})|,
\]
with no \(\Pi_q(2E-n+1)\) tail multiplier for coefficient counting.  The tail
chart remains the active denominator/incidence geometry, not a multiplicative
loss in the slope ledger.
The same coefficient set is also invariant under global-codeword gauge
changes.  If \(C=\operatorname{RS}[F,D,k]\), \(c,d\in C\), and
\(\mu_\phi^{\rm nc,\ge a}(Y)\) denotes the rank-one noncontained coefficient
set, then
\[
  \mu_{\phi+c}^{\rm nc,\ge a}(Y+d)=\mu_\phi^{\rm nc,\ge a}(Y).
\]
Thus it depends only on the quotient classes of \(\phi\) and \(Y\) in
\(F^D/C\).  In the standard scalar branch, \(P_g=QH_g+R_g\) gives
\((-R_g)/Q=H_g-g\), so all \(Qg\)-presentations have the same coefficient set
as \(\mu_{-g}^{\rm nc,\ge a}(f)\).  Similarly all nonzero-slope
\(Qf\)-presentations have the reciprocal-parameter set
\(\mu_{-f}^{\rm nc,\ge a}(g)\), with the original zero slope still separate.
So standard base-dimension residue-line presentations are quotient gauges of
the two endpoint rank-one problems; their denominators remain active-incidence
data, but not separate coefficient ledgers.
The two endpoint charts also glue by reciprocal inversion.  For any
rank-one pair \((\phi,Y)\),
\[
  \lambda\in\mu_\phi^{\rm nc,\ge a}(Y),\quad \lambda\ne0
  \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
  \lambda^{-1}\in \mu_{-Y}^{\rm nc,\ge a}(-\phi).
\]
Thus the nonzero parts of \(\mu_{-g}^{\rm nc,\ge a}(f)\) and
\(\mu_{-f}^{\rm nc,\ge a}(g)\) are inverse coordinate charts for the same
projective line.  The reciprocal \(Qf\) standard branch does not create an
independent nonzero-slope ledger; only the original zero slope is absent from
the reciprocal chart.
What remains of a standard endpoint denominator chart is therefore exactly a
root-free rational-supercode multiplier.  For an endpoint word \(y\) and a
root-free \(Q\) of degree \(e\le n-k\), the quotient class \([-y]\) has a
degree-\(e\) presentation with denominator \(Q\) precisely when
\[
  Qy\in \RS[F,D,k+e].
\]
If \(P_y\) is the degree-\(<k+e\) representative of \(Qy\) and
\(P_y=QH_y+R_y\), then the presentation is forced by
\[
  B=-R_y,\qquad B/Q=H_y-y.
\]
Equivalently, with \(w=\operatorname{Syn}(y)\),
\[
  H_{n-k-e,e}(w)Q=0.
\]
So after quotient-gauge normalization, denominator counting is a concrete
endpoint Hankel-kernel problem for \(f\) and \(g\), not a separate slope
ledger.
This suggests the following exact M1 target.  For a cyclic domain \(H\), let
\(\operatorname{Bad}_{\rm nc}(f,g,a)\) be the finite slopes \(\lambda\) for
which some \(a\)-support \(S\) has
\[
  A+\lambda(-g)=f\quad\hbox{on }S,\qquad A\in\RS[F,H,k],
\]
and the restricted rank-one line is support-wise noncontained on \(S\).  Put
\[
  \operatorname{Per}_{\rm M1}(f,g,a)
  =\{\lambda:\hbox{ some noncontained witness }S
       \hbox{ has }|\operatorname{Stab}_H(S)|>1\},
\]
and
\[
  \operatorname{Prim}_{\rm M1}(f,g,a)
  =\operatorname{Bad}_{\rm nc}(f,g,a)\setminus
    \operatorname{Per}_{\rm M1}(f,g,a).
\]
The proposed M1-v0 conjecture is that, in the polynomial-field high-slack
window
\[
  t\log_2 q\ge(1+\varepsilon)\log_2\binom n a,\qquad
  t\ge C_0 n/\log n,
\]
there is a constant \(B\) such that
\[
  |\operatorname{Prim}_{\rm M1}(f,g,a)|\le n^B
\]
for every line.  Equivalently,
\[
  |\operatorname{Bad}_{\rm nc}(f,g,a)|
  \le |\operatorname{Per}_{\rm M1}(f,g,a)|+n^B.
\]
Thus quotient-periodic support packets are charged explicitly, and the
remaining conjecture is a primitive quotient-normal slope-packing statement.
The companion scanner
\texttt{experimental/scripts/scan\_m1\_exact\_target\_v0.py} enumerates this
quotient-normal object exactly in tiny cases.  Over \(F_5^*\) with
\((n,k,a)=(4,2,3)\) it scans \(625\) quotient pairs and finds maximum primitive
remainder \(4\).  Over \(F_7^*\) with \((n,k,a)=(6,3,4)\) it scans \(117649\)
quotient pairs, with \(3\) of \(15\) supports periodic, and finds maximum
primitive remainder \(7\) and maximum periodic budget \(3\).  These scans are
normalization and falsification harnesses, not asymptotic evidence; in
particular, a full field-sized primitive slope image can already occur in a
tiny quotient-normal example.
The quotient-normal target is exactly the Hankel-pencil target.  For
\(H=\{x:X^n=1\}\), use the weighted syndrome
\[
  \operatorname{Syn}_m(w)=\sum_{x\in H}(x/n)w(x)x^m,\qquad 0\le m<n-k .
\]
If \(S=H\setminus T\), \(|S|=a\), \(|T|=j\), \(t=a-k\), and
\(\ell_T(X)=\prod_{x\in T}(X-x)=\sum_{h=0}^j\ell_hX^h\), then
\[
  w|_S\in\RS[F,S,k]
  \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
  \sum_{h=0}^j\ell_h\operatorname{Syn}_{m+h}(w)=0
  \quad(0\le m<t).
\]
Consequently a quotient-normal pair \((\phi,Y)\) contributes the slope
\(\lambda\) on \(S=H\setminus T\) exactly when
\[
  (H(\operatorname{Syn}(Y))-\lambda H(\operatorname{Syn}(\phi)))\ell_T=0,\qquad
  H(\operatorname{Syn}(\phi))\ell_T\ne0 .
\]
For the original line notation \(\phi=-g\), \(Y=f\), this is Przemek's
\((H(u)+zH(v))\ell_T=0\) form up to the direction sign.  The verifier
\texttt{experimental/scripts/verify\_m1\_exact\_target\_hankel\_equivalence.py}
checks the direct and Hankel classifications over the same \(F_5^*\) and
\(F_7^*\) quotient-normal scans, including all \(117649\) quotient pairs in
the latter.
The first \(t=2\) packet lemma is also extracted.  For a complement \(T\),
write
\[
  a_T=H_{2,j}(\operatorname{Syn}(Y))\ell_T,\qquad
  b_T=H_{2,j}(\operatorname{Syn}(\phi))\ell_T\in F^2 .
\]
Then \(T\) contributes a finite noncontained slope iff
\[
  b_T\ne0,\qquad a_T=\lambda b_T
\]
for a unique \(\lambda\).  If two one-exchange complements
\(T_x=R\cup\{x\}\) and \(T_y=R\cup\{y\}\) contribute the same \(\lambda\), then
with \(w_\lambda=Y-\lambda\phi\),
\[
  H_{3,j-1}(\operatorname{Syn}(w_\lambda))\ell_R=0.
\]
Thus a same-slope one-exchange collision lifts to the next-slack core/root-slice
ledger.  The verifier
\texttt{experimental/scripts/verify\_m1\_hankel\_t2\_one\_exchange\_lift.py}
checks this determinant gate and core lift over exact \(F_5^*\) and \(F_7^*\)
tiny scans, verifying \(3480\) and \(34650\) one-exchange core lifts
respectively.
The same consecutive-Hankel structure classifies the whole fixed-slope
two-root plane over any \((j-2)\)-core.  Write
\[
  c_i=\sum_h(\ell_R)_h\operatorname{Syn}_{i+h}(w_\lambda),
  \qquad 0\le i\le3,
\]
and \(T=R\cup\{x,y\}\), \(\sigma=x+y\), \(\pi=xy\).  Then activity over \(R\)
is
\[
  c_2-\sigma c_1+\pi c_0=0,\qquad
  c_3-\sigma c_2+\pi c_1=0.
\]
If the coefficient matrix has rank two there is at most one domain pair; if
the rank-one system is consistent, the rows are proportional and force
\(c_i=c_0q^i\), so the solution line is
\(\pi=q\sigma-q^2\), equivalently \((x-q)(y-q)=0\).  Thus the only positive
dimensional fixed-slope core-plane packet is a fixed-root/root-slice packet.
The fully degenerate compatible case is exactly
\[
  H_{4,j-2}(\operatorname{Syn}(w_\lambda))\ell_R=0,
\]
so it is already a deeper lower-core Hankel ledger.  Hence non-fixed
product-Mobius or fixed-sum variable-line packets do not occur inside one
fixed same-slope fiber.
Equivalently, every two-edge corner in the active one-exchange graph has an
additive depth charge.  If two active neighbors of \(T\) replace the same root
of \(T\), the corner is a star and is charged to the common
\(H_{3,j-1}\) core.  If they replace two distinct roots \(x_1,x_2\), the three
active two-root extensions over \(R=T\setminus\{x_1,x_2\}\) cannot lie on a
fixed-root rank-one line; therefore the full-plane alternative holds and
\[
  H_{4,j-2}(\operatorname{Syn}(w_\lambda))\ell_R=0.
\]
Thus same-slope graph branching either stays in one star ledger or descends one
rung to a lower Hankel core; it does not create a separate non-fixed
variable-line packet.
The lower-core alternative is the first case of a general additive descent.
For any \(\tau\ge1\), if three \(j\)-locators
\[
  T=R\cup\{x_1,x_2\},\qquad
  T_1=R\cup\{y_1,x_2\},\qquad
  T_2=R\cup\{x_1,y_2\}
\]
all satisfy \(H_{\tau,j}(s)\ell=0\), then subtracting the first two and the
first and third equations gives
\[
  H_{\tau,j-1}(s)(X-x_2)\ell_R=0,\qquad
  H_{\tau,j-1}(s)(X-x_1)\ell_R=0.
\]
Their difference gives \(H_{\tau,j-2}(s)\ell_R=0\); feeding this back into
one linear equation and then into the original quadratic equation gives
\[
  H_{\tau+2,j-2}(s)\ell_R=0.
\]
Thus a distinct-root corner adds two Hankel rows and removes two locator roots
in one lossless step.  This is the structural additive-depth mechanism behind
the \(t=2\) corner audit.
The corresponding row-level graph statement is also lossless.  In the active
one-exchange graph for \(H_{\tau,j}(s)\), any active edge
\[
  C\cup\{x\}\ --\ C\cup\{y\}
\]
lifts to
\[
  H_{\tau+1,j-1}(s)\ell_C=0.
\]
Therefore every nontrivial component is either a single star packet with this
one-row-deeper core, or it contains a distinct-root corner and hence an
\(H_{\tau+2,j-2}\) lower-core witness.  This gives a same-slope component
ledger at every row level: branching is paid by additive Hankel descent.
Writing \(A^+_{\tau,j}(s)\) for non-isolated active \(j\)-locators, this also
gives the summable bound
\[
  |A^+_{\tau,j}(s)|
  \le
  (n-j+1)\,\#\{C: |C|=j-1,\ H_{\tau+1,j-1}(s)\ell_C=0\}.
\]
Star components inject into these one-row cores.  Non-star components inject
into lower cores \(R\) with \(H_{\tau+2,j-2}(s)\ell_R=0\), because such an
active lower core makes the full two-root Johnson plane over \(R\) active and
connected.  Thus both support mass and branching component count are paid by
lower Hankel ledgers with explicit local factors.
The complementary isolated vertices have an exact first-boundary form.  If
\(T\) is active, \(x\in T\), \(C=T\setminus\{x\}\), and
\[
  c_i=\sum_h(\ell_C)_h s_{i+h},
\]
then \(H_{\tau,j}(s)\ell_T=0\) implies
\[
  H_{\tau+1,j-1}(s)\ell_C
  =c_0(1,x,x^2,\ldots,x^\tau).
\]
The extension \(C\cup\{y\}\) is active only when either this vector is zero or
\(y=x\).  Hence, for \(j<n\), \(T\) is isolated exactly when all deletion
cores have nonzero root-marked boundary vectors.  Isolated mass is therefore a
root-marked first-boundary residual, not a hidden same-slope packet.
Writing \(B^{\rm rm}_{\tau+1,j-1}(s)\) for the root-marked boundary pairs
\((C,x)\) with nonzero scalar \(c_0\), every isolated \(T\) contributes its
\(j\) marked deletions injectively, so
\[
  j\,|\mathrm{Iso}_{\tau,j}(s)|
  \le |B^{\rm rm}_{\tau+1,j-1}(s)|.
\]
The full first-boundary incidence ledger is exact.  If
\(E_{\tau+1,j-1}(s)\) is the set of active one-exchange edge cores, then
\(E_{\tau+1,j-1}(s)\) is exactly the zero first-boundary core set, and for
\(1\le j<n\),
\[
  j\,|A_{\tau,j}(s)|
  =
  (n-j+1)|E_{\tau+1,j-1}(s)|
  + |B^{\rm rm}_{\tau+1,j-1}(s)|.
\]
Thus every active deletion incidence is either a zero first-boundary edge-core
incidence or a nonzero root-marked first-boundary incidence.
This split is rootwise.  For each \(x\in H\), let \(A^x_{\tau,j}(s)\) be the
active locators containing \(x\), let \(Z^x_{\tau+1,j-1}(s)\) be the zero
first-boundary cores not containing \(x\), and let
\(B^x_{\tau+1,j-1}(s)\) be the root-marked boundary pairs marked by \(x\).
Then
\[
  |A^x_{\tau,j}(s)|
  =
  |Z^x_{\tau+1,j-1}(s)|+|B^x_{\tau+1,j-1}(s)|.
\]
So the nonzero root-marked chart is exactly the fixed-root active slice after
zero-boundary star cores are removed; it is not an independent same-slope
branching family.
The fixed-root slice itself is a standard Hankel kernel after a root
difference.  With
\[
  (\Delta_x s)_i=s_{i+1}-xs_i,
\]
one has
\[
  H_{\tau,j}(s)((X-x)L)=H_{\tau,j-1}(\Delta_x s)L.
\]
Thus deletion of the fixed root identifies \(A^x_{\tau,j}(s)\) with
\[
  \{C\subset H\setminus\{x\}: |C|=j-1,\,
    H_{\tau,j-1}(\Delta_xs)\ell_C=0\},
\]
and \(B^x\) is this root-slice difference kernel with the zero-boundary
subkernel removed.  Inside the difference kernel, that zero-boundary subkernel
is just the single first-row cut
\[
  H_{1,j-1}(s)\ell_C=0.
\]
Equivalently, \(B^x\) is the nonzero slice \(H_{1,j-1}(s)\ell_C\ne0\) inside
the root-difference Hankel kernel.
The fixed-root identity iterates: for distinct roots \(x_1,\ldots,x_m\),
\[
  H_{\tau,j}(s)((X-x_1)\cdots(X-x_m)L)
  =
  H_{\tau,j-m}(\Delta_{x_m}\cdots\Delta_{x_1}s)L.
\]
Thus every fixed-root deletion rung is an ordinary root-difference Hankel
kernel, not a new packet family.
Moreover the exact first-boundary incidence identity persists on every such
rung.  If \(X=(x_1,\ldots,x_m)\), \(q=j-m>0\), and
\(d=\Delta_{x_m}\cdots\Delta_{x_1}s\), then the active \(q\)-locators over
\(H\setminus X\) satisfy
\[
