  q|K^X_{\tau,q}(s)|
  =
  (n-j+1)|Z^X_{\tau+1,q-1}(s)|
  +|B^{X,\rm rm}_{\tau+1,q-1}(s)|.
\]
The extension factor remains \(n-j+1\), since
\(|H\setminus X|-(q-1)=n-j+1\).  Thus an already stripped fixed-root chain has
the same zero-boundary/root-marked additive split as the original rung.
This is set-theoretic.  The incidence pairs \((C,y)\), with
\(C\in K^X_{\tau,q}(s)\) and \(y\in C\), map by \(C\mapsto C\setminus\{y\}\)
to the next fixed-root layer \(K^{X\cup\{y\}}_{\tau,q-1}(s)\).  For a
\((q-1)\)-core \(D\), this fiber is either the whole zero-boundary star over
\(D\), the single nonzero root-marked extension, or empty.  Hence the
root-marked residual is literally the nonzero part of the next fixed-root
incidence sequence, not a new packet family.
Iterating this gives a first-zero stopping decomposition.  Along any ordered
deletion path of an active fixed-chain kernel, each nonzero boundary step is
root-marked and moves to the next fixed-root layer.  The path either first
hits a zero-boundary star at some depth, or reaches the bottom condition
\[
  H_{\tau,0}(\Delta_{X\cup C}s)1=0.
\]
Thus the fixed-root filtration has finite depth and no independent
multiplicative residual branch.
Consequently the ordered path set has an exact first-zero ledger:
\[
  |\mathrm{Path}^X_{\tau,q}|
  =
  \sum_{h=1}^q |\mathrm{Stop}^X_h|+|\mathrm{Term}^X|.
\]
Each \(\mathrm{Stop}^X_h\) charges to a one-row deeper zero-boundary ledger:
if \(P\) is the ordered prefix before the first zero stop, then the remaining
core \(D\) satisfies
\[
  H_{\tau+1,q-h}(\Delta_{X\cup P}s)\ell_D=0,
\]
and
\[
  |\mathrm{Stop}^X_h|
  \le (q-h)!(n-j+1)
     \sum_P |Z^{X\cup P}_{\tau+1,q-h}(s)|.
\]
Thus all nonterminal ordered mass is paid by deeper zero-boundary ledgers; the
only residual of this filtration is the terminal zero-free flag set.
The terminal flags reduce to unordered bottom supports.  Define
\[
  \mathrm{Bot}^X_{\tau,q}(s)
  =
  \{C\in K^X_{\tau,q}(s):
    H_{\tau,0}(\Delta_{X\cup C}s)1=0\}.
\]
Since the \(\Delta\)-operators commute, the bottom condition is independent of
the deletion order, and
\[
  |\mathrm{Term}^X|\le q!\,|\mathrm{Bot}^X_{\tau,q}(s)|.
\]
By the iterated fixed-root identity, \(\mathrm{Bot}^X_{\tau,q}(s)=
K^X_{\tau,q}(s)\), so this is an ordering-forgetting ledger rather than a
support saving.  The actual terminal condition is zero-freeness of the
intermediate scalar cuts.  If \(C_h=D_h\cup\{y_h\}\) is the current active
rung, then
\[
  H_{\tau+1,|D_h|}(\Delta_{X_h}s)\ell_{D_h}
  =
  c_h(1,y_h,\ldots,y_h^\tau),
  \qquad
  c_h=H_{1,|D_h|}(\Delta_{X_h}s)\ell_{D_h}.
\]
The ordered path is terminal exactly when \(c_1\cdots c_q\ne0\).  Hence the
remaining terminal object is a zero-free flag problem inside the fixed-root
support kernel.
This residual has an exact deletion-tree recursion.  For
\(C\in K^X_{\tau,q}(s)\), put
\[
  c_X(y;C)=H_{1,q-1}(\Delta_Xs)\ell_{C\setminus\{y\}},
  \qquad
  \mathrm{NZ}_X(C)=\{y\in C:c_X(y;C)\ne0\}.
\]
If \(T_X(C)\) denotes the number of terminal deletion flags starting from
\((X,C)\), with \(T_X(\emptyset)=1\), then
\[
  T_X(C)=\sum_{y\in\mathrm{NZ}_X(C)}
    T_{X,y}(C\setminus\{y\}).
\]
Thus any support with more than one terminal ordering contains a descendant
\((X',C')\) with at least two nonzero scalar exits.  The remaining terminal
obstruction is therefore branching of these first-row scalar cuts, not an
unstructured factorial ordering loss.
Each branch pair has a lower-core two-mode normal form.  If \(y,z\in C\)
are two nonzero exits, \(R=C\setminus\{y,z\}\), and
\[
  H_{\tau+2,|R|}(\Delta_Xs)\ell_R=(g_0,\ldots,g_{\tau+1}),
\]
then
\[
  g_i={c_X(y;C)y^i-c_X(z;C)z^i\over y-z},
  \qquad 0\le i\le \tau+1.
\]
Thus any terminal multiflag support contains a productive branch pair carried
by a two-mode lower-boundary packet.
More generally, if \(Y\subseteq\mathrm{NZ}_X(C)\) has size \(m\ge2\),
\(R=C\setminus Y\), and
\[
  H_{\tau+m,|R|}(\Delta_Xs)\ell_R=(g_0,\ldots,g_{\tau+m-1}),
\]
then
\[
  g_i=\sum_{y\in Y}
    {c_X(y;C)\over\prod_{z\in Y,\ z\ne y}(y-z)}\,y^i,
  \qquad 0\le i\le \tau+m-1.
\]
Thus a full terminal branch vertex is a sparse lower-boundary moment packet
on its nonzero exit roots; productive multiflag mass is carried by productive
mode packets.
At a fixed anchor, this packet label reconstructs the branch data.  If
\(Y=NZ_X(C)\), \(R=C\setminus Y\), and
\[
  a_y={c_X(y;C)\over\prod_{z\in Y,\ z\ne y}(y-z)},
\]
then the anchored label \((R,Y,(a_y))\) recovers \(C=R\cup Y\) and all exit
scalars
\[
  c_X(y;C)=a_y\prod_{z\in Y,\ z\ne y}(y-z).
\]
Thus repeated production of a packet label cannot come from ambiguity at one
fixed lower core; it must come from different anchors or a global relation
between them.
If \(m\le\tau+1\), this packet has a nonzero rank certificate:
\[
  \det(g_{r+s})_{0\le r,s<m}
  =
  \left(\prod_{y\in Y}
    {c_X(y;C)\over\prod_{z\in Y,\ z\ne y}(y-z)}\right)
  \prod_{y<z}(z-y)^2 \ne 0.
\]
Thus visible productive mode packets are determinant-nondegenerate sparse
moment packets.
They also peel losslessly.  If \(E\subseteq Y\) and
\(\ell_E=\prod_{e\in E}(X-e)\), then applying \(\ell_E\) to the packet gives
\[
  H_{\tau+m-|E|,|R|+|E|}(\Delta_Xs)\ell_{R\cup E}
  =
  \sum_{y\in Y\setminus E}
    a_y\ell_E(y)(1,y,\ldots,y^{\tau+m-|E|-1}).
\]
Every proper peel is nonzero, while \(E=Y\) gives exactly the active
annihilator for \(C\).  Thus the terminal packet has no hidden premature
zero-boundary collapse inside its mode set.
With one more visible moment, the packet determines its branch-mode locator.
If \(g_0,\ldots,g_{2m-1}\) are visible, then the nonsingular system
\[
  \sum_{h=0}^{m-1} b_h g_{r+h}=-g_{r+m},\qquad 0\le r<m,
\]
recovers the unique monic annihilator
\[
  X^m+b_{m-1}X^{m-1}+\cdots+b_0=\prod_{y\in Y}(X-y).
\]
No lower-degree nonzero polynomial annihilates the visible packet.
This residual also has additive one-exchange descent.  If
\(C_y=R\cup\{y\}\) and \(C_z=R\cup\{z\}\) are two root-marked cores in the
same fixed-root slice, then both are active for
\(H_{\tau,j-1}(\Delta_xs)\), so subtracting the two one-exchange equations
gives
\[
  H_{\tau+1,j-2}(\Delta_xs)\ell_R=0.
\]
Thus non-isolated root-marked residual mass is charged to one-row-deeper
root-difference lower cores, with the same local factor \(n-j+1\).
The isolated residual vertices have the parallel marked-boundary criterion:
for \(2\le j\le n-2\), a core \(C\in B^x\) is isolated in the residual graph
iff every deletion \(C\setminus\{y\}\) has nonzero
\[
  H_{\tau+1,j-2}(\Delta_xs)\ell_{C\setminus\{y\}}.
\]
Consequently isolated residual mass contributes to a second marked-boundary
ledger with weight \(j-1\).
This local statement globalizes across connected components.  In the active
one-exchange graph for fixed \(w_\lambda\), if a nontrivial component has no
lower-core corner, then every two-edge corner is a star corner.  Choosing one
edge with common core \(C\), induction along paths shows that every vertex in
the component contains \(C\), so the component is a single star packet and
\[
  H_{3,j-1}(\operatorname{Syn}(w_\lambda))\ell_C=0.
\]
Otherwise the component contains an \(H_{4,j-2}\) lower-core witness.  Thus
same-slope components are star packets after lower-core charges, rather than
independent branching families.
The triangle-packet verifier
\texttt{experimental/scripts/verify\_m1\_hankel\_t2\_triangle\_packets.py}
checks the next local shape at the combined-syndrome level.  Every pairwise
one-exchange triangle in a fixed-slope fiber is either a star, already covered
by the \(H_{3,j-1}\) core lift, or a top packet contained in a common
\((j+1)\)-set \(U\), in which case
\[
  H_{1,j+1}(\operatorname{Syn}(w_\lambda))\ell_U=0.
\]
If a full top packet is active, meaning all \(j+1\) complements
\(U\setminus\{x\}\) are active at the same slope, then these \(j+1\) locators
form a basis of \(F[X]_{\le j}\).  The two \(t=2\) Hankel rows therefore
annihilate every degree-\(\le j\) polynomial, so all \(j+2=r\) syndrome
coordinates of \(w_\lambda\) vanish.  Thus \(w_\lambda\in C\), and full top
packets belong to the global-codeword/tangent ledger.
Consequently every nonzero top packet has at most \(j\) active complements.
In the first exact top-packet case \(F_7^*\), \((n,k,a,j)=(6,2,4,2)\), the
verifier enumerates all \(2401\) combined syndromes and finds \(420\) star
triangles, \(20\) top triangles, and \(20\) full top cliques; every full top
clique has zero combined syndrome, and the maximum nonzero top active size is
exactly \(j=2\).
The same verifier now also checks all nonzero core planes in the \(F_7^*\)
\((n,k,a,j)=(6,1,3,3)\) and \((6,2,4,2)\) cases: every plane is a point,
fixed-root line, full lower-core plane, or empty/inconsistent, with no
non-fixed same-slope line.  In the \(F_7^*,j=3\) case it also verifies
\(8640\) nonzero star corners and \(3240\) nonzero lower-core corners, with
maximal per-syndrome counts \(60\) and \(90\), respectively.
At the component level, the same \(F_7^*,j=3\) audit has \(540\) nonzero star
components of size \(4\) and \(36\) nonzero lower-core components of size
\(10\); the \(F_7^*,j=2\) audit has \(36\) nonzero star components of size
\(5\) and no nonzero lower-core components.  The component ledger is exact in
the \(F_7^*,j=2\) nonzero audit, while the \(F_7^*,j=3\) nonzero audit uses at
most \(5\) edge cores and \(1\) lower-core witness per syndrome.  The same
scan checks the isolated criterion, with at most \(2\) nonzero isolated active
locators in the \(F_7^*,j=3\) case and at most \(1\) in the other listed
cases; the marked-boundary ledger has maximum nonzero marked-boundary count
\(10\) in the \(F_7^*,j=3\) audit and \(5\) in the \(F_7^*,j=2\) audit.  The
first-boundary identity above is checked exactly: the zero-boundary cores
coincide with the active edge cores, with maximum nonzero counts \(5\) in the
\(F_7^*,j=3\) audit, \(1\) in the \(F_7^*,j=2\) audit, and \(0\) in the
listed \(j=1\) audits.  The rootwise fixed-root decomposition is also checked;
the maximum nonzero fixed-root active counts are \(10\) for \(F_7^*,j=3\),
\(5\) for \(F_7^*,j=2\), and \(1\) in the listed \(j=1\) audits, with zero
rootwise defect in every enumerated syndrome.  The root-difference Hankel
kernel identification is checked in the same run and has the same maximum
nonzero counts as the fixed-root active slices.  The single-row slice
description of the root-marked residual is also checked with zero defect.
Residual one-exchange edges descend as above; the exact scans have maximum
nonzero residual lower-core counts \(5\) for \(F_7^*,j=3\), \(1\) for
\(F_7^*,j=2\), and \(0\) in the listed \(j=1\) audits.  The isolated residual
criterion and second-boundary ledger are checked too; the maximum nonzero
isolated residual counts are \(6\) for \(F_7^*,j=3\), \(2\) for \(F_7^*,j=2\),
and \(1\) in the listed \(j=1\) audits.  The verifier also checks the iterated
root-difference identity exactly, with \(2352980\) checks in the largest
listed case and zero defects.  It also audits the recursive fixed-chain
first-boundary identity with zero defects; the largest listed case checks
\(352947\) rung identities up to chain length \(2\), with maximum nonzero
rung counts \(10\) active cores, \(5\) zero-boundary cores, and \(4\)
root-marked boundaries.  The set-level filtration identity behind this count
is checked too: the largest listed case checks \(61740\) fixed-rung incidence
pairs, with maximum nonzero incidence count \(20\) and zero defects.  The
same scan checks \(102900\) ordered deletion paths in the first-zero stopping
decomposition: \(14700\), \(10080\), and \(4320\) stop at depths \(1\), \(2\),
and \(3\), respectively, while \(73800\) reach the terminal difference
condition; all path checks have zero defect.  Equivalently, the ordered path
partition is \(102900=29100+73800\), with zero partition defect.  Among
nonzero syndromes the maximum first-zero stop count is \(140\), and the
maximum terminal count is \(30\).  The terminal bottom-support reduction is
also audited: the \(73800\) terminal paths reduce to \(37080\) local
bottom-support instances with total factorial capacity \(73800\), maximum
nonzero local bottom support count \(2\), and zero slack.  The scalar-chain
criterion is checked as well: the largest listed case has \(174600\) nonzero
first-row scalar steps, with maximum nonzero per-syndrome count \(66\).
The deletion-tree recursion is checked on \(48020\) active starting cores in
the same largest case, with zero recursion defects; the audit finds \(34560\)
branching vertices and \(19440\) multiflag starting cores.  Among nonzero
syndromes the maximum terminal-tree count is \(6\), with at most \(4\)
branching vertices in one tree and at most \(2\) multiflag starting cores in
one local audit.
The same audit checks the two-mode lower-boundary identity for \(43200\)
branch pairs, with zero defects; \(41040\) of these are productive branch
pairs.  Among nonzero syndromes the maximum branch-pair count in one terminal
tree is \(6\), and all six can be productive.
The all-exit sparse packet identity is checked for \(34560\) branch mode
packets: \(30240\) have mode size \(2\), \(4320\) have mode size \(3\), and
\(32400\) are productive.
All \(34560\) of these packets have enough visible moments for the nonzero
rank-certificate determinant check, again with sizes \(2\) and \(3\) as above.
The lossless peeling identity is checked on \(120960\) nonempty mode subsets;
\(114480\) of these are productive-packet peels.
Minimal-annihilator recovery is checked whenever \(2m\) moments are visible:
in the largest case this recovers all \(30240\) mode-size-\(2\) locators,
including all \(28080\) productive size-\(2\) packets.
The mode-size-\(3\) packets need one additional moment beyond the current
\(t=2\) window.
This caveat is sharp.  An \(m\)-mode terminal packet at row level \(\tau\)
has the window \(g_0,\ldots,g_{\tau+m-1}\), so \(m\le\tau\) gives the \(2m\)
moments needed for locator recovery, while \(m=\tau+1\) gives only
\(2m-1\).  With \(2m-1\) moments, any two different \(m\)-mode explanations
must be disjoint: otherwise their difference is supported on at most
\(2m-1\) roots and is killed by an invertible square Vandermonde matrix.
Conversely, for any \(2m\) distinct roots the \((2m-1)\times 2m\)
Vandermonde matrix has a one-dimensional kernel with no zero coordinate, so
splitting those roots into two \(m\)-sets gives two disjoint nonzero packets
with the same first \(2m-1\) moments.  The next moment separates them by the
full Vandermonde determinant.  More explicitly, for
\(W=Y\sqcup Z\), \(P_W(X)=\prod_{w\in W}(X-w)\), the alias condition is
\[
  a_y=\mu/P_W'(y)\qquad (y\in Y)
\]
for one \(\mu\ne0\), with alias amplitudes \(-\mu/P_W'(z)\) on \(Z\).
For a terminal branch packet this cancels the internal branch Vandermonde
denominators.  Since
\[
  a_y={c_X(y;C)\over d_Y(y)},\qquad
  d_Y(y)=\prod_{u\in Y,\ u\ne y}(y-u),
\]
and \(P_{Y\cup Z}'(y)=d_Y(y)\ell_Z(y)\), a disjoint \(Z\)-alias is
equivalent to the constant-product scalar fit
\[
  c_X(y;C)\ell_Z(y)=\mu\qquad (y\in Y).
\]
For any domain \(H\) of size \(n\), the same square Vandermonde argument gives
a matching-type fiber bound for the boundary moment map: two distinct supports
in one \(2m-1\)-moment fiber are disjoint, and amplitudes are unique once the
support is fixed.  Hence every fiber has size at most \(\lfloor n/m\rfloor\).
Thus the boundary endpoint has no overlapping support cluster even before the
disjoint aliases are classified.
The labeled nonzero \(m\)-mode packet family has size
\[
  (|F|-1)^m\binom{n}{m},
\]
so the boundary image size is between this count divided by \(\lfloor n/m\rfloor\)
and the count itself.  For fixed \(m=\tau+1\) and polynomial field size, the
visible boundary sparse-packet type is therefore polynomial-sized locally.
Together with the \(2m\)-moment recovery theorem, this gives a local
packet-type ledger for all visible terminal branch packets at row level
\(\tau\): the ambient labeled packet space has size at most
\[
  \sum_{m=2}^{\tau+1} (|F|-1)^m\binom{n}{m}.
\]
For fixed \(\tau\) and \(|F|=\operatorname{poly}(n)\), this rules out a new
super-polynomial family of visible sparse packet labels.  It does not count
how often terminal deletion trees produce a label; repeated production must
still be charged separately.
In the full-domain boundary case \(H=\{x:x^n=1\}\), \(n=2m\), the only
possible disjoint domain alias is \(Z=H\setminus Y\), and
\[
  P_H'(y)=ny^{-1}.
\]
Hence the complementary alias exists exactly when
\[
  a_y/y=\hbox{constant}\qquad (y\in Y),
\]
or equivalently when the true packet amplitudes are root-linear on \(Y\).
This gives a full-domain support dichotomy: the first \(2m-1\) moments either
determine \(Y\) uniquely among domain \(m\)-sets, or the packet is
root-linear and the only other domain support is \(H\setminus Y\), with
amplitudes \(-\nu z\).  Once \(Y\) is unique, its amplitudes are recovered
from the first \(m\) moments by the square Vandermonde matrix on \(Y\).
Therefore the labeled full-domain aliased endpoint has exactly
\[
  (|F|-1)\binom{n}{m}
\]
packets, and its visible moment sequences are paired by
\((Y,\nu)\leftrightarrow(H\setminus Y,-\nu)\).  For fixed \(m=\tau+1\), this
is a polynomial ledger in the polynomial-field window, not an arbitrary
\((F^*)^m\) amplitude family.
Equivalently, the full-domain boundary moment map has singleton fibers except
for those root-linear complement pairs, so its image size is
\[
  \bigl((|F|-1)^m-(|F|-1)\bigr)\binom{n}{m}
  +{(|F|-1)\binom{n}{m}\over2}.
\]
Thus rank visibility alone cannot recover boundary packets in complete
generality; M1 must either obtain one further structural moment, charge
possible disjoint aliases, or use the special branch-amplitude constraints to
bound the boundary packet family directly.  The verifier searches for such
aliases in the \(F_7^*\), mode-size-\(3\) boundary packets and checks that any
equal-size alias would have to be disjoint, as predicted by the Vandermonde
argument.  In the largest current audit, only \(120\) of the \(4320\) boundary
packets admit such an alias; the other \(4200\) are support-identifiable among
domain \(3\)-sets despite lacking the formal Prony recovery moment.  The
constant-product scalar fits have exactly the same \(4200/120\) histogram,
and in this full-domain boundary audit the root-linear amplitude test has the
same histogram.  The \(120\) root-linear packets occupy all \(\binom63=20\)
supports with \(6=|F|-1\) scalar multiples each, giving \(4200+120/2=4260\)
visible endpoint sequences.  The boundary fiber-size histogram is \(4200\)
labels in singleton fibers and \(120\) labels in fibers of size \(2\), with
maximum fiber size \(2=\lfloor6/3\rfloor\).
Thus visible packet collisions have a precise scalar-cut form.  Away from the
maximal boundary, or whenever the boundary endpoint is support-unique, two
terminal branch packets from anchors \((X,R)\) and \((X',R')\) with common
mode support \(Y\) collide if and only if
\[
  c_X(y;R\cup Y)=c_{X'}(y;R'\cup Y)\qquad(y\in Y).
\]
At the maximal boundary, the only additional collision is the disjoint alias:
for \(Y'\cap Y=\emptyset\) it is exactly the scalar fit
\[
  c_X(y;R\cup Y)\ell_{Y'}(y)=\mu\qquad(y\in Y).
\]
In the full-domain case \(n=2|Y|\), this is the root-linear condition
\[
  {c_X(y;R\cup Y)\over y\,d_Y(y)}=\hbox{constant}\qquad(y\in Y).
\]
So the remaining repeated-production problem is a same-support scalar-cut
collision problem, plus the isolated boundary disjoint-alias ledger.
The verifier now audits this production question directly: in the largest
\(F_7^*\) case, all \(34560\) visible terminal packet productions have
singleton same-syndrome production fibers, and all \(32400\) productive
productions are singleton as well.
There is also an anchor-base kernel drop.  Collapse a branch anchor to
\(A=X\cup R\), \(d=|A|\).  The iterated difference identity gives
\[
  H_{\tau+m,d}(s)\ell_A
  =H_{\tau+m,|R|}(\Delta_Xs)\ell_R
  =\left(\sum_{y\in Y} a_y y^i\right)_{0\le i\le\tau+m-1}.
\]
Hence two same-size visible moment productions with distinct collapsed anchor
bases \(A,A'\) satisfy
\[
  H_{\tau+m,d}(s)(\ell_A-\ell_{A'})=0,
\]
and \(\deg(\ell_A-\ell_{A'})<d\).  Thus a genuine distinct-anchor-base
collision is a lower-degree Hankel-kernel vector; only same collapsed-anchor
ordering multiplicity and the already isolated boundary disjoint aliases
remain outside this kernel-drop charge.  The verifier checks the anchor-base
image identity for all \(34560\) packets in the largest \(F_7^*\) audit,
including all \(32400\) productive packets.
This kernel drop also has an exchange-distance form.  If
\(A=I\cup U\), \(A'=I\cup V\), \(|U|=|V|=h\), and \(U\cap V=\emptyset\),
then
\[
  \ell_A-\ell_{A'}=\ell_I(\ell_U-\ell_V),\qquad
  \deg(\ell_U-\ell_V)<h.
\]
So an exchange-distance \(h\) collision is a bounded-degree
balanced-difference kernel over the common anchor core.  For \(h=1\),
\(\ell_U-\ell_V\) is a nonzero scalar, hence the collision forces
\[
  H_{\tau+m,|I|}(s)\ell_I=0.
\]
The one-exchange anchor collision is therefore already a lower-core
Hankel-kernel event.  In the largest \(F_7^*\) audit the verifier checks
\(30240\) such one-exchange common cores, including all \(28080\) productive
size-\(2\) packets, with no kernel hits.
The anchor-base packet condition is also reversible.  If \(A\cap Y=\emptyset\)
and
\[
  H_{\tau+m,|A|}(s)\ell_A
  =\left(\sum_{y\in Y}a_y y^i\right)_{0\le i\le\tau+m-1},
  \qquad a_y\ne0,
\]
then \(S=A\cup Y\) is active,
\[
  H_{\tau,|S|}(s)\ell_S=0,
\]
and every \(y\in Y\) is a nonzero root-marked boundary:
\[
  H_{\tau+1,|S|-1}(s)\ell_{S\setminus\{y\}}
  =a_y\prod_{z\in Y,\ z\ne y}(y-z)\,(1,y,\ldots,y^\tau).
\]
Thus the terminal packet is an intrinsic split-support certificate, not only
a deletion-tree artifact.  The verifier checks this in the largest \(F_7^*\)
audit with \(34560\) split-support checks and \(73440\) root-marked
split-boundary checks, including \(32400\) productive supports and \(69120\)
productive boundaries.
Conversely, an active split support with nonzero root-marked selected modes
reconstructs the packet: if
\[
  H_{\tau+1,|S|-1}(s)\ell_{S\setminus\{y\}}
  =c_y(1,y,\ldots,y^\tau)\qquad(y\in Y),
\]
then
\[
  H_{\tau+m,|A|}(s)\ell_A
  =
  \left(\sum_{y\in Y}{c_y\over
    \prod_{z\in Y,\ z\ne y}(y-z)}\,y^i\right)_{0\le i\le\tau+m-1}.
\]
So the split-support certificate is equivalent to the sparse anchor-base
packet.  The verifier checks \(34560\) such roundtrips in the largest
\(F_7^*\) audit, with \(32400\) productive roundtrips.
For a fixed active total support \(S\), define \(b_x(S)\) by
\[
  H_{\tau+1,|S|-1}(s)\ell_{S\setminus\{x\}}
  = b_x(S)(1,x,\ldots,x^\tau).
\]
Let \(M(S)=\{x\in S:b_x(S)\ne0\}\).  Then certificates with total support
\(S\) and mode size \(m\) are exactly the
choices \(Y\subseteq M(S)\), \(|Y|=m\), with amplitudes
\[
  a_y={b_y(S)\over \prod_{z\in Y,\ z\ne y}(y-z)}.
\]
Thus the fixed-\(S\) certificate capacity is \(\binom{|M(S)|}{m}\).  The
verifier audits this factorization in the largest \(F_7^*\) case:
\(25920\) total-support fibers carry \(34560\) labels, maximum fiber size
\(3\), and \(60480\) marked-subset factorizations are checked; the productive
subaudit has \(23760\) fibers, \(32400\) labels, maximum fiber size \(3\),
and \(58320\) factorizations.
Taking all marked exits at once gives a lossless cube.  If \(M=M(S)\), then
for every nonempty \(Y\subseteq M\),
\[
  H_{\tau+|Y|,|S\setminus Y|}(s)\ell_{S\setminus Y}
  =
  \left(\sum_{y\in Y}
    {b_y(S)\over \prod_{z\in Y,\ z\ne y}(y-z)}y^i\right)_
    {0\le i\le \tau+|Y|-1},
\]
and this face is nonzero by the Vandermonde minor on \(Y\).  Thus a support
with \(r\) marked exits contains a canonical zero-free \(r\)-cube of sparse
packet faces.  In the largest \(F_7^*\) audit the verifier checks \(21600\)
produced total supports and \(133920\) nonzero marked-exit faces, with maximum
marked count \(3\); the productive subaudit checks \(19440\) supports and
\(127440\) faces.
The unmarked core \(U(S)=S\setminus M(S)\) is then canonical.  For fixed
\(U\) and \(r=|M(S)|\), the full marked face is an \(r\)-sparse
representation of
\[
  H_{\tau+r,|U|}(s)\ell_U
\]
on \(H\setminus U\).  Hence \(r\le\tau\) gives uniqueness, while
\(r=\tau+1\) gives disjoint boundary fibers of size at most
\(\lfloor(n-|U|)/r\rfloor\).  The largest \(F_7^*\) audit has \(21540\)
canonical marked-core fibers carrying \(21600\) full marked supports, maximum
fiber size \(2\); the productive subaudit has \(19380\) fibers carrying
\(19440\) supports, again maximum \(2\).
The complementary unmarked roots descend to zero ledgers.  If
\(U(S)=S\setminus M(S)\) and \(E\subseteq U(S)\) is nonempty, then the
one-deletion zero identities and the basis
\(\{\ell_{E\setminus\{u\}}:u\in E\}\) give
\[
  H_{\tau+|E|,|S|-|E|}(s)\ell_{S\setminus E}=0.
\]
Thus marked roots form nonzero packet faces, while unmarked roots form a zero
descent cube.  In the largest \(F_7^*\) audit the verifier checks \(4320\)
unmarked zero faces over \(21600\) produced total supports; the productive
subaudit checks \(2160\) such faces.
The two cubes are compatible.  If \(E\subseteq U(S)\) and
\(Y\subseteq M(S)\) are nonempty, deleting \(E\) first keeps each
\(y\in Y\) marked with scalar \(b_y(S)/\ell_E(y)\), so
\[
  H_{\tau+|E|+|Y|,|S|-|E|-|Y|}(s)\ell_{S\setminus(E\cup Y)}
  =
  \left(\sum_{y\in Y}
    {b_y(S)y^i\over
      \ell_E(y)\prod_{z\in Y,\ z\ne y}(y-z)}\right)_
      {0\le i\le\tau+|E|+|Y|-1}.
\]
Thus unmarked deletion shifts depth additively and only rescales the marked
packet.  The largest \(F_7^*\) audit checks \(12960\) mixed faces, with
\(6480\) productive mixed faces.
Consequently the marked frontier is stable under unmarked deletion.  For
\(E\subseteq U(S)\), the active support \(S_E=S\setminus E\) at row
\(\tau+|E|\) has marked exits exactly \(M(S)\): the new marked scalar is
\(b_y(S)/\ell_E(y)\) for \(y\in M(S)\), while every
\(u\in U(S)\setminus E\) remains zero by applying the zero cube to
\(E\cup\{u\}\).  The largest \(F_7^*\) audit checks \(4320\) shifted supports
and \(8640\) shifted boundary roots; the productive subaudit checks \(2160\)
supports and \(4320\) roots.
Equivalently, every face has a two-color normal form.  For
\(E\subseteq U(S)\), \(Y\subseteq M(S)\), and
\(A_{E,Y}=S\setminus(E\cup Y)\),
\[
H_{\tau+|E|+|Y|,|A_{E,Y}|}(s)\ell_{A_{E,Y}}=0
\quad\text{if }Y=\emptyset,
\]
while for \(Y\ne\emptyset\) it is the sparse packet
\[
  \left(\sum_{y\in Y}
    {b_y(S)y^i\over
      \ell_E(y)\prod_{z\in Y,\ z\ne y}(y-z)}\right)_
      {0\le i\le\tau+|E|+|Y|-1},
\]
which is nonzero.  Thus the internal split-support recursion has only zero
descent faces and marked sparse-packet faces, with additive row shift.
This gives a compact closure criterion.  For \(1\le m\le\tau+1\), let
\(\mathcal C_m^{\le\tau+1}(s)\) be the split-support certificates of mode
size \(m\) whose total support has at most \(\tau+1\) marked exits, and let
\[
  \mathrm{Core}_r(s)
  =\{U(S)=S\setminus M(S): S\text{ active and }|M(S)|=r\}.
\]
Then the preceding fixed-total-support factorization and canonical
marked-core fiber bound give
\[
|\mathcal C_m^{\le\tau+1}(s)|
\le
\sum_{r=m}^{\tau}\binom{r}{m} |\mathrm{Core}_r(s)|
+\binom{\tau+1}{m}
  \sum_{U\in\mathrm{Core}_{\tau+1}(s)}
     \left\lfloor {n-|U| \over \tau+1}\right\rfloor .
\]
Indeed, for fixed \(S\) there are exactly \(\binom{|M(S)|}{m}\)
certificates; for fixed \(U\) and \(r\le\tau\) the full marked face recovers
the marked support uniquely, while for \(r=\tau+1\) the possible marked
supports over \(U\) are disjoint and matching-bounded.  Hence, for fixed
\(\tau\), polynomial bounds for the canonical core ledgers
\(\mathrm{Core}_r(s)\), \(r\le\tau+1\), imply a polynomial bound for this
whole split-support branch.  Any remaining super-polynomial obstruction must
come from too many canonical unmarked cores or from active supports with more
than \(\tau+1\) marked exits, not from repeated internal faces of one
split-support cube.
The core ledger also has a simple-pole form.  If \(S\) is active,
\(M=M(S)\), \(U=U(S)\), \(r=|M|\ge1\), and
\[
  a_y={b_y(S)\over\prod_{z\in M,\ z\ne y}(y-z)},
\]
then the full marked face over \(U\) is
\[
  H_{\tau+r,|U|}(s)\ell_U
  =\left(\sum_{y\in M}a_y y^i\right)_{0\le i\le\tau+r-1}.
\]
For every unmarked core root \(u\in U\), the mixed-face identity gives the
one-row longer simple-pole lift
\[
  H_{\tau+r+1,|U|-1}(s)\ell_{U\setminus\{u\}}
  =\left(\sum_{y\in M}{a_y y^i\over y-u}\right)_{0\le i\le\tau+r}.
\]
So a canonical core is a simultaneous family of compatible simple-pole
transforms of one marked sparse packet.  This is a sharper way to attack
\(\mathrm{Core}_r(s)\): after quotient-periodic and tangent cores are removed,
one must bound supports \(U\) for which all these simple-pole packet lifts can
hold.  The largest \(F_7^*\) audit checks \(21600\) full canonical-core
packets and \(4320\) simple-pole lifts, with \(19440\) productive packets and
\(2160\) productive lifts.
There is an immediate boundary-fiber sharpening.  If \(r=\tau+1\) and
\(U\ne\emptyset\), choose \(u\in U\).  The simple-pole lift has exactly
\(2r\) visible moments and nonzero amplitudes \(a_y/(y-u)\), so the Prony
annihilator recovers the marked locator uniquely.  Thus boundary matching
over a fixed canonical core can occur only for the empty core.  Writing
\(\mathrm{Core}_{\tau+1}^+(s)=\mathrm{Core}_{\tau+1}(s)\setminus\{\emptyset\}\)
and \(\epsilon_\emptyset=1\) if \(\emptyset\in\mathrm{Core}_{\tau+1}(s)\),
else \(0\), the closure criterion sharpens to
\[
|\mathcal C_m^{\le\tau+1}(s)|
\le
\sum_{r=m}^{\tau}\binom{r}{m}|\mathrm{Core}_r(s)|
+\binom{\tau+1}{m}
  \left(|\mathrm{Core}_{\tau+1}^+(s)|
  +\epsilon_\emptyset\left\lfloor {n\over\tau+1}\right\rfloor\right).
\]
So the boundary contributes only the empty-core full-marked endpoint, not a
matching factor over every canonical core.  The verifier now enforces this:
in the largest \(F_7^*\) audit there are \(21540\) marked-core fibers with
maximum fiber size \(2\), and no nonempty boundary core occurs; any future
nonempty boundary fiber is required to be singleton.
The same argument gives a general moment-complete uniqueness criterion.  Fix
a canonical core \(U=U(S)\), let \(r=|M(S)|\), and suppose
\[
  |U|\ge \max(0,r-\tau).
\]
The case \(r=0\) is tautological.  Otherwise choose \(E\subseteq U\) with
\(|E|=\max(0,r-\tau)\).  The mixed face with all marked exits gives the fixed
vector
\[
  H_{\tau+|E|+r,|U|-|E|}(s)\ell_{U\setminus E}
  =
  \left(\sum_{y\in M(S)} {a_y y^i\over \ell_E(y)}\right)_
    {0\le i\le\tau+|E|+r-1},
\]
where all amplitudes are nonzero, and the window length is at least \(2r\).
Thus the Prony annihilator recovers the marked locator uniquely.  Therefore
any nonunique fixed-core fiber with \(r\) marked exits must be moment-short:
\[
  |U|<r-\tau .
\]
In the largest \(F_7^*\) audit the verifier checks \(4320\)
moment-complete core fibers, all singleton; the productive subaudit checks
\(2160\), also all singleton.
The complementary moment-short regime is a packing, not an arbitrary fiber.
For fixed \(U\) and \(r\), put \(d=r-\tau-|U|\).  If \(d>0\), then two
distinct marked frontiers \(M_1,M_2\) over the same \(U\) satisfy
\[
  |M_1\cap M_2|<d .
\]
Indeed, after deleting all unmarked roots, both frontiers represent the same
length \(L=\tau+|U|+r\) sequence.  If \(|M_1\cap M_2|\ge d\), then
\(|M_1\cup M_2|\le 2r-d=L\), and the Vandermonde system forces the two sparse
representations to be identical.  Therefore each fixed-core fiber obeys
\[
  |\mathrm{fiber}(U,r)|
  \le
  \left\lfloor{\binom{n-|U|}{d}\over\binom{r}{d}}\right\rfloor .
\]
This recovers uniqueness for \(d\le0\) and boundary matching for \(d=1\).
The largest \(F_7^*\) audit checks \(17220\) deficit-packing fibers, maximum
deficit \(1\), maximum fiber size \(2\); the productive subaudit has the same
counts.
The packing is constructive.  If \(W\subseteq M(S)\) has size
\(d=r-\tau-|U|>0\), then deleting all unmarked roots gives a fixed length
\(L=\tau+|U|+r\) packet \(G_U\).  Applying \(\ell_W\) kills the modes in
\(W\) and leaves
\[
  (\ell_WG_U)_i
  =
  \sum_{y\in M(S)\setminus W}
    {a_y\ell_W(y)y^i\over \ell_U(y)},
  \qquad 0\le i<L-d .
\]
Here \(L-d=2(r-d)\), so the remaining locator
\(\prod_{y\in M(S)\setminus W}(X-y)\) is recovered by Prony uniqueness.
Thus \((U,W)\) determines the whole marked frontier; the map
\((S,W)\mapsto(U(S),W)\) is injective on fixed-\(r\) moment-short fibers.
The verifier now audits this deficit-anchor injection directly: in the largest
\(F_7^*\) audit it checks \(51840\) deficit-anchor labels, with at most \(6\)
labels in one fiber; the productive subaudit has the same counts.
Equivalently, a deficit anchor is a filtered residual Hankel-kernel problem.
With \(q=r-d=\tau+|U|\) and
\[
  h^W_i=(H_{2q,d}(s)\ell_W)_i,
  \qquad 0\le i<2q,
\]
the residual marked locator \(\ell_{M(S)\setminus W}\) is the unique monic
degree-\(q\) annihilator in \(H_{q,q}(h^W)\).  Thus the short-core ledger can
be attacked by counting anchors \((U,W)\) whose filtered sequence has an
admissible squarefree degree-\(\tau+|U|\) kernel locator.  The largest
\(F_7^*\) audit checks \(51840\) such filtered kernel recoveries, maximum
residual size \(2\), with the same productive counts.
If the affine monic kernel for a fixed anchor has direction dimension
\(b\), then its squarefree residual supports inside
\(D'=H\setminus(U\cup W)\) are bounded by
\[
  \sum_{i=0}^b \binom{|D'|}{i}.
\]
Indeed, each root condition \(P(x)=0\) is an affine hyperplane on the kernel
space, and a monic degree-\(q\) residual locator is determined by its \(q\)
roots, so a residual support is determined by at most \(b\) independent root
hyperplanes.  The verifier enumerates these residual fibers: in the largest
\(F_7^*\) audit it checks \(51840\) fibers carrying \(51840\) labels, maximum
fiber size \(1\), maximum direction dimension \(0\), with the same productive
counts.
The filtered residual kernel is also exactly a divisible short Hankel kernel:
if \(E=\ell_W\), \(h^W_i=\sum_a E_a s_{i+a}\), and \(\deg Q\le q\), then
\[
  H_{q,q}(h^W)Q=H_{q,q+d}(s)(EQ).
\]
Hence a positive-dimensional residual-kernel direction is a nonzero
lower-degree \(Q\) whose product \(EQ\) is killed by the same \(q\) Hankel
rows.  The verifier checks this identity for every squarefree residual
candidate in the enumeration, so the high-dimensional residual-anchor
obstruction is a fixed-divisor short-kernel ledger rather than an
unstructured completion multiplicity.
Residual collisions then charge to root slices.  If two same-anchor residual
supports \(R_1,R_2\) occur, then
\(Q=\ell_{R_1}-\ell_{R_2}\) is a nonzero element of the residual direction
space.  Any shared root \(x\in R_1\cap R_2\) divides \(Q\), so
\[
  H_{q,q+d-1}(s)(\ell_W(X-x)Q_x)=0,\qquad \deg Q_x<q-1.
\]
Thus shared-root collisions are fixed-root short-kernel directions.  More
generally, after charging bad \(c\)-root slices \(Z_c(U,W)\), the residual
fiber obeys
\[
|F(U,W)|\binom qc
\le
\binom Nc-|Z_c(U,W)|
  +|Z_c(U,W)|\binom{N-c}{q-c},
\quad N=|H\setminus(U\cup W)|.
\]
The verifier audits the one-root case: in the largest \(F_7^*\) audit it
checks \(51840\) anchors and \(259200\) one-root residual slices, with no bad
root-slice labels and the same productive counts.
The first positive-dimensional case is sharper than the general arrangement
bound.  If the residual-kernel direction space has dimension one, write the
affine kernel as \(P_0+\lambda Q\) with \(0\ne Q\in F[X]_{<q}\), and let
\(Z_Q=\{x\in D':Q(x)=0\}\), \(z=|Z_Q|\).  For two distinct residual supports
\(R_\lambda,R_\mu\), the difference
\(\ell_{R_\lambda}-\ell_{R_\mu}=(\lambda-\mu)Q\); hence all their common roots
lie in \(Z_Q\).  Counting incidences outside \(Z_Q\) gives
\[
  |F(U,W)|\le \left\lfloor {|D'|-z\over q-z}\right\rfloor ,
\]
and, for \(q>1\), \(Z_Q\) is exactly the one-root bad-slice set.  Thus a
one-dimensional residual kernel is already a root-slice packing, not a new
unstructured multiplicity source.  The verifier now extracts this unique
direction and asserts the bound whenever \(b(U,W)=1\); in the current largest
\(F_7^*\) audit no produced anchor has \(b(U,W)=1\).
More generally, let \(V(U,W)=\operatorname{Dir}K(U,W)\) have dimension
\(b\), \(1\le b<q\).  For a \(b\)-subset \(C\subset D'\), let
\(\operatorname{ev}_C:V(U,W)\to F^C\) be evaluation on \(C\), and call \(C\)
bad when \(\operatorname{ev}_C\) is not injective.  If two residual supports
share \(C\), then their locator difference is a nonzero element of
\(V(U,W)\) vanishing on \(C\), so \(C\) is bad.  Hence, writing \(Z_b^{\rm dir}\)
for the bad \(b\)-subsets and \(N=|D'|\),
\[
  |F(U,W)|\binom qb
  \le
  \binom Nb-|Z_b^{\rm dir}|
  +|Z_b^{\rm dir}|\binom{N-b}{q-b}.
\]
In the direction-MDS case \(Z_b^{\rm dir}=\emptyset\), this gives
\(|F(U,W)|\le\lfloor\binom Nb/\binom qb\rfloor\).  By the divisible-kernel
identity, the bad \(b\)-subsets are exactly fixed-divisor \(b\)-root
short-kernel slices.  The verifier now audits this rank-defect packing
criterion whenever \(0<b<q\); the present largest \(F_7^*\) audit has no
positive-dimensional residual anchors.
Moreover this bad-set term is exactly the projective root shadow of
\(V(U,W)\).  If \(Z_{D'}(Q)=\{x\in D':Q(x)=0\}\), then
\[
  Z_b^{\rm dir}(U,W)
  =
  \bigcup_{[Q]\in {\bf P}(V(U,W))}
  \{C\subset Z_{D'}(Q): |C|=b\}.
\]
Thus
\[
  |Z_b^{\rm dir}(U,W)|
  \le
  \sum_{[Q]\in{\bf P}(V(U,W))}
  \binom{|Z_{D'}(Q)|}{b},
\]
and direction-MDS follows whenever every nonzero residual direction has fewer
than \(b\) available roots.  Via the divisible-kernel identity, this rewrites
the bad-subset ledger as a projective fixed-divisor root-count ledger.
The verifier checks this projective-shadow identity together with the
direction-MDS packing audit.
Equivalently, the same bad \(b\)-subsets are exactly absorbed multi-root
rank defects.  For \(C\subset D'\), \(|C|=b\), put
\[
  h^{W,C}_i=\sum_a(\ell_W\ell_C)_a s_{i+a},
  \qquad
  A_C(U,W)=(h^{W,C}_{i+j})_{0\le i<q,\ 0\le j<q-b}.
\]
Then
\[
  C\in Z_b^{\rm dir}(U,W)
  \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
  \operatorname{rank} A_C(U,W)<q-b .
\]
The proof is just divisibility in the direction polynomial:
a nonzero \(Q\in V(U,W)\) vanishes on \(C\) iff
\(Q=\ell_C Q_C\), \(\deg Q_C<q-b\), and the divisible-kernel identity turns
this into \(H_{q,q+d-b}(s)(\ell_W\ell_C Q_C)=0\).  Thus the abstract
rank-defect ledger, the projective root-shadow ledger, and the absorbed
fixed-divisor Hankel ledger are the same object; the verifier now checks all
three descriptions together.
This also gives a universal bounded-dimension closure.  Since each nonzero
direction has degree \(<q\), it has at most \(q-1\) roots, and
\(|{\bf P}(V(U,W))|=(|F|^b-1)/(|F|-1)\).  Hence
\[
  |Z_b^{\rm dir}(U,W)|
  \le
  { |F|^b-1\over |F|-1}
  \binom{\min(N,q-1)}{b}.
\]
Combining this with the direction-packing inequality gives, with
\[
  Z_b^{\max}
  =
  { |F|^b-1\over |F|-1}
  \binom{\min(N,q-1)}{b},
\]
\[
  |F(U,W)|\binom qb
  \le
  \binom Nb + Z_b^{\max}\binom{N-b}{q-b}.
\]
Thus bounded residual direction dimension is polynomially controlled in the
polynomial-field window; sharper M1 work must either prove smaller root-shadow
ledgers for the actual fixed-divisor kernels or classify the unbounded/persistent
direction families.  The verifier now checks this projective root-count bound
whenever the direction-MDS bad-set audit is triggered.
There is also a sharper one-root envelope.  Let \(Z_1(U,W)\) be the bad
one-root slice set and put \(z=|Z_1(U,W)|\).  If
\(C\in Z_b^{\rm dir}(U,W)\), then some nonzero direction is divisible by
\(\ell_C\), hence by \(X-x\) for every \(x\in C\).  Therefore every
\(x\in C\) lies in \(Z_1(U,W)\), and
\[
  Z_b^{\rm dir}(U,W)
  \subset \{C\subset Z_1(U,W): |C|=b\},
  \qquad
  |Z_b^{\rm dir}(U,W)|\le \binom zb .
\]
Thus
\[
  |F(U,W)|\binom qb
  \le
  \binom Nb+\binom zb\binom{N-b}{q-b}.
\]
If \(z<b\), the direction-MDS bound applies.  Higher-dimensional bad subsets
are therefore charged to the one-root fixed-root ledger rather than to a new
independent obstruction.  The verifier now checks this envelope whenever the
direction-MDS bad-set audit is triggered.
Combining this with the finite/persistent dichotomy for the absorbed pencil
closes the finite branch.  If
\[
  A_x(U,W)=B(U,W)-xC(U,W)
\]
is not persistently rank-defective, some maximal minor is a nonzero polynomial
of degree at most \(q-1\), so \(z=|Z_1(U,W)|\le q-1\).  Hence
\[
  |Z_b^{\rm dir}(U,W)|\le \binom{q-1}{b}
\]
and
\[
  |F(U,W)|\binom qb
  \le
  \binom Nb+\binom{q-1}{b}\binom{N-b}{q-b}.
\]
Thus the nonpersistent one-root branch closes the whole higher bad-subset
hierarchy with a field-size-free bound; only the persistent one-root absorbed
pencil remains, and that branch is the moving-kernel endpoint obstruction
described below.  The verifier checks this finite-branch implication together
with the direction-MDS audit.
Moreover, when \(|F|>q-1\), full-field rank defect of the one-root pencil is
genuine persistence: all maximal minors have degree at most \(q-1\).  For a
produced anchor, such persistence forces endpoint defect and in fact
\[
  b(U,W)\ge 2 .
\]
The endpoint defect follows from the moving-kernel coefficient ladder.  If
\(b(U,W)=1\), the unique residual direction \(Q\) has degree \(<q\), so its
available root set has size at most \(q-1\); but persistence makes every
available root one-root-bad, while the produced residual support gives at least
\(q\) available roots.  Thus the remaining persistent obstruction is genuinely
higher-dimensional, not a line-kernel case.  The verifier checks this whenever
full-field probing certifies persistence.
Conversely, if \(b(U,W)\ge2\), then every available root is one-root-bad:
evaluation \(Q\mapsto Q(x)\) maps a space of dimension at least two to a line,
so it has a nonzero kernel.  Hence \(Z_1(U,W)=D'\).  For a produced anchor
\(|D'|\ge q\), so the finite branch \(|Z_1|\le q-1\) is impossible.  Thus, in
the usual \(|F|>q-1\) regime, the one-root absorbed pencil is persistent
exactly when \(b(U,W)\ge2\).  The verifier checks this equivalence whenever a
positive-dimensional produced residual anchor appears.
The first surviving case, \(b(U,W)=2\), is a projective-fiber ledger.  For a
basis \(V(U,W)=\langle P,Q\rangle\), define \(B_0=\{x:P(x)=Q(x)=0\}\) and,
off \(B_0\), the projective evaluation map
\[
  x\longmapsto [P(x):Q(x)]\in{\bf P}^1 .
\]
A pair \(\{x,y\}\) is direction-bad exactly when one point lies in \(B_0\), or
both points are outside \(B_0\) and have the same projective value.  Each
non-base fiber is cut out by \(bP-aQ\) for some \([a:b]\in{\bf P}^1\), so it
has size at most \(q-1\), and \(B_0\) also has size at most \(q-1\).  Hence the
\(b=2\) persistent branch is a rational-fiber packing problem, not an arbitrary
rank-defect matroid; the verifier checks this projective-fiber description
whenever \(b(U,W)=2\).
Writing \(s=|B_0|\), \(m_\lambda=|\{x\notin B_0:[P(x):Q(x)]=\lambda\}|\), and
\(N=|D'|\), this gives the exact count
\[
  |Z_2^{\rm dir}(U,W)|
  =
  \binom N2-\binom{N-s}{2}
  +\sum_{\lambda\in{\bf P}^1}\binom{m_\lambda}{2}.
\]
If all non-base fibers have size at most \(M\), then
\[
  |Z_2^{\rm dir}(U,W)|
  \le
  \binom N2-\binom{N-s}{2}
  +\left\lfloor{(M-1)(N-s)\over 2}\right\rfloor .
\]
Thus base-free injectivity gives direction-MDS, and bounded projective fibers
give a linear bad-pair ledger outside the base locus.  The verifier checks the
exact count and this envelope whenever \(b(U,W)=2\).
Equivalently, the good cross-fiber pairs are the pairs
\(\{x,y\}\subset D'\setminus B_0\) with distinct projective values.  Their
total number is
\[
  G_{\rm tot}
  =
  \binom{N-s}{2}
  -\sum_{\lambda\in{\bf P}^1}\binom{m_\lambda}{2}.
\]
No such good pair can occur in two residual supports.  Hence, if every residual
support contains at least \(g_{\min}>0\) good cross-fiber pairs, then
\[
  |F(U,W)|\le \left\lfloor {G_{\rm tot}\over g_{\min}}\right\rfloor .
\]
The verifier checks this good-pair packing bound whenever \(b(U,W)=2\).
For an individual residual support \(R\), with
\[
  a(R)=|R\cap B_0|,\qquad
  r_\lambda(R)=|R\cap ev^{-1}(\lambda)|,
\]
one has
\[
  g(R)=\binom{q-a(R)}2-\sum_\lambda\binom{r_\lambda(R)}2 .
\]
Thus if \(a(R)\le A\) and every projective fiber contributes at most \(M\)
roots to \(R\), then with \(L=q-A\),
\[
  g(R)\ge
  \binom L2
  -\left(
    \left\lfloor {L\over M}\right\rfloor\binom M2
    +\binom{L\bmod M}{2}
  \right).
\]
So failure to obtain many good pairs is exactly concentration in the base locus
or in a few projective fibers; the verifier checks this support-level
occupancy formula.
These concentration alternatives are fixed-divisor slices, not new residual
multiplicity.  Roots in \(B_0\) are common zeros of every direction in
\(\langle P,Q\rangle\).  For a non-base projective fiber \(\lambda=[a:b]\),
the nonzero direction \(Q_\lambda=bP-aQ\) vanishes on that fiber.  Thus any
heavy subset \(C\) in \(B_0\) or in one projective fiber gives a divisor
\(\ell_C\) of the relevant residual direction and hence a DKI slice
\[
  H_{q,q+d-|C|}(s)(\ell_W\ell_C Q_C)=0 .
\]
The remaining \(b=2\) M1 task is therefore to bound these fixed-root and
projective-fiber slice ledgers.
This gives a root-shadow-height closure.  Put
\[
  h=\max(|B_0|,\max_\lambda |ev^{-1}(\lambda)|).
\]
When a residual support exists, \(h\ge1\).  Every residual support has
\(a(R)\le h\) and \(r_\lambda(R)\le h\), hence with \(L=q-h\)
\[
  g(R)\ge
  \gamma_2(q,h):=
  \binom L2-
  \left(\left\lfloor {L\over h}\right\rfloor\binom h2
  +\binom{L\bmod h}{2}\right).
\]
If \(\gamma_2(q,h)>0\), then
\[
  |F(U,W)|\le \left\lfloor {G_{\rm tot}\over \gamma_2(q,h)}\right\rfloor .
\]
In particular the unclosed \(b=2\) branch requires a fixed-divisor slice of
height at least \(q/2\); small root shadows are packing-bounded.
If such a half-height slice \(S\) exists, it also produces a short quotient
kernel.  For \(S=B_0\), every direction in \(\langle P,Q\rangle\) is divisible
by \(\ell_S\); for a projective fiber \(S=ev^{-1}([a:b])\), the direction
\(Q_S=bP-aQ\) is divisible by \(\ell_S\).  Hence
\[
  Q_S=\ell_S R_S,\qquad \deg R_S<q-|S|\le q/2,
\]
and the DKI identity gives
\[
  H_{q,q+d-|S|}(s)(\ell_W\ell_S R_S)=0 .
\]
Thus the surviving \(b=2\) obstruction is a half-width quotient Hankel kernel,
not diffuse two-dimensional residual multiplicity.
In the base-locus case this is stronger: if \(h=|B_0|\ge q/2\), then the
whole pencil descends,
\[
  \langle P,Q\rangle=\ell_{B_0}V',
  \qquad \dim V'=2,\qquad V'\subset F[X]_{<q-h}.
\]
For \(x\notin B_0\), \(\ell_{B_0}(x)\ne0\), so
\([P(x):Q(x)]=[P'(x):Q'(x)]\).  Thus a large base locus lowers the entire
projective-fiber packing problem to degree \(<q-h\le q/2\); only large
non-base fibers remain as single short quotient directions.
The same dichotomy is support-local.  If a residual support \(R\) has
\(g(R)=0\), then all non-base roots of \(R\) lie in one projective fiber.  Thus
either \(|R\cap B_0|\ge q/2\) or \(|R\cap ev^{-1}(\lambda)|\ge q/2\) for that
fiber \(\lambda\).  Consequently every support not charged by the good-pair
packing sum carries its own half-width quotient certificate
\[
  H_{q,q+d-|C|}(s)(\ell_W\ell_C R_C)=0,\qquad |C|\ge q/2 .
\]
Writing \(s=|B_0|\) and \(m_\lambda=|ev^{-1}(\lambda)|\), the zero-good
envelope is
\[
  Z_0^{env}
  =
  {\bf 1}_{s\ge q}\binom sq
  +\sum_\lambda\sum_{r=1}^q
    \binom{m_\lambda}{r}\binom{s}{q-r}.
\]
Positive-good supports inject into the \(G_{\rm tot}\) good pairs, while
zero-good supports lie in this envelope, so
\[
  |F(U,W)|\le G_{\rm tot}+Z_0^{env}.
\]
There is also a half-certificate incidence form.  With
\[
  c=\lceil q/2\rceil,\qquad
  H_c=\binom sc+\sum_\lambda\binom{m_\lambda}{c},
\]
every zero-good support contains a \(c\)-subset either in \(B_0\) or in one
projective fiber.  Therefore
\[
  Z_0^{env}\le H_c\binom{N-c}{q-c},
  \qquad
  |F(U,W)|\le G_{\rm tot}+H_c\binom{N-c}{q-c},
\]
where \(N=|H\setminus(U\cup W)|\).  This rewrites the remaining zero-good
branch as a half-width fixed-divisor certificate count times residual
completions.
The completion factor can be localized.  Put
\[
  B_c=\binom{s-c}{q-c}
  +\sum_\lambda\sum_{r=1}^{q-c}
    \binom{m_\lambda}{r}\binom{s-c}{q-c-r},
\]
and
\[
  F_{c,\lambda}=\sum_{r=0}^{q-c}
    \binom{m_\lambda-c}{r}\binom{s}{q-c-r}.
\]
A base half-certificate completes only inside the base locus plus one
projective fiber, and a fiber half-certificate completes only inside that
fiber plus the base locus.  Hence
\[
  Z_0^{env}
  \le
  \binom{s}{c}B_c+\sum_\lambda\binom{m_\lambda}{c}F_{c,\lambda},
\]
and the same expression may replace \(Z_0^{env}\) in the fixed-anchor closure.
Thus the zero-good branch carries no artificial all-domain completion factor.
In fact the genuine \(b=2\) pencil has a degree gap.  For every projective
fiber \(\lambda=[a:b]\), the set \(B_0\cup ev^{-1}(\lambda)\) is contained in
the zero set of the nonzero direction \(bP-aQ\), which has degree \(<q\).
Also \(B_0\) is contained in the zero set of a nonzero degree-\(<q\) direction.
Hence
\[
  |B_0|<q,\qquad |B_0\cup ev^{-1}(\lambda)|<q
  \quad\hbox{for all }\lambda,
\]
so no zero-good \(q\)-support exists:
\[
  Z_0^{env}=0,\qquad |F(U,W)|\le G_{\rm tot}.
\]
Thus the \(b=2\) zero-good branch closes by degree, leaving only the global
good-pair ledger to charge in the M1 packing argument.
This closure is constructive.  Fix an origin locator
\[
  L_0\in K(U,W),\qquad K(U,W)=L_0+\langle P,Q\rangle .
\]
For a good pair \(\{x,y\}\), the matrix
\[
  E_{x,y}=\begin{pmatrix}P(x)&Q(x)\\ P(y)&Q(y)\end{pmatrix}
\]
is invertible, so the unique affine-kernel locator through that pair is
\[
  L_{x,y}=L_0+\alpha P+\beta Q,\qquad
  E_{x,y}(\alpha,\beta)^T=-(L_0(x),L_0(y))^T .
\]
Every \(b=2\) residual support contains a good pair, and any such pair
reconstructs its monic locator by this formula.  Thus the fixed-anchor fiber is
an explicit image of the good-pair interpolation map.
More precisely, if \(L_{x,y}\) is the interpolated locator for a good pair, then
\[
  F(U,W)=
  \{\operatorname{roots}_{D'}(L_{x,y}):
      \{x,y\}\hbox{ good},\
      |\operatorname{roots}_{D'}(L_{x,y})|=q,\
      \ell_{\operatorname{roots}_{D'}(L_{x,y})}=L_{x,y}\}.
\]
So the \(b=2\) fixed-anchor fiber is exactly a split-locator image of
two-root interpolation.
Moreover the fiber over a support \(R\) is exactly the set of good pairs inside
\(R\).  If \(I\) is the set of good pairs whose interpolated locator splits,
then
\[
  |I|=\sum_{R\in F(U,W)}g(R),
  \qquad
  |F(U,W)|=\sum_{e\in I}{1\over g(\operatorname{roots}_{D'}(L_e))}.
\]
This is the exact weighted form of the good-pair ledger.
The split test also descends by two roots.  Since \(L_{x,y}\) vanishes at the
good pair, write
\[
  L_{x,y}=\ell_{\{x,y\}}M_{x,y},\qquad \deg M_{x,y}=q-2 .
\]
Then
\[
  H_{q,q+d-2}(s)(\ell_W\ell_{\{x,y\}}M_{x,y})=0,
\]
and \(L_{x,y}\) splits over \(D'\) iff \(M_{x,y}\) splits over
\(D'\setminus\{x,y\}\).  Thus the endpoint split test is a two-root absorbed
quotient-locator problem of width \(q-2\).
Equivalently, for \(z\notin\{x,y\}\), the quotient-root condition is the
three-row determinant gate
\[
  \det\begin{pmatrix}
    L_0(x)&P(x)&Q(x)\\
    L_0(y)&P(y)&Q(y)\\
    L_0(z)&P(z)&Q(z)
  \end{pmatrix}=0.
\]
So after a good pair is fixed, the possible remaining roots are cut out by an
explicit one-variable determinant condition.
This determinant is just a normalization of the interpolated locator.  If
\[
  D_{x,y}=\det\begin{pmatrix}P(x)&Q(x)\\P(y)&Q(y)\end{pmatrix}\ne0,
\]
then
\[
  \Delta_{x,y}=D_{x,y}L_{x,y},\qquad
  \Delta_{x,y}/\ell_{\{x,y\}}=D_{x,y}M_{x,y}.
\]
Thus the determinant gate and the two-root quotient have the same roots away
from the forced good pair.
Summarizing, a \(b=2\) fixed anchor has no zero-good residual supports; every
support is obtained from a good pair by interpolation, and the residual fiber
is the weighted split image
\[
  |F(U,W)|=\sum_{e\in I}{1\over g(\operatorname{roots}_{D'}(L_e))}.
\]
Each image point is equivalently a two-root absorbed quotient-locator or the
same determinant gate normalized by the nonzero good-pair determinant.  The
remaining \(b=2\) task is therefore the global charging of these good-pair
determinant gates, not a local residual \(q\)-subset enumeration.
For any family \(\mathcal A_2\) of \(b=2\) anchors this gives the exact global
ledger
\[
  \sum_{A\in\mathcal A_2}|F(A)|
  =
  \sum_{A\in\mathcal A_2}\sum_{e\in I_A}
    {1\over g_A(\pi_A(e))},
\]
where \(I_A\) is the set of good pairs whose normalized determinant gate
splits.  Closing the local \(b=2\) branch is therefore equivalent to charging
this weighted split determinant-gate family across anchors.
The reciprocal weights in this ledger have a sharp concentration filter.  For
a fixed anchor \(A\) and split support \(R\), put
\[
  a_A(R)=|R\cap B_0|,\qquad L_A(R)=q-a_A(R),
\]
and let \(m_A(R)\) be the largest projective-fiber occupancy among the
non-base roots of \(R\).  Define the projective escape
\[
  e_A(R)=L_A(R)-m_A(R).
\]
Since good pairs are exactly pairs of non-base roots lying in distinct
projective fibers,
\[
  g_A(R)
  ={1\over2}\left(L_A(R)^2-\sum_\lambda r_\lambda(R)^2\right)
  =\sum_{\lambda<\mu}r_\lambda(R)r_\mu(R).
\]
In particular, using
\(\sum_\lambda r_\lambda(R)^2\le m_A(R)L_A(R)\),
\[
  g_A(R)\ge {L_A(R)e_A(R)\over2},
  \qquad
  e_A(R)\le {2g_A(R)\over L_A(R)}.              \tag{DGW}
\]
Thus a determinant-gate support with a dangerous reciprocal weight is forced
to be almost contained in the base locus together with one projective fiber.
More quantitatively, after choosing a base-heavy cutoff \(A_0<q\) and an
escape cutoff \(c\ge1\), every support with
\[
  a_A(R)\le A_0,\qquad e_A(R)\ge c
\]
has
\[
  {1\over g_A(R)}\le {2\over c(q-A_0)}.
\]
The complementary supports are exactly the base-heavy supports
\(a_A(R)>A_0\) and the near-fiber supports \(e_A(R)<c\).  Hence the global
weighted determinant-gate ledger splits into a generic part with an explicit
reciprocal saving and two structural parts already shaped like
fixed-divisor/projective-fiber residue-line charges.
The near-fiber part is in fact a bounded-complement quotient problem.  If
\(a_A(R)\le A_0\) and \(e_A(R)<c\), then for a largest projective fiber
\(\lambda\)
\[
  |R\cap ev_A^{-1}(\lambda)|
  =L_A(R)-e_A(R)
  \ge q-A_0-c+1 .
\]
The nonzero direction \(Q_\lambda=bP-aQ\) vanishes on this slice, so
\[
  Q_\lambda=\ell_{R\cap ev_A^{-1}(\lambda)}\,Q'_\lambda,
  \qquad \deg Q'_\lambda< A_0+c-1,
\]
and
\[
  H_{q,q+d-|C|}(s)(\ell_W\ell_C Q'_\lambda)=0,
  \qquad C=R\cap ev_A^{-1}(\lambda).
\]
Thus every non-base-heavy support escaping the generic weight saving carries a
short quotient certificate of width at most \(A_0+c-1\).  The base-heavy
alternative similarly supplies fixed-divisor slices inside the common base
locus \(B_0\), where every residual direction vanishes.  Hence the weighted
determinant-gate obstruction has been reduced to a generic saved term plus
explicit fixed-divisor or bounded-complement quotient ledgers.
This gives a precise cutoff reduction.  For any family \(\mathcal A_2\) of
\(b=2\) anchors and cutoffs \(A_0<q\), \(c\ge1\), partition each residual
support \(R\in F(A)\) into
\[
\begin{array}{ll}
\mathcal G_A: & a_A(R)\le A_0,\ e_A(R)\ge c,\\
\mathcal B_A: & a_A(R)>A_0,\\
\mathcal N_A: & a_A(R)\le A_0,\ e_A(R)<c.
\end{array}
\]
Let \(I_A^{\mathcal G}\) be the split good pairs whose image support lies in
\(\mathcal G_A\).  Then
\[
  \sum_{A\in\mathcal A_2}|F(A)|
  \le
  {2\over c(q-A_0)}\sum_{A\in\mathcal A_2}|I_A^{\mathcal G}|
  +\sum_{A\in\mathcal A_2}|\mathcal B_A|
  +\sum_{A\in\mathcal A_2}|\mathcal N_A|.
\]
Moreover \(\mathcal B_A\) is a common-base fixed-divisor ledger, and
\(\mathcal N_A\) is a projective-fiber quotient ledger of width at most
\(A_0+c-1\).  Thus, once the unweighted generic determinant-gate count and
these two explicit quotient ledgers are polynomial after the existing reserve
charges, the \(b=2\) branch closes.
The common-base ledger has an exact fixed-subset incidence descent.  Let
\[
  B_0^*(A)=B_0\cap \operatorname{roots}_{D'}(L_0).
\]
If \(R\in F(A)\) and \(x\in R\cap B_0\), then \(x\in B_0^*(A)\), because the
directions vanish at \(x\) and \(L_R=L_0+\alpha P+\beta Q\).  For
\(C\subset B_0^*(A)\), \(|C|=d\), put
\[
  F_C(A)=\{R'\subset D'\setminus C:\ |R'|=q-d,
          \ell_C\ell_{R'}\in K(A)\}.
\]
Multiplication by \(\ell_C\) identifies \(F_C(A)\) with the residual supports
containing \(C\).  Hence, for \(d=A_0+1\),
\[
  \sum_{C\subset B_0^*(A),\, |C|=d}|F_C(A)|
  =
  \sum_{R\in F(A)}\binom{a_A(R)}{d},
  \qquad
  |\mathcal B_A|\le
  \sum_{C\subset B_0^*(A),\, |C|=A_0+1}|F_C(A)|.
\]
Thus the base-heavy exceptional term is an incidence sum of lower-width
fixed-divisor quotient fibers.
The near-fiber term has the analogous projective-fiber incidence form.  Assume
\(1\le c\le q-A_0\) and set \(h=q-A_0-c+1\).  For a projective fiber
\(\lambda=[a:b]\), let \(Q_\lambda=bP-aQ\).  Every
\(R\in\mathcal N_A\) contains an \(h\)-subset \(C\) in one non-base
projective fiber.  For such \(C\subset ev_A^{-1}(\lambda)\), put
\[
  F_{\lambda,C}(A)=
  \{R'\subset D'\setminus C:\ |R'|=q-h,
        \ell_C\ell_{R'}\in K(A)\}.
\]
If this fiber is nonempty and \(L_C\in K(A)\) is divisible by \(\ell_C\),
then the locators in \(K(A)\) divisible by \(\ell_C\) form
\[
  L_C+\langle Q_\lambda\rangle,
\]
because the direction space has rank one on a non-base projective fiber.  Thus
after division by \(\ell_C\), the near-fiber term is a quotient-line ledger of
width \(q-h=A_0+c-1\), and
\[
  |\mathcal N_A|\le
  \sum_\lambda
  \sum_{\substack{C\subset ev_A^{-1}(\lambda)\\ |C|=h}}
    |F_{\lambda,C}(A)|.
\]
In particular, taking \(A_0=0\), \(c=w+1\), and \(h=q-w\) gives the
constant-width form
\[
  \sum_{A\in\mathcal A_2}|F(A)|
  \le
  {2\over (w+1)q}\sum_{A\in\mathcal A_2}|I_A^{\mathrm{gen},w}|
  +\sum_{A\in\mathcal A_2}\sum_{x\in B_0^*(A)}|F_x(A)|
  +\sum_{A\in\mathcal A_2}\sum_\lambda
    \sum_{\substack{C\subset ev_A^{-1}(\lambda)\\ |C|=q-w}}
      |F_{\lambda,C}(A)|.
\]
Thus, for fixed \(w\), the \(b=2\) branch is reduced to a generic
determinant-gate count with a \(2/((w+1)q)\) saving, one-root common-base
quotient fibers, and width-\(w\) projective-fiber quotient-line fibers.
At \(w=0\), the projective-fiber quotient-line term is empty by the degree
gap: it would require \(q\) roots in one non-base projective fiber, but every
such fiber is the zero set of a nonzero degree-\(<q\) residual direction.
In fact the no-base reciprocal weight is sharper than the generic
\(2/q\) cutoff.  A no-base support cannot lie in one projective fiber, so if
the largest fiber inside the support has size \(m\), the cross-fiber good
pairs from that fiber to its complement already give
\[
  g_A(R)\ge m(q-m)\ge q-1 .
\]
Equivalently, if \(j=q-m\) is the escape from the largest projective fiber,
then the profile has reciprocal weight at most \(1/(j(q-j))\).  For an escape
cutoff \(u\), the profiles \(j<u\) descend to width-\(j\) projective-fiber
quotient lines by dividing the direction \(bP-aQ\) by the \(q-j\) roots in the
dominant fiber; the profiles \(j\ge u\) remain in the weighted determinant-gate
ledger with this profile weight.
The spread profiles have a sharper exact occupancy saving.  If
\(q=a_m m+r_m\), \(0\le r_m<m\), then every no-base support whose projective
fibers have maximum size \(m\) has
\[
  g_A(R)\ge
  \Phi_q(m):={1\over2}\bigl(q^2-a_m m^2-r_m^2\bigr),
\]
since the fiber-square sum is maximized by as many size-\(m\) fibers as
possible and one remainder fiber.  Thus the profile weight may be replaced by
\(1/\Phi_q(q-j)\), with equality against \(1/(j(q-j))\) only in the
concentrated range \(q-j\ge q/2\).
Equivalently, for any cutoff \(0\le w\le q-2\), after moving the profiles
\(j\le w\) to width-\(j\) projective-fiber quotient-line ledgers, every
remaining no-base support has maximum projective-fiber occupancy at most
\(q-w-1\), and therefore pays the uniform determinant-gate coefficient
\[
  {1\over \Phi_q(q-w-1)} .
\]
The case \(w=0\) gives the sharp \(1/(q-1)\) width-zero coefficient; larger
\(w\) trades bounded-width quotient-line charges for a stronger saved
determinant-gate term.
Thus
\[
  \sum_{A\in\mathcal A_2}|F(A)|
  \le
  {1\over q-1}\sum_{A\in\mathcal A_2}|I_A^{\mathrm{nb}}|
  +\sum_{A\in\mathcal A_2}\sum_{x\in B_0^*(A)}|F_x(A)|,
\]
where \(I_A^{\mathrm{nb}}\) is the split-good-pair domain whose image support
avoids \(B_0\).  Hence the width-zero endpoint is a no-base determinant-gate
term with the sharp \(1/(q-1)\) saving plus the one-root common-base quotient
ledger.
The common-base term can be peeled.  For
\(S\subset B_0^*(A)\), write
\[
  L_0=\ell_S L_{0,S},\qquad P=\ell_S P_S,\qquad Q=\ell_S Q_S,
  \qquad q_S=q-|S|.
\]
Let \(F_S(A)\) be the quotient fiber of supports \(R'\subset D'\setminus S\)
with \(|R'|=q_S\) and \(\ell_S\ell_{R'}\in K(A)\).  If
\(\dim\langle P_S,Q_S\rangle=2\), then the width-zero ledger applies again:
\[
  |F_S(A)|
  \le {1\over q_S-1}|I_S^{\mathrm{nb}}|
     +\sum_{x\in B_0^*(A)\setminus S}|F_{S\cup\{x\}}(A)|.
\]
If the quotient direction dimension is \(<2\), the branch has dropped to the
lower-dimensional residual ledgers.  Therefore the one-root common-base term
unfolds into a finite base-peeling tree with no-base determinant-gate payments
at each \(b=2\) node.
The peeling can be made canonical to avoid multiplicity.  Fix an order on
\(B_0^*(A)\) and always peel the least remaining base root.  Each residual
support then follows a unique chain of base subsets.  If \(F_S^{can}(A)\) is
the canonical quotient fiber at a peeled subset \(S\), then at a \(b=2\) node
\[
  |F_S^{can}(A)|
  \le
  L_S^{can}
  +{1\over q-|S|-1}|I_S^{\mathrm{nb},can}|
  +\sum_{x>\max S}|F_{S\cup\{x\}}^{can}(A)|,
\]
where \(L_S^{can}\) is the lower-dimensional terminal contribution, and for
\(S=\emptyset\) the last sum ranges over all base roots.  Iterating gives a
multiplicity-free tree bound: the remaining \(b=2\) task is no-base
determinant-gate control at canonical peeled anchors, plus lower-dimensional
terminal ledgers.
The occupancy cutoff can be applied at each internal \(b=2\) node.  If
\(q_S=q-|S|\) and \(0\le w_S\le q_S-2\), then the spread no-base labels at
node \(S\) pay the coefficient \(1/\Phi_{q_S}(q_S-w_S-1)\), while the
concentrated profiles \(j\le w_S\) become width-\(j\) projective-fiber
quotient-line ledgers.  Iterating over the disjoint canonical tree gives an
endpoint consisting only of lower-dimensional leaves, spread determinant-gate
ledgers with these occupancy savings, and bounded-width quotient-line ledgers
for concentrated profiles.
Each bounded-width quotient-line ledger is itself a one-dimensional
root-slice packing.  For a quotient line of width \(j\) with direction
\(Q'\ne0\), let \(Z=\{x:Q'(x)=0\}\) on the remaining canonical domain,
\(z=|Z|\), and \(N\) be the domain size.  Distinct quotient supports can
intersect only inside \(Z\), so
\[
  |F_{\lambda,C}^{can}|\le
  \left\lfloor {N-z\over j-z}\right\rfloor .
\]
Thus concentrated no-base profiles descend to fixed-width root-slice packing
ledgers; the remaining new \(b=2\) term is the spread determinant-gate count.
That spread term is bounded by ambient good-pair capacity at the same
canonical node.  If \(D_S^{can}\) is the remaining domain, \(B_S\) the
descended base locus, and \(D_{S,\lambda}\) the non-base projective fibers,
then
\[
  G_S^{can}=
  \binom{|D_S^{can}\setminus B_S|}{2}
  -\sum_\lambda \binom{|D_{S,\lambda}|}{2}
\]
counts all ambient cross-fiber good pairs, and
\(|I_S^{\mathrm{nb},spread(w_S),can}|\le G_S^{can}\).  Therefore the canonical
\(b=2\) endpoint can be written using only lower-dimensional leaves, the
explicit node capacities \(G_S^{can}/\Phi_{q_S}(q_S-w_S-1)\), and the
fixed-width root-slice packing floors for the concentrated quotient lines.
Since the cutoff is free at each node, define
\[
  R_{S,j}^{can}=\sum_{\lambda,C}
    \left\lfloor {N_{S,C}-z_{S,\lambda,C}\over j-z_{S,\lambda,C}}\right\rfloor
\]
over width-\(j\) quotient-line slices, and set
\[
  Cap_S^*=\min_{0\le w\le q_S-2}
  \left(
    {G_S^{can}\over \Phi_{q_S}(q_S-w-1)}
    +\sum_{j=1}^w R_{S,j}^{can}
  \right).
\]
The optimized canonical endpoint is then
\[
  |F(A)|\le \sum_{S\in Tree(A)}L_S^{can}
  +\sum_{S\in Tree_2(A)} Cap_S^* .
\]
There is also a height-only corollary.  Since a width-\(j\) quotient direction
has fewer than \(j\) roots, each quotient-line packing floor is at most
\(N_S-q_S+1\), independent of its bad-root count.  Hence, with
\[
  H_{S,j}^{can}=\sum_\lambda \binom{|D_{S,\lambda}|}{q_S-j},
\]
one has
\[
  R_{S,j}^{can}\le (N_S-q_S+1)H_{S,j}^{can}.
\]
Replacing \(R_{S,j}^{can}\) by this upper bound gives a bad-root-free
capacity depending only on node sizes and projective-fiber heights.
In particular, if \(h_S=\max_\lambda |D_{S,\lambda}|\), then choosing
\(w_S=q_S-h_S-1\) makes all concentrated terms vanish:
\(H_{S,j}^{can}=0\) for \(j\le w_S\).  Thus
\[
  WidehatCap_S^*\le {G_S^{can}\over \Phi_{q_S}(h_S)}
\]
when \(h_S>0\), and the canonical fixed-anchor bound has the compact
max-height form
\[
  |F(A)|\le \sum_{S\in Tree(A)}L_S^{can}
   +\sum_{\substack{S\in Tree_2(A)\\ h_S>0}}
    {G_S^{can}\over \Phi_{q_S}(h_S)} .
\]
The denominator has a two-regime lower bound: \(\Phi_q(h)\ge q(q-h)/2\), so
\(\Phi_q(h)\ge q^2/4\) for \(h\le q/2\), while for \(h>q/2\) the exact value
is \(\Phi_q(h)=h(q-h)\).  Hence diffuse-height canonical nodes pay at most
\(4G_S^{can}/q_S^2\), and the only nodes outside this automatic quadratic
saving are the half-height projective-shadow nodes.
These exceptional nodes are short quotient-kernel certificates.  If
\(\lambda=[a:b]\) is a fiber with
\(|D_{S,\lambda}|=h_S>q_S/2\), then
\[
  Q_{S,\lambda}=bP_S-aQ_S
  =\ell_{D_{S,\lambda}}R_{S,\lambda},\qquad
  \deg R_{S,\lambda}<q_S-h_S<q_S/2 .
\]
By the divisible-kernel identity, \(R_{S,\lambda}\) is the fixed-divisor
Hankel kernel for the absorbed canonical anchor
\(S\cup D_{S,\lambda}\).  Thus the non-diffuse term
\(G_S^{can}/(h_S(q_S-h_S))\) is attached to a canonical short
projective-shadow quotient direction of width \(q_S-h_S\); the remaining
global task is to count or charge these short directions across the
base-peeling tree.
Equivalently, choose at every canonical \(b=2\) node the half-width cutoff
\[
  w_S^{sh}=\lceil q_S/2\rceil-1,\qquad
  q_S-w_S^{sh}-1=\lfloor q_S/2\rfloor .
\]
Then the spread term pays at most \(4G_S^{can}/q_S^2\), and every concentrated
profile has width \(j<q_S/2\).  With
\[
  Short_S^{can}=\sum_{1\le j<q_S/2}R_{S,j}^{can},
\]
the canonical endpoint becomes
\[
  |F(A)|\le \sum_{S\in Tree(A)}L_S^{can}
   +\sum_{S\in Tree_2(A)} {4G_S^{can}\over q_S^2}
   +\sum_{S\in Tree_2(A)} Short_S^{can}.
\]
Thus all non-diffuse projective-fiber profiles are explicit short
quotient-line root-slice ledgers, while the remaining spread determinant-gate
term has a uniform quadratic saving.
The short ledger localizes further.  For a projective fiber
\(D_{S,\lambda}\) put \(h_\lambda=|D_{S,\lambda}|\) and
\(r_\lambda=q_S-h_\lambda\).  Fibers with \(h_\lambda\le q_S/2\) contribute
nothing to \(Short_S^{can}\).  If \(h_\lambda>q_S/2\), write
\[
  Q_{S,\lambda}=\ell_{D_{S,\lambda}}R_{S,\lambda},\qquad
  \deg R_{S,\lambda}<r_\lambda .
\]
For a short summand \(C\subset D_{S,\lambda}\), let
\(E=D_{S,\lambda}\setminus C\).  Then
\[
  Q'_{S,\lambda,C}=Q_{S,\lambda}/\ell_C
  =\ell_E R_{S,\lambda},\qquad j=r_\lambda+|E|.
\]
If \(Z_{S,\lambda}^{\perp}\) denotes the roots of \(R_{S,\lambda}\) outside
\(D_{S,\lambda}\), with size \(z_{S,\lambda}^{\perp}\), then the forced roots
\(E\) cancel from the quotient-line packing floor:
\[
  \left\lfloor {N_{S,C}-z_{S,\lambda,C}\over j-z_{S,\lambda,C}}\right\rfloor
  =
  \left\lfloor
    {N_S-h_\lambda-z_{S,\lambda}^{\perp}
     \over r_\lambda-z_{S,\lambda}^{\perp}}
  \right\rfloor .
\]
Hence
\[
  Short_S^{can}\le
  \sum_{\lambda:h_\lambda>q_S/2}
  B_{S,\lambda}^{prim}
  \sum_{e=0}^{\lfloor(q_S-1)/2\rfloor-r_\lambda}
  \binom{h_\lambda}{e},
\]
where \(B_{S,\lambda}^{prim}\) is the displayed primitive packing floor.
Thus the short side is only a low-complement tail around primitive half-height
projective-shadow kernels.
Since \(h_\lambda=q_S-r_\lambda\), this primitive factor has the exact surplus
form
\[
  B_{S,\lambda}^{prim}
  =
  1+\left\lfloor
    {N_S-q_S\over r_\lambda-z_{S,\lambda}^{\perp}}
  \right\rfloor ,
\]
with positive denominator because
\(z_{S,\lambda}^{\perp}\le r_\lambda-1\).  In the root-free primitive case it
is \(1+\lfloor(N_S-q_S)/r_\lambda\rfloor\); every loss in the denominator is
accounted for by an explicit perpendicular fixed-root/root-slice defect.
The low-complement multiplicity is a lower binomial tail with a gap from the
center:
\[
  \sum_{e=0}^{\lfloor(q_S-1)/2\rfloor-r_\lambda}\binom{h_\lambda}{e}
  =
  \sum_{e=0}^{\lfloor(h_\lambda-r_\lambda-1)/2\rfloor}
  \binom{h_\lambda}{e}.
\]
If
\(\theta=\lfloor(h_\lambda-r_\lambda-1)/2\rfloor/h_\lambda\), then
\[
  T(h_\lambda,r_\lambda)
  \le (h_\lambda+1)2^{h_\lambda\Htwo(\theta)}
  \quad\text{and}\quad
  T(h_\lambda,r_\lambda)
  \le 2^{h_\lambda}\exp\!\left(-{(r_\lambda+1)^2\over2h_\lambda}\right).
\]
Hence, after perpendicular fixed-root defects are charged, the root-free
primitive branch splits at any cutoff \(R\): denominators
\(r_\lambda<R\) form a bounded-width primitive core, while
\(r_\lambda\ge R\) have the Gaussian tail saving above and packing factor at
most \(1+\lfloor(N_S-q_S)/R\rfloor\).
After fixed-root/root-slice charges, this tail simplifies further.  The
quotient-line direction for the summand indexed by
\(E=D_{S,\lambda}\setminus C\) is
\(\ell_E R_{S,\lambda}\), whose bad-root set is
\(E\sqcup Z_{S,\lambda}^{\perp}\).  Hence every term with
\(E\ne\emptyset\), or with \(z_{S,\lambda}^{\perp}>0\), is already a
displayed fixed-root/root-slice thickening.  The uncharged root-free primitive
half-height core is therefore only
\[
  PrimHalf_S^{rf}=
  \sum_{\lambda:h_\lambda>q_S/2,\ z_{S,\lambda}^{\perp}=0}
  \left(1+\left\lfloor {N_S-q_S\over r_\lambda}\right\rfloor\right),
\]
plus the separately charged fixed-root tail.  Thus the final primitive
half-height obstruction is a global count of root-free primitive directions,
not a binomial-tail multiplicity.
This core is automatically base-free.  If \(x\in B_S\), then
\(P_S(x)=Q_S(x)=0\), hence every \(Q_{S,\lambda}\) vanishes at \(x\); after
dividing by the non-base fiber locator, \(R_{S,\lambda}(x)=0\).  Therefore
\(B_S\subseteq Z_{S,\lambda}^{\perp}\), and \(z_{S,\lambda}^{\perp}=0\)
forces \(B_S=\emptyset\).  At a base-free node, let
\[
  u_S=\lfloor(q_S-1)/2\rfloor,\qquad
  m_{S,r}^{hh}=\#\{\lambda: |D_{S,\lambda}|=q_S-r\}.
\]
The half-height fibers are disjoint, so
\[
  \sum_{r=1}^{u_S}m_{S,r}^{hh}(q_S-r)\le N_S,
\]
and
\[
  PrimHalf_S^{rf}\le
  \sum_{r=1}^{u_S}
  m_{S,r}^{hh}\left(1+\left\lfloor {N_S-q_S\over r}\right\rfloor\right).
\]
In particular, after all bounded-width denominators \(r<R\) have been
charged, the remaining root-free primitive core is at most
\[
  \left\lfloor {N_S\over \lfloor q_S/2\rfloor+1}\right\rfloor
  \left(1+\left\lfloor {N_S-q_S\over R}\right\rfloor\right).
\]
Combining these reductions gives a post-charge canonical endpoint.  For a
chosen primitive-width cutoff \(R_S\), let \(BWPrim_S(R_S)\) be the sum of
\(1+\lfloor(N_S-q_S)/r_\lambda\rfloor\) over root-free primitive
half-height fibers with \(r_\lambda<R_S\), and put
\[
  OccHalf_S(R_S)=
  \begin{cases}
    0, & B_S\ne\emptyset,\\[2mm]
    \left\lfloor {N_S\over \lfloor q_S/2\rfloor+1}\right\rfloor
    \left(1+\left\lfloor {N_S-q_S\over R_S}\right\rfloor\right),
      & B_S=\emptyset .
  \end{cases}
\]
Then
\[
  Short_S^{can}\le
  FixedRootTail_S+BWPrim_S(R_S)+OccHalf_S(R_S),
\]
and hence
\[
  |F(A)|\le \sum_{S\in Tree(A)}L_S^{can}
   +\sum_{S\in Tree_2(A)}{4G_S^{can}\over q_S^2}
   +\sum_{S\in Tree_2(A)}FixedRootTail_S
   +\sum_{S\in Tree_2(A)}BWPrim_S(R_S)
   +\sum_{S\in Tree_2(A)}OccHalf_S(R_S).
\]
Thus, after lower-dimensional, fixed-root, bounded-width primitive, and
diffuse charges, the remaining half-height term is only the base-free
occupancy count \(OccHalf_S(R_S)\).
Keeping the height histogram gives a sharper local optimization.  For
\(1\le R\le u_S+1\), set
\[
  HOcc_S(R)=
  \begin{cases}
    0, & B_S\ne\emptyset,\\[1mm]
    \displaystyle
    \sum_{r=R}^{u_S}
      m_{S,r}^{hh}\left(1+\left\lfloor {N_S-q_S\over r}\right\rfloor\right),
      & B_S=\emptyset,
  \end{cases}
\]
with empty sum when \(R=u_S+1\).  Then
\[
  Short_S^{can}\le FixedRootTail_S+BWPrim_S(R)+HOcc_S(R),
\]
and the cutoff can be optimized locally:
\[
  PostCore_S^*=\min_{1\le R\le u_S+1}\bigl(BWPrim_S(R)+HOcc_S(R)\bigr).
\]
Consequently
\[
  |F(A)|\le \sum_{S\in Tree(A)}L_S^{can}
   +\sum_{S\in Tree_2(A)}{4G_S^{can}\over q_S^2}
   +\sum_{S\in Tree_2(A)}FixedRootTail_S
   +\sum_{S\in Tree_2(A)}PostCore_S^* .
\]
This is the histogram-optimized post-charge endpoint; the coarser
\(OccHalf_S(R_S)\) follows by replacing the histogram by the trivial
half-height fiber-count bound.
There is a useful canonical cutoff.  If the node is nonempty, set
\[
  s_S=N_S-q_S,\qquad R_S^{bal}=\min(u_S+1,s_S+1).
\]
For this choice, the wide histogram tail has no surplus factor:
\[
  HOcc_S(R_S^{bal})
  \le \left\lfloor {N_S\over \lfloor q_S/2\rfloor+1}\right\rfloor .
\]
Indeed, if \(s_S\ge u_S\) then the wide histogram sum is empty; otherwise
\(r\ge s_S+1\) gives \(1+\lfloor s_S/r\rfloor=1\), and only the disjoint
half-height fiber count remains.  Hence
\[
  PostCore_S^*
  \le BWPrim_S(R_S^{bal})
     +\left\lfloor {N_S\over \lfloor q_S/2\rfloor+1}\right\rfloor ,
\]
where \(BWPrim_S(R_S^{bal})\) contains only denominators
\(r_\lambda\le\min(u_S,s_S)\).
The bare half-height count is itself a surplus ledger.  With
\(h_{0,S}=\lfloor q_S/2\rfloor+1\) and \(s_S=N_S-q_S\ge0\),
\[
  \left\lfloor {N_S\over h_{0,S}}\right\rfloor
  =
  1+\left\lfloor {s_S+\lceil q_S/2\rceil-1\over h_{0,S}}\right\rfloor
  \le 1+\left\lceil {s_S\over h_{0,S}}\right\rceil
  \le 1+\left\lceil {2s_S\over q_S}\right\rceil .
\]
Hence
\[
  PostCore_S^*
  \le BWPrim_S(R_S^{bal})
     +1+\left\lceil {N_S-q_S\over \lfloor q_S/2\rfloor+1}\right\rceil .
\]
After fixed-root tails and primitive denominators of width at most the node
surplus have been charged, the uncharged half-height core is therefore a
one-per-node term plus a surplus-over-half-width term.
The surplus term has exact canonical bookkeeping.  Fix the canonical order on
\(B_0^*(A)\).  For a nonempty node \(S\), let \(m(S)=\max S\) and set
\[
  b(S)=|\{x\in B_0^*(A):x\le m(S)\}|,\qquad
  \operatorname{skip}(S)=b(S)-|S|,
\]
with \(b(\emptyset)=\operatorname{skip}(\emptyset)=0\).  Since
\(Old(S)=\{x\in B_0^*(A)\setminus S:x<m(S)\}\), the canonical domain obeys
\[
  D_S^{can}=D'\setminus(S\cup Old(S)),\qquad
  S\cup Old(S)=\{x\in B_0^*(A):x\le m(S)\}.
\]
Thus, writing \(s_\emptyset=|D'|-q\),
\[
  s_S=N_S-q_S=s_\emptyset-\operatorname{skip}(S).
\]
If \(T=S\cup\{x\}\) is a canonical child and \(\gamma_S(x)\) is the number of
base roots strictly between \(m(S)\) and \(x\) (or strictly before \(x\) when
\(S=\emptyset\)), then
\[
  \operatorname{skip}(T)=\operatorname{skip}(S)+\gamma_S(x),
  \qquad s_T=s_S-\gamma_S(x).
\]
Hence surplus never increases along canonical peeling; skipped older base
roots debit it one-for-one.  The remaining surplus-over-half-width term is
therefore an explicit unused-initial-surplus ledger, not a multiplicative
loss in the tree depth.
This also gives a terminal skip-ball envelope for the one-per-node term.  Put
\(b_0=|B_0^*(A)|\) and \(s_0=|D'|-q\).  If \(S\) is base-free, \(B_S=\emptyset\),
then either \(S=\emptyset\) and \(b_0=0\), or \(S\) contains the largest base
root.  For if \(y\in B_0^*(A)\setminus(S\cup Old(S))\), then \(y\) remains in
\(D_S^{can}\) and \(P_S(y)=Q_S(y)=0\), so \(y\in B_S\).  Hence
\[
  B_S=\emptyset\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
  S\cup Old(S)=B_0^*(A).
\]
Thus base-free half-height nodes are terminal leaves of the canonical base
peeling.  If \(b_0>0\), a leaf with \(\ell\) skipped roots has
\[
  |S|=b_0-\ell,\qquad q_S=q-b_0+\ell,\qquad s_S=s_0-\ell,
\]
and activity forces \(\ell\le s_0\).  Therefore
\[
 \sum_{\substack{S\in Tree_2(A)\\ B_S=\emptyset}}
 \left(1+\left\lceil {s_S\over \lfloor q_S/2\rfloor+1}\right\rceil\right)
 \le
 \begin{cases}
  1+\left\lceil {s_0\over \lfloor q/2\rfloor+1}\right\rceil,
    & b_0=0,\\[4pt]
  \displaystyle
  \sum_{\ell=0}^{\min(s_0,b_0-1)}
    \binom{b_0-1}{\ell}
    \left(1+\left\lceil
      {s_0-\ell\over \lfloor(q-b_0+\ell)/2\rfloor+1}
    \right\rceil\right),
    & b_0>0.
 \end{cases}
\]
So the residual root-free half-height baseline is supported on a terminal
skip ball of radius \(s_0\), not on every level of the peeling tree.
Consequently this baseline is polynomial in every fixed-surplus regime.  Since
\(b_0\le q-1\) at a \(b=2\) root node and every node in \(Tree_2(A)\) has
\(q_S\ge2\),
\[
  1+\left\lceil {s_0-\ell\over \lfloor q_S/2\rfloor+1}\right\rceil
  \le 1+\left\lceil {s_0\over2}\right\rceil .
\]
Hence, for \(b_0>0\),
\[
 \sum_{\substack{S\in Tree_2(A)\\ B_S=\emptyset}}
 \left(1+\left\lceil {s_S\over \lfloor q_S/2\rfloor+1}\right\rceil\right)
 \le
 \left(1+\left\lceil {s_0\over2}\right\rceil\right)
 \sum_{\ell=0}^{\min(s_0,b_0-1)}\binom{b_0-1}{\ell}
 \le
 \left(1+\left\lceil {s_0\over2}\right\rceil\right)(s_0+1)q^{s_0},
\]
with the \(b_0=0\) case satisfying the same final bound by the singleton
bound above.  Thus for fixed initial surplus the residual base-free
half-height baseline is a polynomial terminal ledger after the fixed-root,
bounded-width, diffuse, and lower-dimensional terms have been separated.
The bounded-width primitive part of the same root-free half-height core is
also polynomial for fixed surplus.  At a base-free node, the fibers counted by
\(BWPrim_S(R_S^{bal})\) are disjoint half-height fibers, so there are at most
\(\lfloor N_S/(\lfloor q_S/2\rfloor+1)\rfloor\) of them; each has primitive
weight at most \(s_S+1\).  The bare-occupancy estimate therefore gives
\[
  BWPrim_S(R_S^{bal})\le (s_S+1)^2.
\]
Combining this node bound with the skip-ball parametrization gives
\[
 \sum_{S\in Tree_2(A)}PostCore_S^*
 \le
 \begin{cases}
  (s_0+1)^2+
  1+\left\lceil {s_0\over\lfloor q/2\rfloor+1}\right\rceil,
    & b_0=0,\\[4pt]
  \displaystyle
  \sum_{\ell=0}^{\min(s_0,b_0-1)}
    \binom{b_0-1}{\ell}
    \left((s_0-\ell+1)^2
      +1+\left\lceil
        {s_0-\ell\over \lfloor(q-b_0+\ell)/2\rfloor+1}
      \right\rceil\right),
    & b_0>0.
 \end{cases}
\]
In particular,
\[
 \sum_{S\in Tree_2(A)}PostCore_S^*
 \le
 \left((s_0+1)^2+1+\left\lceil {s_0\over2}\right\rceil\right)
 (s_0+1)q^{s_0}.
\]
Thus the whole root-free half-height post-core is \(O_\sigma(q^\sigma)\)
when \(s_0\le\sigma\) is fixed; fixed-root tails, diffuse capacity, and
lower-dimensional leaves are the remaining ledgers.
The active canonical tree itself is also polynomial in fixed surplus.  If
\(Active(A)\) denotes the canonical nodes with nonempty residual fiber, then
activity implies \(\operatorname{skip}(S)\le s_0\).  Writing
\(B_0^*(A)=\{x_1<\cdots<x_{b_0}\}\), a nonempty node with maximum \(x_m\) and
\(\operatorname{skip}(S)=\ell\) is determined by the \(\ell\) skipped roots
among \(x_1,\ldots,x_{m-1}\).  Hence
\[
  |Active(A)|
  \le
  1+\sum_{\ell=0}^{\min(s_0,b_0-1)}
      \sum_{m=\ell+1}^{b_0}\binom{m-1}{\ell}
  \le
  \sum_{j=0}^{s_0+1}\binom{b_0}{j}
  \le (s_0+2)q^{s_0+1}.
\]
For an active \(b=2\) node, \(q_S\ge2\), \(N_S=q_S+s_S\), and
\(0\le s_S\le s_0\), while \(G_S^{can}\le\binom{N_S}{2}\).  Therefore
\[
  \sum_{S\in Tree_2(A)}{4G_S^{can}\over q_S^2}
  \le 2\left(1+{s_0\over2}\right)^2(s_0+2)q^{s_0+1}.
\]
The lower-dimensional leaves are polynomial as well: \(b=0\) leaves contribute
at most \(1\), and \(b=1\) leaves satisfy
\[
