  |F_S^{can}(A)|\le
  \left\lfloor {N_S-z_S\over q_S-z_S}\right\rfloor
  \le N_S\le q+s_0.
\]
Thus
\[
  \sum_{S\in Tree(A)}L_S^{can}
  \le (q+s_0)(s_0+2)q^{s_0+1}.
\]
Combining these bounds with the post-charge endpoint and the fixed-surplus
post-core bound gives
\[
  |F(A)|\le \sum_{S\in Tree_2(A)}FixedRootTail_S
  +O_\sigma(q^{\sigma+2})
  \qquad (s_0\le\sigma).
\]
So, in fixed-slack regimes, this canonical \(b=2\) branch is reduced to the
explicit fixed-root/root-slice tail ledger up to a polynomial remainder.
This remaining tail has an exact absorbed one-root form.  For a half-height
fiber \(\lambda\) and a thickening set \(E\subset D_{S,\lambda}\), put
\[
  C=D_{S,\lambda}\setminus E,\qquad
  e=|E|,\qquad j=r_\lambda+e,\qquad
  B_{\rm tail}(E)=E\sqcup Z_{S,\lambda}^{\perp}.
\]
Then
\[
  FixedRootTail_S
  =
  \sum_{\lambda:h_\lambda>q_S/2}
  \sum_{\substack{E\ {\rm admissible}\\B_{\rm tail}(E)\ne\emptyset}}
  B_{S,\lambda}^{prim}.
\]
Choose the least root \(x(E)\in B_{\rm tail}(E)\).  Since
\(Q'_{S,\lambda,E}=\ell_E R_{S,\lambda}\) vanishes at \(x(E)\),
\[
  Q'_{S,\lambda,E}=(X-x(E))Q^x_{S,\lambda,E},
  \qquad \deg Q^x_{S,\lambda,E}<j-1.
\]
The root \(x(E)\) is still in the quotient domain, and the same
divisible-kernel identity used for quotient-line packings makes
\(Q^x_{S,\lambda,E}\) a one-root absorbed fixed-divisor direction.  The case
\(j=1\) cannot occur in the tail, so the absorbed residual width \(j-1\) is
positive.  If \(N_{S,C}=N_S-q_S+j\) is the quotient domain size before
absorbing \(x(E)\), then
\[
  (N_{S,C}-1)-(j-1)=N_S-q_S=s_S.
\]
Thus every fixed-root tail summand preserves surplus and lowers the quotient
width by one after a canonical one-root absorption.  The remaining unclosed
ledger is therefore a precise one-root absorbed fixed-divisor ledger, not a
new \(b=2\) reservoir.
Absorbing all bad roots in the same summand gives an even sharper normal
form.  With \(B=B_{\rm tail}(E)\) and \(z=z_{S,\lambda}^{\perp}\),
\[
  Q^{B}_{S,\lambda,E}
  ={Q'_{S,\lambda,E}\over \ell_B}
  ={R_{S,\lambda}\over \ell_{Z_{S,\lambda}^{\perp}}},
\]
and the fully absorbed width and domain size are
\[
  j_B=j-|B|=r_\lambda-z,\qquad
  N_B=N_{S,C}-|B|=N_S-h_\lambda-z.
\]
Thus
\[
  N_B-j_B=N_S-q_S=s_S.
\]
The direction \(Q^{B}_{S,\lambda,E}\) has no roots on the fully absorbed
domain: \(C\cup E\) removes the whole half-height fiber, and
\(Z_{S,\lambda}^{\perp}\) removes the remaining roots of \(R_{S,\lambda}\).
Therefore each tail packing factor is exactly
\[
  B_{S,\lambda}^{prim}
  =\left\lfloor {N_B\over j_B}\right\rfloor
  =1+\left\lfloor {s_S\over r_\lambda-z_{S,\lambda}^{\perp}}\right\rfloor .
\]
The residual tail difficulty is consequently the multiplicity of the absorbed
bad-root certificates, not an additional packing loss after absorption.
Moreover the fully absorbed kernel no longer depends on the tail subset
\(E\).  For fixed \(S,\lambda\), every admissible tail summand collapses to
the same absorbed certificate
\[
  A^{abs}_{S,\lambda}=
  S\cup D_{S,\lambda}\cup Z_{S,\lambda}^{\perp},
  \qquad
  Q^{abs}_{S,\lambda}=
  {R_{S,\lambda}\over \ell_{Z_{S,\lambda}^{\perp}}},
  \qquad
  w^{abs}_{S,\lambda}=r_\lambda-z_{S,\lambda}^{\perp}.
\]
The number of such collapsed certificates is polynomial in fixed surplus.
Indeed, at an active node the half-height fibers are disjoint and each has
size at least \(h_{0,S}=\lfloor q_S/2\rfloor+1\), so
\[
  |\{\lambda:|D_{S,\lambda}|>q_S/2\}|
  \le \left\lfloor {N_S\over h_{0,S}}\right\rfloor
  \le 1+\left\lceil {s_0\over2}\right\rceil .
\]
Together with the active skip-ball bound, this gives
\[
  \#\{A^{abs}_{S,\lambda}:S\in Tree_2(A),\ F_S^{can}\ne\emptyset\}
  \le
  \left(1+\left\lceil {s_0\over2}\right\rceil\right)
  (s_0+2)q^{s_0+1}.
\]
Thus fixed surplus leaves only polynomially many fully absorbed tail
certificates; the remaining issue is the lifting multiplicity from each
collapsed certificate back to its admissible tail thickenings.
This lifting multiplicity factors exactly.  Define
\[
  L_{S,\lambda}^{tail}=
  \begin{cases}
    T(h_\lambda,r_\lambda),& z_{S,\lambda}^{\perp}>0,\\
    T(h_\lambda,r_\lambda)-1,& z_{S,\lambda}^{\perp}=0.
  \end{cases}
\]
Then
\[
  FixedRootTail_S
  =
  \sum_{\lambda:h_\lambda>q_S/2}
  L_{S,\lambda}^{tail}
  \left(1+\left\lfloor
    {s_S\over r_\lambda-z_{S,\lambda}^{\perp}}
  \right\rfloor\right).
\]
If \(s_0\le\sigma\), the packing factor is at most \(\sigma+1\).  With
\[
  TailLift(A)=
  \sum_{\substack{S\in Tree_2(A)\\F_S^{can}\ne\emptyset}}
  \sum_{\lambda:h_\lambda>q_S/2}L_{S,\lambda}^{tail},
\]
the fixed-surplus endpoint sharpens to
\[
  |F(A)|\le (\sigma+1)TailLift(A)+O_\sigma(q^{\sigma+2}).
\]
Thus the exact remaining target in this branch is polynomial control of
\(TailLift(A)\), or a classification of the collapsed absorbed certificates
with large low-complement lift multiplicity.
There is an immediate polynomial regime.  Fix \(d\ge0\).  If
\(h_\lambda-r_\lambda\le2d+2\), then
\[
  L_{S,\lambda}^{tail}\le T(h_\lambda,r_\lambda)
  \le\sum_{e=0}^d\binom{h_\lambda}{e}
  \le (d+1)q_S^d.
\]
Let \(TailLift_{>d}(A)\) be the part of \(TailLift(A)\) supported on
\(h_\lambda-r_\lambda\ge2d+3\).  The collapsed-certificate count gives
\[
  TailLift(A)\le TailLift_{>d}(A)
  +(d+1)\left(1+\left\lceil{s_0\over2}\right\rceil\right)
  (s_0+2)q^{s_0+d+1}.
\]
Thus, for fixed \(\sigma,d\),
\[
  |F(A)|\le (\sigma+1)TailLift_{>d}(A)
  +O_{\sigma,d}(q^{\sigma+d+1}+q^{\sigma+2})
  \qquad (s_0\le\sigma).
\]
The only remaining non-polynomial tail risk is therefore the deep-tail
subledger \(h_\lambda-r_\lambda\ge2d+3\), where the dominant projective fiber
exceeds its complement width by an unbounded amount.
The entropy estimate gives a sharper scalar split.  Put
\[
  \theta_{S,\lambda}
  ={ \lfloor(h_\lambda-r_\lambda-1)/2\rfloor\over h_\lambda},
  \qquad
  E_{S,\lambda}=h_\lambda H_2(\theta_{S,\lambda}).
\]
For fixed \(B\ge0\), let \(TailLift_{\mathrm{ent}>B}(A)\) be supported on
certificates with \(E_{S,\lambda}>B\log_2 q\).  On the complement,
\[
  L_{S,\lambda}^{tail}\le (h_\lambda+1)2^{E_{S,\lambda}}
  \le (q+1)q^B.
\]
Together with the collapsed-certificate count,
\[
  TailLift(A)\le TailLift_{\mathrm{ent}>B}(A)
  +(q+1)q^B
  \left(1+\left\lceil{s_0\over2}\right\rceil\right)
  (s_0+2)q^{s_0+1}.
\]
Hence, for fixed \(\sigma,B\),
\[
  |F(A)|\le(\sigma+1)TailLift_{\mathrm{ent}>B}(A)
  +O_{\sigma,B}(q^{\sigma+B+2}+q^{\sigma+2})
  \qquad (s_0\le\sigma).
\]
So the remaining obstruction may be restricted to entropy-large collapsed
absorbed certificates, not all deep-tail certificates.
Combining the depth and entropy splits gives one critical residual ledger.
Let \(TailLift_{\mathrm{crit}(d,B)}(A)\) be supported on certificates with
\[
  h_\lambda-r_\lambda\ge2d+3,
  \qquad E_{S,\lambda}>B\log_2 q.
\]
Every non-critical certificate is either near-threshold or entropy-small, so
\[
  TailLift(A)\le TailLift_{\mathrm{crit}(d,B)}(A)
  +O_{s_0,d,B}(q^{s_0+d+1}+q^{s_0+B+2}).
\]
Consequently, for fixed \(\sigma,d,B\),
\[
  |F(A)|\le(\sigma+1)TailLift_{\mathrm{crit}(d,B)}(A)
  +O_{\sigma,d,B}(q^{\sigma+d+1}+q^{\sigma+B+2}+q^{\sigma+2})
  \qquad (s_0\le\sigma).
\]
Thus the fixed-surplus canonical \(b=2\) branch is reduced to collapsed
absorbed certificates that are simultaneously deep and entropy-large.
This gives a closure criterion.  Fix \(\sigma,d,B\).  If, for every
fixed-anchor canonical \(b=2\) problem with \(s_0\le\sigma\),
\[
  TailLift_{\mathrm{crit}(d,B)}(A)\le C_{\sigma,d,B}q^{K_{\sigma,d,B}},
\]
or if this critical ledger is charged to already-polynomial quotient-periodic,
tangent, fixed-root, or aperiodic ledgers, then
\[
  |F(A)|\le C'_{\sigma,d,B}q^{K'_{\sigma,d,B}}.
\]
Equivalently, any obstruction to this fixed-surplus route must now be a family
of critical collapsed absorbed certificates with bounded surplus,
simultaneous depth and entropy largeness, and super-polynomial lift
multiplicity outside the existing charged ledgers.
The critical tail can be localized to one-root slices.  For one collapsed
certificate set
\[
  a=\left\lfloor {h_\lambda-r_\lambda-1\over2}\right\rfloor
\]
and let \(M_{S,\lambda}\) be the largest number of same-size admissible tail
thickenings \(E\) whose bad set \(B_{\rm tail}(E)\) contains one fixed root.
The largest slice occurs at the top level \(e=a\): it has size
\(\binom{h_\lambda}{a}\) if a perpendicular bad root is present, and
\(\binom{h_\lambda-1}{a-1}\) otherwise.  Hence
\[
  L_{S,\lambda}^{tail}\le q_SM_{S,\lambda}.
\]
Hence, with
\[
  RootTail_{\mathrm{crit}(d,B)}(A)
  =\sum_{(S,\lambda)\ {\rm critical}}M_{S,\lambda},
\]
one has
\[
  TailLift_{\mathrm{crit}(d,B)}(A)
  \le q\,RootTail_{\mathrm{crit}(d,B)}(A).
\]
Thus polynomial control of the one-root critical tail-slice ledger also closes
the fixed-surplus branch.  Conversely, if a critical certificate is
entropy-large, the type bound
\[
  \binom{h_\lambda}{a}\ge 2^{h_\lambda H_2(a/h_\lambda)}/(h_\lambda+1)
\]
shows that some top-level one-root slice has size at least
\[
  M_{S,\lambda}\ge q^B/(q+1)^2.
\]
So an entropy-large tail obstruction must already appear as a large fixed-level
one-root absorbed slice, not merely as diffuse mass over many tail levels.
In the root-free case this top slice descends without loss.  If
\(Z_{S,\lambda}^{\perp}=\emptyset\), \(a\ge1\), and
\(x\in D_{S,\lambda}\), then every top-level thickening containing \(x\) has
\(E=\{x\}\sqcup E'\) with \(|E'|=a-1\), and after absorbing \(x\)
\[
  Q^x_{S,\lambda,E}=\ell_{E'}R_{S,\lambda}.
\]
With
\[
  q^x=q_S-1,\qquad h^x=h_\lambda-1,\qquad r^x=r_\lambda,
\]
one has \(q^x=h^x+r^x\), \(h^x-r^x=h_\lambda-r_\lambda-1\), and
\[
  \left\lfloor {h^x-r^x-1\over2}\right\rfloor-(a-1)
  =
  \begin{cases}
  0,& h_\lambda-r_\lambda\ {\rm odd},\\
  1,& h_\lambda-r_\lambda\ {\rm even}.
  \end{cases}
\]
The surplus is unchanged:
\[
  (N_{S,C}-1)-(j-1)=N_S-q_S.
\]
Thus root-free critical top slices are the same primitive tail problem one
residual degree lower, at top level or one below top, not a fresh
multiplicative reservoir.
Iterating gives an exact flag bijection.  If
\(P=(x_1,\ldots,x_m)\subset D_{S,\lambda}\) is an ordered distinct prefix,
\(0\le m\le a\), then top thickenings containing \(P\) are in bijection with
sets \(E_m\subset D_{S,\lambda}\setminus P\), \(|E_m|=a-m\), and after
absorbing \(P\)
\[
  Q^P_{S,\lambda,E}=\ell_{E_m}R_{S,\lambda}.
\]
The descended parameters are
\[
  q^P=q_S-m,\quad h^P=h_\lambda-m,\quad r^P=r_\lambda,\quad s^P=s_S,
\]
so the depth gap is lowered by exactly \(m\).  If \(g=h_\lambda-r_\lambda\)
and \(a^P=\lfloor(h^P-r^P-1)/2\rfloor\), then
\[
  a^P-(a-m)=
  \begin{cases}
  \lfloor m/2\rfloor,& g\ {\rm odd},\\
  \lceil m/2\rceil,& g\ {\rm even}.
  \end{cases}
\]
For fixed \(P\) there are exactly \(\binom{h_\lambda-m}{a-m}\) descended
choices, and altogether the ordered \(m\)-step flags number
\((a)_m\binom{h_\lambda}{a}\), with \((a)_m\) the falling factorial.  Thus
rung-by-rung descent only expands the original top-level flag family; it does
not multiply in an independent loss at each absorbed root.
The perpendicular case is instead a fixed-root full star.  If
\(Z_{S,\lambda}^{\perp}\ne\emptyset\) and \(z\in Z_{S,\lambda}^{\perp}\),
then \(z\in B_{\rm tail}(E)\) for every top thickening
\(|E|=a\), so
\[
  \#\Theta_a(S,\lambda;z)=\binom{h_\lambda}{a}.
\]
Writing \(R_{S,\lambda}=(X-z)R^z_{S,\lambda}\), absorption gives
\[
  Q^z_{S,\lambda,E}=\ell_E R^z_{S,\lambda},
\]
with descended parameters
\[
  q^z=q_S-1,\quad h^z=h_\lambda,\quad r^z=r_\lambda-1,\quad s^z=s_S.
\]
Thus \(h^z-r^z=h_\lambda-r_\lambda+1\), and the old top level is the new top
level if \(h_\lambda-r_\lambda\) is odd and one below top if it is even.
The surplus identity remains
\[
  (N_{S,C}-1)-(j-1)=N_S-q_S.
\]
So perpendicular entropy-large witnesses are full-star fixed-root absorbed
slices; after those are charged, the root-free flag recursion is the only
remaining critical top-slice mechanism.
Equivalently, define
\[
\begin{aligned}
PerpStar_{\mathrm{crit}(d,B)}(A)
&=\sum_{\substack{(S,\lambda)\ {\rm critical}\\Z_{S,\lambda}^{\perp}\ne\emptyset}}
  \binom{h_\lambda}{a},\\
RFFlag_{\mathrm{crit}(d,B)}(A)
&=\sum_{\substack{(S,\lambda)\ {\rm critical}\\Z_{S,\lambda}^{\perp}=\emptyset}}
  \binom{h_\lambda-1}{a-1}.
\end{aligned}
\]
Then
\[
  RootTail_{\mathrm{crit}(d,B)}(A)
  =PerpStar_{\mathrm{crit}(d,B)}(A)+RFFlag_{\mathrm{crit}(d,B)}(A),
\]
and
\[
  TailLift_{\mathrm{crit}(d,B)}(A)
  \le q\bigl(PerpStar_{\mathrm{crit}(d,B)}(A)
             +RFFlag_{\mathrm{crit}(d,B)}(A)\bigr).
\]
Thus, for fixed \(\sigma,d,B\),
\[
  |F(A)|\le(\sigma+1)q(PerpStar_{\mathrm{crit}(d,B)}(A)
  +RFFlag_{\mathrm{crit}(d,B)}(A))
  +O_{\sigma,d,B}(q^{\sigma+d+1}+q^{\sigma+B+2}+q^{\sigma+2})
  \qquad(s_0\le\sigma).
\]
After the perpendicular full stars are charged to fixed-root/root-slice
ledgers, the remaining critical-tail target is exactly the root-free flag
ledger \(RFFlag_{\mathrm{crit}(d,B)}\).
The final ambient \(q\) in this post-star endpoint can be replaced by local
weights.  Define
\[
\begin{aligned}
WPerpStar_{\mathrm{crit}(d,B)}(A)
&=\sum_{\substack{(S,\lambda)\ {\rm critical}\\Z_{S,\lambda}^{\perp}\ne\emptyset}}
  (a+1)\binom{h_\lambda}{a},\\
WRFFlag_{\mathrm{crit}(d,B)}(A)
&=\sum_{\substack{(S,\lambda)\ {\rm critical}\\Z_{S,\lambda}^{\perp}=\emptyset}}
  h_\lambda\binom{h_\lambda-1}{a-1}.
\end{aligned}
\]
For one critical certificate, the perpendicular case contributes at most
\(\sum_{e=0}^a\binom{h_\lambda}{e}\le(a+1)\binom{h_\lambda}{a}\), while the
root-free case contributes at most
\(\sum_{e=1}^a\binom{h_\lambda}{e}\le a\binom{h_\lambda}{a}
=h_\lambda\binom{h_\lambda-1}{a-1}\).  Hence
\[
  TailLift_{\mathrm{crit}(d,B)}(A)
  \le WPerpStar_{\mathrm{crit}(d,B)}(A)
     +WRFFlag_{\mathrm{crit}(d,B)}(A),
\]
and therefore, for fixed \(\sigma,d,B\),
\[
  |F(A)|\le(\sigma+1)(WPerpStar_{\mathrm{crit}(d,B)}(A)
  +WRFFlag_{\mathrm{crit}(d,B)}(A))
  +O_{\sigma,d,B}(q^{\sigma+d+1}+q^{\sigma+B+2}+q^{\sigma+2})
  \qquad(s_0\le\sigma).
\]
This is a sharper sufficient top-level target after fixed-root/full-star
charges: control the locally weighted root-free flag ledger, not an ambient
\(q\)-multiple of it.
There is also an exact canonical version.  Define
\[
\begin{aligned}
PerpTailStar_{\mathrm{crit}(d,B)}(A)
&=\sum_{\substack{(S,\lambda)\ {\rm critical}\\Z_{S,\lambda}^{\perp}\ne\emptyset}}
  \sum_{e=0}^a\binom{h_\lambda}{e},\\
CanRFFlag_{\mathrm{crit}(d,B)}(A)
&=\sum_{\substack{(S,\lambda)\ {\rm critical}\\Z_{S,\lambda}^{\perp}=\emptyset}}
  \sum_{e=1}^a\binom{h_\lambda}{e}.
\end{aligned}
\]
Then
\[
  TailLift_{\mathrm{crit}(d,B)}(A)
  =PerpTailStar_{\mathrm{crit}(d,B)}(A)
   +CanRFFlag_{\mathrm{crit}(d,B)}(A).
\]
For the root-free summands, fix the ambient order on \(D_{S,\lambda}\).  Each
nonempty thickening \(E\) has a unique increasing enumeration
\(E=\{x_1<\cdots<x_e\}\), and absorbing an initial segment gives
\[
  Q^{(x_1,\ldots,x_m)}_{S,\lambda,E}
  =\ell_{E\setminus\{x_1,\ldots,x_m\}}R_{S,\lambda},\quad
  q^{P_m}=q_S-m,\quad h^{P_m}=h_\lambda-m,\quad r^{P_m}=r_\lambda,\quad
  s^{P_m}=s_S.
\]
Thus the root-free descent has a canonical increasing-flag partition: each
tail thickening follows one path through the recursion, and no multiplicative
branching factor is introduced by iterating the descent.  Moreover
\[
  CanRFFlag_{\mathrm{crit}(d,B)}(A)
  \le WRFFlag_{\mathrm{crit}(d,B)}(A),
\]
so the weighted ledger is a sufficient top-level proxy for the sharper exact
canonical residual.
The exact canonical ledger also has a denominator-gap top bound.  For a
root-free critical certificate, put \(g=h_\lambda-r_\lambda\) and
\(a=\lfloor(g-1)/2\rfloor\).  Since
\[
  \frac{\binom{h_\lambda}{e}}{\binom{h_\lambda}{e+1}}
  =\frac{e+1}{h_\lambda-e}
  \le\frac{a}{h_\lambda-a+1}\qquad(0\le e<a),
\]
the lower tail is geometrically dominated by its top level:
\[
  \sum_{e=1}^a\binom{h_\lambda}{e}
  \le
  \frac{h_\lambda-a+1}{h_\lambda-2a+1}\binom{h_\lambda}{a}.
\]
Moreover \(h_\lambda-2a+1\ge r_\lambda+2\), so
\[
  CanRFFlag_{\mathrm{crit}(d,B)}(A)
  \le
  \sum_{\substack{(S,\lambda)\ {\rm critical}\\Z_{S,\lambda}^{\perp}=\emptyset}}
  \frac{h_\lambda+1}{r_\lambda+2}\binom{h_\lambda}{a}.
\]
Thus wide residual denominators pay only a gap-weighted top-level cost; a
counterexample retaining a near-ambient loss must be concentrated in small
root-free residual widths.
This gives a cutoff endpoint for the critical tail.  For an integer \(R\ge1\),
define
\[
\begin{aligned}
CanRFFlag_{\mathrm{crit}(d,B),<R}(A)
&=\sum_{\substack{(S,\lambda)\ {\rm critical}\\Z_{S,\lambda}^{\perp}=\emptyset\\
                  r_\lambda<R}}
  \sum_{e=1}^a\binom{h_\lambda}{e},\\
GapTopRF_{\mathrm{crit}(d,B),\ge R}(A)
&=\sum_{\substack{(S,\lambda)\ {\rm critical}\\Z_{S,\lambda}^{\perp}=\emptyset\\
                  r_\lambda\ge R}}
  \frac{h_\lambda-a+1}{h_\lambda-2a+1}\binom{h_\lambda}{a},\\
TopRF_{\mathrm{crit}(d,B),\ge R}(A)
&=\sum_{\substack{(S,\lambda)\ {\rm critical}\\Z_{S,\lambda}^{\perp}=\emptyset\\
                  r_\lambda\ge R}}
  \binom{h_\lambda}{a}.
\end{aligned}
\]
Then
\[
  TailLift_{\mathrm{crit}(d,B)}(A)
  \le PerpTailStar_{\mathrm{crit}(d,B)}(A)
     +CanRFFlag_{\mathrm{crit}(d,B),<R}(A)
     +GapTopRF_{\mathrm{crit}(d,B),\ge R}(A),
\]
and since \(h_\lambda\le q\),
\[
  GapTopRF_{\mathrm{crit}(d,B),\ge R}(A)
  \le {q+1\over R+2}TopRF_{\mathrm{crit}(d,B),\ge R}(A).
\]
Consequently, for fixed \(\sigma,d,B\),
\[
  |F(A)|\le(\sigma+1)\bigl(
     PerpTailStar_{\mathrm{crit}(d,B)}(A)
    +CanRFFlag_{\mathrm{crit}(d,B),<R}(A)
    +GapTopRF_{\mathrm{crit}(d,B),\ge R}(A)\bigr)
  +O_{\sigma,d,B}(q^{\sigma+d+1}+q^{\sigma+B+2}+q^{\sigma+2})
  \qquad(s_0\le\sigma).
\]
After perpendicular full-star charges, the residual is therefore a low-width
root-free classification problem plus a wide-denominator top-packet packing
problem with factor \((q+1)/(R+2)\), rather than an ambient one-root loss.
Equivalently, the wide side closes under one denominator of top-packet saving
on dyadic bins.  Fix \(R_0\ge1\), and for dyadic \(R=2^iR_0\) put
\[
TopRF_{\mathrm{crit}(d,B),[R,2R)}(A)
=\sum_{\substack{(S,\lambda)\ {\rm critical}\\Z_{S,\lambda}^{\perp}=\emptyset\\
                 R\le r_\lambda<2R}}
  \binom{h_\lambda}{a}.
\]
Then
\[
  CanRFFlag_{\mathrm{crit}(d,B),\ge R_0}(A)
  \le
  \sum_{\substack{R\ {\rm dyadic}\\R_0\le R\le q}}
  {q+1\over R+2}TopRF_{\mathrm{crit}(d,B),[R,2R)}(A).
\]
Hence if the narrow ledger is \(O(q^K)\) and each dyadic top-packet bin
satisfies \(TopRF_{\mathrm{crit}(d,B),[R,2R)}(A)\le C Rq^K\), then the full
canonical root-free critical flag ledger is polynomial.  This is the exact
denominator-saving target left by the cutoff: low-width classification plus
one factor of \(R\) saving for wide aperiodic top packets.
The low-width side is itself an exclusion target.  Fix \(R_0\).  For a
root-free critical certificate with \(r_\lambda<R_0\), set
\(h=h_\lambda\), \(a=\lfloor(h-r_\lambda-1)/2\rfloor\), and
\(m=\lfloor h/2\rfloor\).  Then \(0\le m-a\le R_0\), so
\[
  \binom h a
  \ge {2^h\over (h+1)^{R_0+1}}
  \ge {2^{q_S-R_0}\over (q+1)^{R_0+1}}.
\]
Since the canonical root-free flag ledger contains this top level, a single
uncharged low-width critical certificate with \(q_S\gg\log q\) already exceeds
any fixed polynomial budget.  Thus low residual width must be structurally
excluded or charged; it cannot be handled by merely counting certificates.
The logarithmic exception is polynomial.  If \(s_0\le\sigma\) and the
low-width ledger is restricted further to \(q_S\le L\log_2 q\), then each
certificate contributes at most \(2^{q_S}\le q^L\), while
(PF2-collapsed-tail-fixed) gives at most
\((1+\lceil\sigma/2\rceil)(\sigma+2)q^{\sigma+1}\) collapsed certificates.
Therefore
\[
  CanRFFlag_{\mathrm{crit}(d,B),<R_0,\log L}(A)
  \le
  (1+\lceil\sigma/2\rceil)(\sigma+2)q^{\sigma+1+L}.
\]
So after the logarithmic-width part is discarded as polynomial, the low-width
M1 task is exactly to exclude or charge fixed residual-width certificates at
large quotient width.
Fixed residual width has a bounded fully absorbed leaf.  If
\(Z_{S,\lambda}^{\perp}=\emptyset\), \(r=r_\lambda<R_0\), and
\(\emptyset\ne E\subset D_{S,\lambda}\) is an admissible thickening, then
full absorption of \(E\) gives
\[
  Q^{E}_{S,\lambda,E}=R_{S,\lambda},\qquad
  j_E^{\rm abs}=r,\qquad
  N_E^{\rm abs}=N_S-h_\lambda=s_S+r.
\]
The absorbed direction is root-free on that domain, and its primitive packing
factor is
\[
  \left\lfloor{N_E^{\rm abs}\over j_E^{\rm abs}}\right\rfloor
  =1+\left\lfloor{s_S\over r}\right\rfloor\le s_S+1.
\]
Thus at fixed surplus and fixed residual width, the fully absorbed residual
domain is bounded; the large low-width contribution is purely the fixed-root
flag-lift multiplicity inside the dominant projective fiber.  For \(r=1\),
\(R_{S,\lambda}\) is constant and \(Q_{S,\lambda}\) is a scalar multiple of
\(\ell_{D_{S,\lambda}}\).
More precisely, a root-free width-one certificate at a canonical node \(S\) is
equivalent to a bounded exceptional complement
\[
  O\subset D_S^{can},\qquad |O|=s_S+1,
\]
such that \(\operatorname{span}(\ell_{D_S^{can}\setminus O})\subset
\operatorname{span}(P_S,Q_S)\).  Then
\[
  D_{S,\lambda}=D_S^{can}\setminus O,\qquad
  Q_{S,\lambda}=c\ell_{D_{S,\lambda}},\qquad c\ne0.
\]
Two such fibers at the same node are disjoint, so if \(q_S>s_S+2\) there is
at most one.  Its flag contribution is exactly
\[
  \sum_{e=1}^{\lfloor(q_S-2)/2\rfloor}\binom{q_S-1}{e}.
\]
Thus the width-one obstruction is a single co-small projective-fiber cube per
large active node, determined by a bounded exceptional complement.
This cube has a bounded-complement rank test.  The root-free width-one branch
is base-free: a common base root would force every direction, including
\(c\ell_{D_{S,\lambda}}\), to vanish there, contradicting either
root-freeness outside the fiber or non-base membership inside it.  For a
base-free node and \(O\subset D_S^{can}\), \(|O|=s_S+1\), put
\(L_O=\ell_{D_S^{can}\setminus O}\).  Then \(O\) supports a width-one cube
if and only if
\[
  L_O\in \operatorname{span}(P_S,Q_S),
\]
equivalently all \(3\times3\) coefficient minors of \(P_S,Q_S,L_O\) vanish.
In fixed surplus, the number of such tests over the active tree is at most
\[
  (s_0+2)q^{s_0+1}\binom{q+s_0}{s_0+1}.
\]
Thus width-one is a polynomial family of bounded-complement rank tests, each
dangerous only because a single passing test carries the large co-small flag
cube.
Equivalently, at a base-free canonical \(b=2\) node, width-one cubes are exactly
maximal root-shadow directions.  For \(0\ne A\in V_S=\langle P_S,Q_S\rangle\),
put \(Z_S(A)=\{x\in D_S^{can}:A(x)=0\}\).  Since \(\deg A<q_S\),
\(|Z_S(A)|\le q_S-1\).  A width-one cube gives
\(A=c\ell_{D_S^{can}\setminus O}\), so \(|Z_S(A)|=q_S-1\).  Conversely, if
\(|Z_S(A)|=q_S-1\), then with \(O=D_S^{can}\setminus Z_S(A)\) one has
\(|O|=s_S+1\) and \(A=c\ell_{Z_S(A)}\), giving the width-one cube.  Thus the
first low-width obstruction is exactly the existence of a maximal-root
direction in the descended two-dimensional pencil.
It is also a near-constant projective map.  If \(A\) is the maximal-root
direction, \(Z=Z_S(A)\), \(O=D_S^{can}\setminus Z\), and \(B\in V_S\) is
independent of \(A\), then base-freeness gives \(B(x)\ne0\) on \(Z\), so
\([A(x):B(x)]=[0:1]\) for all \(x\in Z\).  For \(y\in O\), \(A(y)\ne0\), so
the exceptional set \(O\) is the only place where the projective map is not on
that large fiber.  Hence
\[
  G_S^{can}=(q_S-1)(s_S+1)+G_O,\qquad
  0\le G_O\le\binom{s_S+1}{2}.
\]
All nonconstant projective information in a width-one obstruction is therefore
confined to the bounded complement \(O\).
The lower-dimensional terminal term is explicit.  Let
\(V_S=\langle P_S,Q_S\rangle\), \(b_S=\dim V_S\), and let
\(D_S^{can}\) be the remaining domain after removing the already peeled roots
and the older unpeeled base roots forbidden by the canonical order.  If
\(b_S=0\), then the leaf contains at most one support.  If \(b_S=1\), choose a
nonzero direction \(Q_S^*\), put
\[
  Z_S=\{x\in D_S^{can}: Q_S^*(x)=0\},\qquad z_S=|Z_S|,
  \qquad N_S=|D_S^{can}|.
\]
Distinct quotient supports can intersect only inside \(Z_S\), so
\[
  |F_S^{can}(A)|\le
  \left\lfloor {N_S-z_S\over q-|S|-z_S}\right\rfloor .
\]
Thus canonical terminal leaves are only singleton zero-direction leaves or the
ordinary one-dimensional root-slice packing already isolated earlier.
Equivalently, for each available root \(x\) the bad-slice test is the failure
of full column rank for the absorbed-anchor matrix
\[
  A_x(U,W)=
  (h^{W,x}_{i+j})_{0\le i<q,\ 0\le j<q-1},
  \qquad h^{W,x}_i=\sum_a(\ell_W(X-x))_a s_{i+a}.
\]
Full column rank for all \(A_x(U,W)\) gives the disjoint residual-packing
bound \(\lfloor |H\setminus(U\cup W)|/q\rfloor\).  The verifier checks this
absorbed-anchor identity while computing the root-slice ranks; in the
largest \(F_7^*\) audit all \(259200\) absorbed matrices have full column
rank.
Let \(Z_1(U,W)\) be the absorbed roots where this rank drops, \(z=|Z_1|\),
and \(N=|H\setminus(U\cup W)|\).  Counting root incidences gives the
fixed-anchor closure bound
\[
  |F(U,W)|
  \le
  \left\lfloor
    { (N-z)+z\binom{N-1}{q-1} \over q}
  \right\rfloor .
\]
Good roots contribute to at most one residual support; bad roots are charged
by the trivial number of \(q\)-subsets through that root.  The verifier asserts
this absorbed-rank bound for every produced anchor with \(q\ge2\); in the
largest \(F_7^*\) audit all absorbed-root tests have \(z=0\), so the checked
case is the disjoint-packing bound.
The absorbed bad-root set also has a finite/persistent dichotomy.  Since
\[
  h^{W,x}_i=h^W_{i+1}-x h^W_i,
\]
the matrix \(A_x(U,W)\) is an affine \(q\times(q-1)\) pencil in \(x\).  If
some maximal minor is not identically zero, then it has degree at most
\(q-1\), so \(|Z_1(U,W)|\le q-1\).  Otherwise every maximal minor vanishes
identically, equivalently the pencil has rank \(<q-1\) over \(F(x)\); this is
the persistent absorbed-rank obstruction.  Thus nonpersistent anchors are
closed by the absorbed-rank bound with \(z\le q-1\), and the only remaining
case is a named persistent low-rank affine Hankel pencil.  The current
\(F_7^*\) audit sees the finite branch with \(z=0\) for every produced anchor.
The persistent branch is certificate-form: it is equivalent to a nonzero
moving kernel
\[
  Q_z(X)=\sum_{a=0}^{q-2} Q_a(z)X^a,\qquad
  \max_a \deg_z Q_a\le q-2,
\]
satisfying the polynomial identity
\[
  H_{q,q+d-1}(s)(\ell_W(X-z)Q_z(X))=0
\]
in \(F[z]^q\).  This is just a right-kernel vector for the affine absorbed
pencil over \(F(z)\), with denominators cleared and the degree bound supplied
by the cofactor construction.  Hence the remaining persistent obstruction is
an explicit bounded-degree moving denominator to classify or charge.
Expanding \(Q_z(X)=\sum_{m=0}^D z^mR_m(X)\) in this identity also gives the
endpoint ladder
\[
  H_{q,q+d-1}(s)(\ell_W X R_0)=0,\qquad
  H_{q,q+d-1}(s)(\ell_W(XR_m-R_{m-1}))=0,
\]
for \(1\le m\le D\), and
\[
  H_{q,q+d-1}(s)(\ell_W R_D)=0 .
\]
Thus any persistent absorbed-rank defect forces a nonzero kernel of the
endpoint map \(R\mapsto H_{q,q+d-1}(s)(\ell_W R)\), \(\deg R<q-1\).  Full
column rank of this endpoint matrix therefore rules out the persistent branch
for that anchor.  The verifier audits this endpoint rank for the produced
deficit anchors with \(q\ge2\); in the largest \(F_7^*\) audit it checks
\(51840\) endpoint matrices and finds no endpoint-rank defects.
Moreover this endpoint obstruction is contained in the residual-direction
obstruction already isolated above.  If
\[
  M(U,W)=(h^W_{i+a})_{0\le i<q,\ 0\le a<q}
\]
is the square residual moment matrix and \(E(U,W)\) is the endpoint matrix,
then \(E(U,W)\) is the first \(q-1\) columns of \(M(U,W)\).  Hence, with
\[
  b(U,W)=q-\operatorname{rank}M(U,W),\qquad
  e(U,W)=(q-1)-\operatorname{rank}E(U,W),
\]
one has \(e(U,W)\le b(U,W)\).  Thus a persistent absorbed-rank defect forces
\(b(U,W)>0\), and after the positive-dimensional fixed-divisor residual kernels
are charged there is no separate persistent absorbed-pencil branch.  The
verifier now asserts this endpoint-prefix identity and nullity inequality on
the same \(51840\) endpoint checks; in the largest \(F_7^*\) audit both
nullities are zero.
This gives the all-frontier closure criterion.  Let
\(\mathcal C_m(s)\) be all split-support certificates of mode size \(m\), and
define
\[
  d(U,r)=\max(0,r-\tau-|U|),\qquad
  P(U,r)=
  \left\lfloor
    {\binom{n-|U|}{d(U,r)}\over\binom{r}{d(U,r)}}
  \right\rfloor .
\]
Then
\[
|\mathcal C_m(s)|
\le
\sum_{r\ge m}\binom{r}{m}
  \sum_{U\in\mathrm{Core}_r(s)} P(U,r).
\]
For fixed \(U,r\), this is just the marked-exit factorization
\(\binom rm\) times the moment-complete or deficit-packing fiber bound.  The
old \(r\le\tau+1\) closure is recovered because nonempty boundary cores have
\(d=0\), while the empty boundary core has \(d=1\).  Hence the remaining
split-support M1 task is to bound the weighted sum over \(r\) of these
deficit-weighted canonical core ledgers after quotient-periodic, tangent, and
endpoint charges.  Uniform \(n^B\) bounds for the inner weighted ledgers give
\(|\mathcal C_m(s)|=O_m(n^{B+m+1})\).
The empty-core exception is exactly the boundary moment map.  For
\(r=\tau+1\), full-marked supports \(S=M(S)\), \(|S|=r\), are precisely the
nonzero \(r\)-sparse representations of
\[
  g=(s_0,\ldots,s_{2r-2}),
  \qquad
  g_i=\sum_{y\in S}a_y y^i,
  \quad
  a_y={b_y(S)\over\prod_{z\in S,\ z\ne y}(y-z)}.
\]
Conversely, any such representation gives the active equation after applying
\(\ell_S\) and gives nonzero marked boundaries after applying
\(\ell_{S\setminus\{y\}}\).  Hence distinct supports in one empty-core
boundary fiber are disjoint, and the fiber size is at most
\(\lfloor n/r\rfloor\).  In the full-domain case \(n=2r\), the only
nontrivial fibers are the root-linear complement pairs
\((Y,\nu y)\leftrightarrow(H\setminus Y,-\nu z)\).  The largest \(F_7^*\)
audit records \(17220\) produced empty-core boundary fibers carrying
\(17280\) labels, maximum fiber size \(2\), with \(60\) complement-pair
checks and \(480\) root-linear produced labels.
For fixed \(A\) and \(m\), this also gives a fixed-anchor fiber bound.  If
\(m\le\tau\), the first \(2m\) moments recover the mode locator and
amplitudes uniquely.  At the boundary \(m=\tau+1\), two supports in one
fiber must be disjoint, and amplitudes are unique after the support is fixed;
hence the fiber size is at most
\[
  \left\lfloor {n-|A| \over m}\right\rfloor .
\]
The verifier audits these fixed-anchor fibers in the largest \(F_7^*\) case:
\(34500\) fibers carry \(34560\) labels with maximum fiber size \(2\), and
the productive subaudit has \(32340\) fibers carrying \(32400\) labels, again
with maximum fiber size \(2\).
The certificate is stable under partial mode absorption.  For
\(E\subset Y\), \(A_E=A\cup E\), and \(Y_E=Y\setminus E\),
\[
  H_{\tau+m-|E|,|A_E|}(s)\ell_{A_E}
  =
  \left(\sum_{y\in Y_E}a_y\ell_E(y)y^i\right)_
        {0\le i\le\tau+m-|E|-1}.
\]
Every proper absorption is nonzero, while \(E=Y\) is exactly the active
support equation.  The verifier checks this for all nonempty mode subsets; in
the largest \(F_7^*\) audit this is \(120960\) absorption checks, with
\(86400\) proper nonzero checks, and \(114480\) productive absorptions with
\(82080\) proper.
Consequently each split-support packet has an intrinsic zero-free mode cube:
for any ordering of \(Y\), the successive boundary scalars after absorbing a
prefix \(E_h\) are
\[
  a_{y_h}\ell_{E_h}(y_h)
  \prod_{z\in Y\setminus E_h,\ z\ne y_h}(y_h-z),
\]
all nonzero.  Thus a size-\(m\) packet contributes \(m!\) ordered mode flags
inside the collapsed split-support chart.  In the largest \(F_7^*\) audit the
verifier records \(86400\) such intrinsic ordered flags, with \(82080\)
productive.
The common-core and two-denominator formulas persist verbatim after this
root-free replacement.  With \(C=\gcd(D:D\in M_{\min})\), \(\gamma=\deg C\),
and \(E_D=D/C\),
\[
  \mathrm{RFArrBudget}_{hw}
  =\sum_{\emptyset\ne I\subseteq M_{\min}}(-1)^{|I|+1}
     \mathrm{RFPhi}_D\!\left(
       h-\gamma-\deg\operatorname{lcm}_{D\in I}E_D\right).
\]
If two minimal denominators survive, this becomes
\[
  \mathrm{RFArrBudget}_{hw}
  =\mathrm{RFPhi}_D(h-\gamma-e_1)
   +\mathrm{RFPhi}_D(h-\gamma-e_2)
   -\mathrm{RFPhi}_D(h-\gamma-e_1-e_2),
\]
with the direct ledger again obtained by adding \(2h\).
There is an analogous bottom-route criterion that does not use the half-window
hypothesis.  For any bottom recurrence kernel \(K\subset F_q[X]_{<h}\), let
\[
  K(-J)=\{Q\in K:Q(\alpha)=0\text{ for all }\alpha\in J\}.
\]
The root-free projective witness count is exactly
\[
  \sum_{J\subseteq D}(-1)^{|J|}\Phi(\dim K(-J)).
\]
If \(K\) is in domain-MDS position, meaning
\(\dim K(-J)=\max(\dim K-|J|,0)\) for every \(J\subseteq D\), this count is
\(\mathrm{RFPhi}_D(\dim K)\).  Hence, if the four bottom residual kernels have
domain-MDS dimensions \(d_F\le L\), the bottom route closes the mixed ladder
with
\[
  \sum_F \mathrm{RFPhi}_D(d_F)+2h
  \le 4\,\mathrm{RFPhi}_D(L)+2h.
\]
This turns the bottom longer-Pade obstruction into a concrete restricted-rank
target on the four bottom kernels, independent of the half-window denominator
arrangement.
The domain-MDS condition itself is the existing root-slice rank condition in
another form.  For \(J\subseteq D\), multiplication by
\(L_J=\prod_{\alpha\in J}(X-\alpha)\) identifies the root slice \(K_0(F)(-J)\)
of each bottom residual family with the stripped Hankel kernel obtained by
replacing \(u,v\) with \(\Delta_Ju,\Delta_Jv\) and reducing the locator degree
by \(|J|\).  Hence
\[
  |K_0(F)^{\mathrm{rf}}|
  =\sum_{J\subseteq D}(-1)^{|J|}\Phi(\dim K_J(F)),
\]
and \(K_0(F)\) is domain-MDS exactly when
\[
  \dim K_J(F)=\max(\dim K_0(F)-|J|,0)
\]
for every root set \(J\).  Thus closing the bottom route is a no-excess
fixed-root/root-slice rank problem, not a separate denominator formalism.
The no-excess condition has a finite top-slice test.  If a bottom kernel
\(K\) has dimension \(d\le |D|\), then it is domain-MDS if and only if no
nonzero \(Q\in K\) vanishes on \(d\) distinct domain points.  Equivalently, for
a basis \(Q_1,\ldots,Q_d\) of \(K\), every \(d\times d\) evaluation determinant
\[
  \det(Q_i(\alpha_j))_{1\le i,j\le d},\qquad
  \{\alpha_1,\ldots,\alpha_d\}\subset D,
\]
is nonzero.  By the root-slice identification, this is the same as vanishing
of the stripped top root-slice kernels \(K_J(F)\) for \(|J|=d_F\).  Thus the
bottom route can be attacked by finite determinant tests on the four bottom
kernels.
For two-dimensional bottom kernels this determinant test has an exact line
count.  If every evaluation functional on \(K\) is nonzero and
\[
  s_K=|\{[\operatorname{ev}_\alpha]\in{\bf P}(K^*):\alpha\in D\}|,
\]
then
\[
  |K^{\mathrm{rf}}|=q+1-s_K
  =\mathrm{RFPhi}_D(2)+(|D|-s_K).
\]
Thus the \(L=2\) bottom route is controlled by collisions of the projective
evaluation-line map \(D\to{\bf P}(K^*)\); injectivity gives the sharp
\(q+1-|D|\) count for that kernel, while repeated evaluation lines are the
entire defect.
More generally, if \(C_K\) is the number of unordered pairs
\(\{\alpha,\beta\}\subset D\) with
\([\operatorname{ev}_\alpha]=[\operatorname{ev}_\beta]\), then
\[
  |K^{\mathrm{rf}}|\le \mathrm{RFPhi}_D(2)+C_K.
\]
The pair collision is equivalent to \(K(-\{\alpha,\beta\})\ne0\), hence to a
nonzero stripped two-root kernel by the root-slice identity.  Therefore an
\(L=2\) bottom route may also be charged by
\[
  4\,\mathrm{RFPhi}_D(2)+C,
\]
where \(C\) is the total two-root-slice collision count over the
two-dimensional bottom families.
Factoring a two-dimensional kernel gives a still more intrinsic control.  If
\(K=\langle Q_0,Q_1\rangle\), \(G=\gcd(Q_0,Q_1)\), \(A=Q_0/G\),
\(B=Q_1/G\), and \(r_K=\max(\deg A,\deg B)\), then each fiber of
\(\alpha\mapsto[A(\alpha):B(\alpha)]\) has size at most \(r_K\).  Therefore
\[
  |K^{\mathrm{rf}}|
  \le \mathrm{RFPhi}_D(2)+(1-1/r_K)|D|,
  \qquad
  C_K\le {r_K-1\over2}|D|.
\]
In particular projective-degree-one bottom kernels have no collision defect
and contribute exactly \(\mathrm{RFPhi}_D(2)\).
The projective degree is controlled by the common factor of the two-dimensional
kernel.  With \(G=\gcd(Q_0,Q_1)\) and \(\gamma_K=\deg G\), either \(G\) has a
domain root, in which case the root-free set is empty, or
\(\gamma_K\le h-2\) and
\[
  r_K\le h-1-\gamma_K.
\]
Thus
\[
  |K^{\mathrm{rf}}|
  \le \mathrm{RFPhi}_D(2)
      +(1-1/(h-1-\gamma_K))|D|,
  \qquad
  C_K\le {h-2-\gamma_K\over2}|D|.
\]
In particular \(\gamma_K\ge h-2\) forces the sharp
\(\mathrm{RFPhi}_D(2)\) count.
The same common-factor mechanism works in every dimension.  If \(K\) has
dimension \(d\), \(G_K=\gcd(Q:Q\in K)\), and \(\gamma_K=\deg G_K\), then either
\(G_K\) has a domain root, so \(K^{\mathrm{rf}}\) is empty, or
\(\gamma_K\le h-d\).  Equality \(\gamma_K=h-d\) forces \(K/G_K\) to be the
full degree-\(<d\) polynomial space, hence
\[
  |K^{\mathrm{rf}}|=\mathrm{RFPhi}_D(d).
\]
Thus the bottom domain-MDS condition is automatic whenever the common factor
is as large as the kernel dimension permits.
More generally, a common factor gives a bottom residual-window bound without
any domain-MDS hypothesis.  If \(m_K=h-\deg G_K\) and \(G_K\) is root-free on
\(D\), then division by \(G_K\) injects \(K^{\mathrm{rf}}\) into the full set
of root-free degree-\(<m_K\) polynomial classes, so
\[
  |K^{\mathrm{rf}}|\le \mathrm{RFPhi}_D(m_K).
\]
Hence, if every bottom residual family has \(h-\deg\gcd(K_0(F))\le L\) after
discarding common factors with domain roots, the bottom route closes with
\[
  4\,\mathrm{RFPhi}_D(L)+2h.
\]
This common-factor bottom route now plugs directly into the ladder bottleneck.
If
\[
  BCF_0=\sum_F b_F,\qquad
  b_F=
  \begin{cases}
  0,&K_0(F)=0\text{ or }\gcd(K_0(F))\text{ has a root in }D,\\
  \mathrm{RFPhi}_D(h-\deg\gcd(K_0(F))),&\text{otherwise},
  \end{cases}
\]
then, after fixed-root/root-slice pieces have been charged, the multiplicative
domain residual ledger can be taken to be
\[
  \min\{BCF_0,\ \mathrm{Charge}(P_{\rm pre})+
  \mathrm{RFArrBudget}_{hw}\}+2h.
\]
Thus the current M1 residual route is reduced to two parallel structural
targets: small bottom common-factor residual windows, or a small root-free
half-window tail arrangement together with the chosen pre-half charge.
The bottom common-factor target has an exact Hankel row-span certificate.  For
a monic \(G\) of degree \(g\), let
\[
  \operatorname{Rem}_G:F_q[X]_{<h}\to F_q[X]_{<g}
\]
be the remainder map modulo \(G\).  If \(A_0(F)\) is one of the four bottom
Hankel matrices defining \(K_0(F)=\ker A_0(F)\), then
\[
  K_0(F)\subset G F_q[X]_{<h-g}
  \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
  \operatorname{row}(\operatorname{Rem}_G)
  \subset \operatorname{row}(A_0(F)),
\]
equivalently
\[
  \operatorname{rank}A_0(F)
  =\operatorname{rank}\begin{bmatrix}A_0(F)\\ \operatorname{Rem}_G\end{bmatrix}.
\]
Thus proving \(h-\deg\gcd(K_0(F))\le L\) is the same as finding a
divisor \(G\) with \(\deg G\ge h-L\) whose remainder rows are already
generated by the bottom Hankel rows.  This turns the bottom route into a
concrete finite-rank certificate problem on the actual M1 kernels.
When \(G\) is squarefree and split,
\[
  G(X)=\prod_{i=1}^g(X-\beta_i),
\]
the certificate has the external-anchor form
\[
  \operatorname{row}(\operatorname{Rem}_G)
  =\operatorname{span}\{(1,\beta_i,\ldots,\beta_i^{h-1}):1\le i\le g\}.
\]
Thus the bottom row-span condition is equivalent to requiring each evaluation
row at the anchors \(\beta_i\) to lie in the bottom Hankel row span.  Repeated
roots give the same statement with Hasse-jet rows.  Hence, for each nonzero
bottom family, the split common-factor route asks for many non-domain
external-anchor rows already generated by its actual bottom Hankel matrix,
unless a domain root empties the corresponding root-free family.
Equivalently, external-anchor membership is common-root duality.  For a bottom
matrix \(A\) with kernel \(K\),
\[
  (1,\beta,\ldots,\beta^{h-1})\in\operatorname{row}(A)
  \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
  Q(\beta)=0\ \text{for every }Q\in K
  \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
  X-\beta\mid\gcd(K).
\]
Thus simple split common factors can be found by the one-row rank test
\[
  \operatorname{rank}A
  =\operatorname{rank}\begin{bmatrix}
      A\\
      (1,\beta,\ldots,\beta^{h-1})
    \end{bmatrix},
\]
with Hasse-jet rows detecting repeated roots.  Domain roots detected by this
test empty the corresponding root-free bottom family rather than contributing
a residual obstruction.
This gives a direct window bound from anchor counts.  If the bottom kernel is
nonzero and its row space has rank \(\rho\) and contains \(g\) distinct
non-domain evaluation rows, then
\[
  g\le \rho,\qquad
  |K^{\mathrm{rf}}|\le \mathrm{RFPhi}_D(h-g).
\]
If \(g=\rho\), the row space is exactly the span of those evaluation rows and
\[
  K=G_BF_q[X]_{<h-g},\qquad
  |K^{\mathrm{rf}}|=\mathrm{RFPhi}_D(h-g),
\]
where \(G_B=\prod_{\beta\in B}(X-\beta)\).  Thus a rank-\(h-L\) bottom Hankel
row space spanned by \(h-L\) non-domain anchor rows gives the exact ideal
\(\mathrm{RFPhi}_D(L)\) contribution.
The Hankel form also turns anchor membership into a moment certificate.  For a
scalar bottom window \(H_{s,h-1}(w)\),
\[
  (1,\beta,\ldots,\beta^{h-1})\in\operatorname{row}H_{s,h-1}(w)
\]
if and only if there is \(C(X)=\sum_{a<s}c_aX^a\) such that
\[
  \sum_{a<s}c_aw_{a+b}=\beta^b\qquad(0\le b<h).
\]
When \(w_m=\sum_{x\in D}\mu_xx^m\), this is the truncated quadrature identity
\[
  \sum_{x\in D}\mu_xC(x)x^b=\beta^b\qquad(0\le b<h).
\]
For paired bottom windows, the same statement uses two short multipliers
\(C_u,C_v\) and the weights \(\mu_xC_u(x)+\nu_xC_v(x)\); for the shifted pair,
replace \((\mu_x,\nu_x)\) by \((x\mu_x,x\nu_x)\).  Thus the bottom anchor
search is an explicit short-multiplier representation of external point
masses by the actual syndrome moments.
In the full-domain moment window \(h=n=|D|\), this becomes a Lagrange degree
test.  For \(\beta\notin D\), let
\[
  \lambda_\beta(x)=\prod_{y\in D,\ y\ne x}{\beta-y\over x-y}
  ={L_D(\beta)\over(\beta-x)L_D'(x)} .
\]
Then the scalar anchor condition is exactly
\[
  \mu_xC(x)=\lambda_\beta(x)\qquad(x\in D),
\]
so, when all \(\mu_x\ne0\), it asks for the interpolant of
\(\lambda_\beta(x)/\mu_x\) to have degree \(<s\).  The paired condition is
\[
  \mu_xC_u(x)+\nu_xC_v(x)=\lambda_\beta(x)\qquad(x\in D),
\]
with the same shifted-weight replacement for the shifted pair.  If \(h\ge n+1\),
no non-domain external anchor can occur from \(D\)-supported moment data,
because the first \(n\) moments force the Lagrange weights and the next moment
misses by \(L_D(\beta)\).
The paired full-domain test also has a dual common-zero form.  Let
\[
W_{\mu,\nu,s}
=\{(\mu_xC_u(x)+\nu_xC_v(x))_{x\in D}:\deg C_u,\deg C_v<s\}.
\]
Its orthogonal annihilator is the space of \(P\in F_q[X]_{<n}\) such that
\[
  \sum_{x\in D}P(x)\mu_xx^a=\sum_{x\in D}P(x)\nu_xx^a=0
  \qquad(0\le a<s).
\]
Then a non-domain \(\beta\) is a paired anchor iff every such \(P\) vanishes at
\(\beta\).  Equivalently, unless this annihilator is zero, the paired anchors
are exactly the non-domain roots of its common gcd.  Thus the full-domain
paired anchor count is reduced to bounding one explicit low-moment common
divisor.
The same duality works in the genuinely short window \(h<|D|\).  Let
\[
  M_h(q)=\left(\sum_{x\in D}q_xx^b\right)_{0\le b<h}
\]
and let \(W_{\mu,\nu,s}\) be the paired short-multiplier value space above.
Then
\[
  (1,\beta,\ldots,\beta^{h-1})\in M_h(W_{\mu,\nu,s})
\]
if and only if every \(P\in F_q[X]_{<h}\) satisfying
\[
  \sum_{x\in D}P(x)\mu_xx^a=\sum_{x\in D}P(x)\nu_xx^a=0
  \qquad(0\le a<s)
\]
also satisfies \(P(\beta)=0\).  Therefore short-window paired anchors are the
non-domain roots of the common gcd of this degree-\(<h\) annihilator space,
unless the annihilator is zero and the truncated moment map is already
surjective.
This gives a bottom-route ledger in terms of four explicit dual gcds.  Define
the scalar annihilators for \(u\) and \(v\) using moments through
\(s_1=t+r_0-1\), and the paired annihilators for \((u,v)\) and \((Su,Sv)\)
using moments through \(s_2=t+r_0-2\), with the shifted pair using weights
\((x\mu_x,x\nu_x)\).  If an annihilator is zero, the corresponding bottom
kernel is zero.  If its common gcd has a domain root, the root-free family is
empty.  Otherwise, if \(a_F\) is the number of distinct non-domain roots of
that common gcd, the family contributes at most
\[
  \mathrm{RFPhi}_D(h-a_F).
\]
Hence the bottom route closes with
\[
  \sum_F \mathrm{RFPhi}_D(h-a_F)+2h,
\]
empty families contributing zero.  In particular, \(a_F\ge h-L\) for all
nonempty root-free families gives \(4\mathrm{RFPhi}_D(L)+2h\).
This dual-gcd root lower bound is extra structure, not a formal consequence
of the moment setup.  If \(|D|=n\ge s+2\) and \(h\ge2\), one can choose a
nonzero weight vector \(\mu\) whose moments \(0,\ldots,s\) vanish.  Then both
\(1\) and \(X\) lie in the scalar annihilator, so its common gcd is \(1\).
Taking \(\mu=\nu\) gives the same obstruction for the ordinary paired
annihilator, and for \(0\notin D\) the shifted paired version follows by
annihilating moments \(1,\ldots,s+1\).  Thus the bottom dual-gcd route must
use special structure of the actual M1 syndromes; dimension counts and moment
formalism alone cannot supply the needed roots.
This obstruction is realized by local Reed-Solomon syndrome data, not only by
formal moment sequences.  In the standard parity-check normalization the
column at \(x\) is \(\lambda_x(1,x,\ldots,x^{r-1})\) with
\(\lambda_x\ne0\), so choosing a supported word with
\(\lambda_xy_x=\mu_x\) gives
\[
  \operatorname{Syn}(y)_m=\sum_{x\in D}\mu_xx^m
\]
on every coordinate lying in the syndrome window.  The same scalar absorption
realizes arbitrary paired weights \((\mu_x,\nu_x)\).  Hence the trivial
dual-gcd examples above can occur as local syndrome windows, although this is
not an active noncontained M1 counterexample and does not show the actual
bottom families are arbitrary.
In the common two-denominator case \(M_{\min}=\{D_1,D_2\}\), writing
\(C=\gcd(D_1,D_2)\), \(D_i=C E_i\), and \(e_i=\deg E_i\), the quotient
denominators \(E_1,E_2\) are automatically coprime and
\[
  \mathrm{ArrBudget}_{hw}
  =\Phi_+(h-\gamma-e_1)+\Phi_+(h-\gamma-e_2)
   -\Phi_+(h-\gamma-e_1-e_2).
\]
The named overlap ledgers remain useful diagnostics:
\[
  \mathrm{OverlapSep}_{hw}
  =\min\{\mathrm{SideHybridOverlap}_{hw},\mathrm{UnionRankOverlap}_{hw}\},
\]
where \(\mathrm{SideHybridOverlap}_{hw}\) is the four one-sided hybrid overlap
sum and the endpoint-pair residual is omitted as a separate hybrid summand
because it is contained in the one-sided overlap union.  These overlap ledgers
are not additional denominator-class charges in the direct raw-tail closure,
since the overlap systems are already subsets of
\(\mathrm{TailUnion}_{hw}\).
Equivalently, the full three-shift common-image branch is endpoint-only.  If
\(\mathrm{GCI}\) denotes the monic split core locators satisfying some
three-shift common-image line, then
\[
\mathrm{GCI}
=E_{\mathrm{deep}}\cup E_\infty\cup\bigcup_{\theta\in\F}E_\theta,
\]
where \(E_{\mathrm{deep}}\) is cut out by
\(H_{t+2,c}(u)L=H_{t+2,c}(v)L=0\), \(E_\infty\) by
\(H_{t+1,c}(u)L=H_{t+1,c}(v)L=0\), and \(E_\theta\) by the two
first-difference equations above.  Thus, once these endpoint-type ledgers have
direction dimension at most \(b\), this whole common-image branch contributes
at most \((q+2)\binom{n}{b}\), with the same root-free \(b/c\) replacement
after common-root core pieces are charged.
Combining this with the globally full core classification gives an endpointized
closure: globally full cores are covered by
\[
U,\ V,\ D_\infty,\ \{K_\lambda\}_{\lambda\in\F},\
\{D_\theta\}_{\theta\in\F},
\]
where \(U,V\) are the two deeper endpoint systems,
\(K_\lambda\) is the deeper fixed-kernel endpoint system for
\(v-\lambda u\), \(D_\infty\) is the ordinary endpoint intersection, and
\(D_\theta\) is the first-difference endpoint intersection.  If all these
endpoint-type charge systems have direction dimension at most \(b\), then
\[
|\mathrm{GlobalFullCore}|\le (2q+3)\binom{n}{b},
\]
again with the root-free \(b/c\) replacement after common-root pieces are
charged.
Combining this endpointized global bound with the non-global anchor-multiplicity
collapse gives the corresponding all-anchor full-core closure:
\[
\begin{aligned}
|\mathcal Z_{\rm all}^{\rm endpoint,\le b}|
&\le
\left(\frac{2q}{m}+2(n-m+1)\right)\binom{n}{m-1}\\
&\quad + q(n-m+1)(2q+3)\binom{n}{b}.
\end{aligned}
\]
After common-root global core pieces are charged, the final term gains the same
factor \(b/c\).  Thus the globally full branch of the all-anchor full-core
component no longer carries the projective common-image factor
\((q^t-1)/(q-1)\); the remaining large-rank work is concentrated in the
endpointized charge systems and the separate nondegenerate shadow ledger.
The first-difference endpoint systems themselves are determinantal.  With
\(\Delta_\theta w=w^+-\theta w\), the stacked direction matrix
\[
J_\theta Q=
\bigl(H_{t+1,c-1}(\Delta_\theta u)Q,\,
      H_{t+1,c-1}(\Delta_\theta v)Q\bigr)
\]
has affine form \(J_\theta=J_+-\theta J_0\).  Hence either at most \(c-b\)
parameters \(\theta\) have direction dimension \(>b\), or the pencil is
persistently low-rank.  In the persistent case both endpoint intersections
\(J_0\) and \(J_+\) have direction dimension \(>b\), and any moving kernel
\(Q(\theta)=\sum_iq_i\theta^i\) satisfies
\[
J_+q_0=0,\qquad J_+q_i-J_0q_{i-1}=0,\qquad J_0q_D=0.
\]
Thus the remaining high-dimensional first-difference charges are finite
exceptions or explicit moving-kernel certificates between endpoint
intersections.
The endpointized finite-exception charge therefore reduces to four base
endpoint checks.  If
\[
\dim\ker H_{t+2,c-1}(u)\le b,\quad
\dim\ker H_{t+2,c-1}(v)\le b,\quad
\dim\ker J_0\le b,\quad
\dim\ker J_+ \le b,
\]
then neither the fixed-kernel pencil nor the first-difference pencil can be
persistent.  Hence the high-dimensional finite-parameter sets satisfy
\[
|\Lambda_{K,>b}|\le c-b,\qquad |\Theta_{D,>b}|\le c-b.
\]
After charging these at most \(2(c-b)\) endpointized exceptional systems,
the preceding global full-core and all-anchor full-core bounds apply to the
remaining ledgers.
The base checks and exceptional parameters have a short-annihilator
certificate form.  Put \(h=c-b\).  Any endpointized matrix on degree-\(<c\)
directions with kernel dimension \(>b\) has a nonzero kernel vector supported
in degrees \(<h\).  Consequently
\[
\Lambda_{K,>b}\subseteq
\{\lambda:\ker H_{t+2,h-1}(v-\lambda u)\ne0\},
\]
and
\[
\Theta_{D,>b}\subseteq
\{\theta:\ker J_\theta^{(h)}\ne0\},
\quad
J_\theta^{(h)}Q=
\bigl(H_{t+1,h-1}(\Delta_\theta u)Q,\,
      H_{t+1,h-1}(\Delta_\theta v)Q\bigr).
\]
Likewise, failure of one of the four base endpoint checks forces a nonzero
kernel in one of
\[
H_{t+2,h-1}(u),\quad H_{t+2,h-1}(v),\quad
J_0^{(h)},\quad J_+^{(h)}.
\]
Thus the next endpoint obstruction is a short-denominator recurrence
certificate, not an unstructured large-kernel event.
The corresponding bad parameters are themselves projective landing images.
For \(Q\ne0\) of degree \(<h\), put
\[
U_h(Q)=H_{t+2,h-1}(u)Q,\qquad V_h(Q)=H_{t+2,h-1}(v)Q.
\]
If there is no common short kernel \(U_h(Q)=V_h(Q)=0\), then every short
fixed-kernel parameter is the unique scalar attached to a projective locator
\([Q]\) with \(U_h(Q)\ne0\) and
\[
\operatorname{rank}[\,U_h(Q)\ V_h(Q)\,]\le1.
\]
The first-difference parameters have the same form with the stacked endpoint
vectors
\[
P_0^h(Q)=
\bigl(H_{t+1,h-1}(u)Q,\ H_{t+1,h-1}(v)Q\bigr),\quad
P_+^h(Q)=
\bigl(H_{t+1,h-1}(u^+)Q,\ H_{t+1,h-1}(v^+)Q\bigr).
\]
Unless \(P_0^h\) and \(P_+^h\) have a common short kernel, each bad
\(\theta\) is the unique scalar for a projective \([Q]\) satisfying
\(\operatorname{rank}[\,P_0^h(Q)\ P_+^h(Q)\,]\le1\).  Thus the endpoint
exceptional sets reduce to projective short-locator landing varieties plus
common short endpoint kernels.
There is also a one-sided injection test.  If either
\[
H_{t+2,h-1}(u)\quad\text{or}\quad H_{t+2,h-1}(v)
\]
is injective on degree-\(<h\) directions, then a nonzero \(h\times h\)
minor of \(H_{t+2,h-1}(v-\lambda u)\) cuts all short fixed-kernel parameters,
so \(|\Lambda_h|\le h\), and hence \(|\Lambda_{K,>b}|\le h\).  Likewise, if
either \(J_0^{(h)}\) or \(J_+^{(h)}\) is injective, then
\(|\Theta_h|\le h\), and hence \(|\Theta_{D,>b}|\le h\).  Any larger short
exception family must therefore already expose short endpoint annihilators on
both sides of the relevant pencil.
Finally, short annihilators split cleanly into fixed-root and root-free parts.
For \(\alpha\in\F\), \((\Delta_\alpha w)_a=w_{a+1}-\alpha w_a\), and if
\(Q=(X-\alpha)R\), then
\[
H_{s,d}(w)Q=H_{s,d-1}(\Delta_\alpha w)R.
\]
Iterating, \(Q=L_A R\) gives
\[
H_{s,d}(w)Q=H_{s,d-|A|}(\Delta_A w)R.
\]
Thus a short fixed-kernel witness with a domain-root factor \(L_A\) becomes a
short witness for the differenced pencil
\[
H_{t+2,h-|A|-1}(\Delta_Av-\lambda\Delta_Au)R=0,
\]
and a first-difference witness becomes the pair of equations for
\(\Delta_A\Delta_\theta u\) and \(\Delta_A\Delta_\theta v\).  The remaining
endpoint obstruction is therefore the root-free short-annihilator family after
fixed-root/root-slice factors have been charged.
The first-difference short parameters can also be viewed as marked roots of
ordinary common endpoint recurrences.  Let
\[
C_h^+=\{P:\deg P\le h,\ H_{t+1,h}(u)P=H_{t+1,h}(v)P=0\}.
\]
Then
\[
\ker J_\theta^{(h)}
\cong \{P\in C_h^+:P(\theta)=0\},
\qquad Q\mapsto (X-\theta)Q.
\]
Hence
\[
\Theta_h=\{\theta:\exists\,0\ne P\in C_h^+,\ P(\theta)=0\}.
\]
Over \(\F_q\), if \(g=\dim C_h^+\), this gives
\[
|\Theta_h|\le \min\left(q,\ h\,\frac{q^g-1}{q-1}\right).
\]
In particular, a one-dimensional common recurrence space contributes at most
\(h\) first-difference parameters.
The short fixed-kernel parameters have the parallel finite/persistent
dichotomy.  For
\[
U_hQ=H_{t+2,h-1}(u)Q,\qquad V_hQ=H_{t+2,h-1}(v)Q,
\]
either some \(h\times h\) minor of \(V_h-\lambda U_h\) is nonzero, in which
case \(|\Lambda_h|\le h\) and hence \(|\Lambda_{K,>b}|\le h\), or every such
minor vanishes identically.  In the persistent case there is a nonzero moving
short kernel \(Q(\lambda)=\sum_i q_i\lambda^i\), and its coefficients satisfy
\[
V_hq_0=0,\qquad V_hq_i-U_hq_{i-1}=0,\qquad U_hq_D=0.
\]
Thus the remaining fixed-kernel obstruction is finite unless it is an explicit
moving short-recurrence certificate between the two short endpoint kernels.
Common root factors in these moving certificates also strip losslessly.  If
\[
Q(\lambda,X)=L_A(X)R(\lambda,X)
\]
is a moving fixed-kernel certificate, then
\[
H_{t+2,h-|A|-1}(\Delta_Av-\lambda\Delta_Au)R(\lambda,X)=0.
\]
Likewise a moving first-difference certificate with the same common factor
strips to the two equations for
\(\Delta_A\Delta_\theta u\) and \(\Delta_A\Delta_\theta v\).  Therefore the
moving-certificate alternatives can be split into fixed-root/root-slice pieces
and common-root-free moving short certificates.
These reductions give a concise closure criterion.  If the four short endpoint
systems
\[
H_{t+2,h-1}(u),\quad H_{t+2,h-1}(v),\quad J_0^{(h)},\quad J_+^{(h)}
\]
are injective, then the four base endpoint checks hold, and
\[
|\Lambda_{K,>b}|\le h,\qquad |\Theta_{D,>b}|\le h.
\]
After charging these at most \(2h\) endpointized exceptional systems, every
remaining endpointized system in the global full-core ledger has direction
dimension at most \(b\), so the endpointized global full-core and all-anchor
full-core bounds apply.
The four short injectivity checks are explicit Hankel-minor targets.  A single
short map \(H_{s,h-1}(w):\F^h\to\F^s\) can be injective only if \(h\le s\),
and for \(h\le s\) the injectivity locus is a nonempty determinantal open
condition.  A stacked pair
\[
Q\mapsto (H_{s,h-1}(a)Q,\ H_{s,h-1}(b)Q)
\]
can be injective only if \(h\le2s\), and for \(h\le2s\) its injectivity locus
is also nonempty determinantal open.  Thus Corollary 53 is dimensionally
available only when \(h\le t+2\) for the two single endpoint checks and
\(h\le2(t+1)\) for \(J_0^{(h)}\) and \(J_+^{(h)}\); outside these ranges the
short-annihilator obstruction is row-count forced.
Equivalently, since \(h=c-b\), this route has the threshold
\[
b\ge b_{\min}:=\max(0,c-(t+2)).
\]
For \(b<b_{\min}\), the single endpoint maps cannot be injective by row count.
For \(b\ge b_{\min}\), all four short checks are dimensionally feasible and
the endpointized charge is reduced to explicit nonvanishing of four
\(h\times h\) Hankel minors plus the finite \(2h\)-exception charge.
Finally, a short Hankel kernel is exactly a denominator recurrence.  If
\(Q(X)=\sum_iq_iX^i\) has degree \(<h\), then
\[
H_{s,h-1}(w)Q=0
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
\sum_i q_i w_{a+i}=0\quad(0\le a<s).
\]
After the root-stripping reductions, the remaining short failures are
\(D\)-root-free denominator recurrences: for fixed-kernel parameters, a
root-free \(Q\) annihilates the combined syndrome \(v-\lambda u\); for
first-difference parameters, the same root-free \(Q\) annihilates both
\(\Delta_\theta u\) and \(\Delta_\theta v\).  Thus the endpoint-short
obstruction is now expressed in the same denominator language as the
residue-line packing problem.
Equivalently, if \(Q(X)=q_0+\cdots+q_eX^e\),
\[
Q^*(T)=q_e+q_{e-1}T+\cdots+q_0T^e,\qquad
W_w(T)=\sum_{a\ge0}w_aT^a,
\]
then
\[
H_{s,h-1}(w)Q=0
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
Q^*(T)W_w(T)\equiv N_{Q,w}(T)\pmod {T^{e+s}},
\qquad \deg N_{Q,w}<e.
\]
Thus the remaining root-free fixed-kernel and first-difference failures are
truncated Pade/residue-denominator certificates for \(v-\lambda u\) and for
the pair \((\Delta_\theta u,\Delta_\theta v)\), respectively.
Moreover, the root-free condition has the expected reciprocal-pole meaning:
for \(\alpha\ne0\),
\[
Q(\alpha)=\alpha^eQ^*(1/\alpha).
\]
Thus \(D\)-root-free \(Q\) is equivalent to \(Q^*\) having no zeros on the
reciprocal domain \(\{1/\alpha:\alpha\in D,\alpha\ne0\}\), together with no
zero at the projective point \(\infty\) when \(0\in D\).  Fixed-root/root-slice
charges are therefore exactly the reciprocal-domain denominator factors
removed before the root-free residue-denominator obstruction remains.
There is also a half-window uniqueness compression.  If
\[
D_i(T)W_w(T)\equiv N_i(T)\pmod {T^{e_i+s}},\qquad \deg N_i<e_i
\quad (i=1,2),
\]
and \(s\ge\max(e_1,e_2)\), then
\[
N_1D_2=N_2D_1.
\]
Indeed the difference is divisible by
\(T^{\min(e_1+s,e_2+s)}\) but has degree \(<e_1+e_2\).  Thus all
order-\(\le s\) denominator certificates for one syndrome series have the
same reduced rational function; componentwise, common certificates for a pair
of series have one primitive vector denominator dividing every common
certificate denominator.  In this range the root-free endpoint-short
obstruction is reduced to primitive reciprocal-domain-pole-free denominators
and root-free multipliers; outside it, the remaining obstruction is a genuine
longer vector-Pade problem.
If the primitive denominator has degree \(\delta\), then every order-\(<h\)
certificate denominator lies in
\[
D(T)=D_0(T)M(T),\qquad \deg M\le h-1-\delta.
\]
Thus, over \(\F_q\), the projective multiplier ledger attached to a fixed
primitive denominator has size at most
\[
1+q+\cdots+q^{h-1-\delta}=\frac{q^{h-\delta}-1}{q-1},
\]
and the root-free and truncation conditions only cut this ledger down.  Hence
the half-window denominator problem is reduced to bounding primitive
reciprocal-domain-pole-free denominators, then paying this explicit multiplier
factor.
The primitive denominator itself still witnesses the obstruction: if
\(D=D_0M\) is a certificate and \(D_0\) is the reduced primitive denominator,
then \(M(0)\ne0\), so \(D_0W-N_0\) is divisible by the same initial power of
\(T\) after cancelling \(M\).  Thus multipliers affect certificate counting,
not existence.  Parameter collisions also collapse to base endpoint charges.
If the same denominator works for two distinct fixed-kernel parameters
\(\lambda_1,\lambda_2\), then subtracting
\[
W_{v-\lambda_1u}-W_{v-\lambda_2u}=(\lambda_2-\lambda_1)W_u
\]
shows that the denominator works for both \(u\) and \(v\) at depth \(t+2\).
Similarly, if the same common denominator works for two distinct
first-difference parameters \(\theta_1,\theta_2\), then
\[
W_{\Delta_{\theta_1}w}-W_{\Delta_{\theta_2}w}
      =(\theta_2-\theta_1)W_w
\]
for \(w=u,v\), so the denominator lies in the base intersection
\(\ker H_{t+1,h-1}(u)\cap\ker H_{t+1,h-1}(v)\).  After charging these base
endpoint intersections, bad parameters inject into primitive denominator
classes.
The remaining primitive denominators have an explicit rank-one landing form.
For an order-\(d\) reversed denominator \(D\), write
\[
R_s(D;w)=([T^d]DW_w,\ldots,[T^{d+s-1}]DW_w).
\]
For fixed-kernel parameters, with
\[
U_D=R_{t+2}(D;u),\qquad V_D=R_{t+2}(D;v),
\]
one has \(D\) supporting \(\lambda\) exactly when \(V_D=\lambda U_D\).
Outside the base endpoint intersection \(U_D=V_D=0\), this is the unique
rank-one landing \(V_D\in \F U_D\).  For first differences, set
\[
B_D=(R_{t+1}(D;u),R_{t+1}(D;v)),\quad
A_D=(R_{t+1}(D;Su),R_{t+1}(D;Sv)).
\]
Then \(D\) supports \(\theta\) exactly when \(A_D=\theta B_D\), and outside
\(B_D=0\) this is the unique landing \(A_D\in\F B_D\).  The entries are linear
in the coefficients of \(D\), so after endpoint charges the remaining primitive
denominator families are explicit \(2\times2\)-minor incidence loci, plus the
root-free open condition.
This covers the whole endpoint-short closure range.  At the threshold
\[
b\ge b_{\min}=\max(0,c-(t+2)),\qquad h=c-b,
\]
one has \(h\le t+2\).  Hence every order-\(<h\) fixed-kernel certificate has
order \(e\le t+2\), and every order-\(<h\) first-difference certificate has
order \(e\le h-1\le t+1\).  Thus all short certificates at the row-count
feasible threshold are in the half-window regime above; the longer vector-Pade
range only appears below the threshold where the short-injectivity route is
already impossible by row count.
Finally, each fixed denominator order has a finite-landing alternative.  In
the projective coefficient space \(P_d\), the rank-one landing equations are
homogeneous quadrics.  Therefore, if the remaining base-free root-free
order-\(d\) landing layer is zero-dimensional, projective Bezout bounds it by
\(2^d\) points.  After endpoint collision charges, bad parameters inject into
primitive denominator classes, so finite order layers contribute at most
\[
\sum_{d=0}^{h-1}2^d=2^h-1
\]
per fixed-kernel or first-difference family.  Thus the unresolved primitive
denominator obstruction is now either this explicit finite ledger or a
positive-dimensional rank-one landing component.
Such a positive-dimensional component is itself certificate-form.  Over the
function field \(K\) of an irreducible component, the generic denominator
\[
D_C(T)=a_0+\cdots+a_dT^d,\qquad a_0\ne0,
\]
is root-free on the reciprocal domain and has a unique parameter: in the
fixed-kernel case \(V_{D_C}=\lambda_CU_{D_C}\), giving
\[
D_CW_{v-\lambda_Cu}\equiv N_C\pmod {T^{d+t+2}},\qquad \deg N_C<d,
\]
and in the first-difference case \(A_{D_C}=\theta_CB_{D_C}\), giving the two
analogous congruences for \(\Delta_{\theta_C}u\) and \(\Delta_{\theta_C}v\)
modulo \(T^{d+t+1}\).  The parameter is either constant, hence a fixed-slice
charge, or genuinely moving.  Thus positive-dimensional primitive landing
components are not opaque: they are moving denominator certificates to
classify or charge.
The constant-parameter subcase is a fixed-slice recurrence charge.  For fixed
\(\lambda\),
\[
K_{d,\lambda}=\{D:R_{t+2}(D;v-\lambda u)=0\}
\]
is a linear recurrence space, and a positive-dimensional constant-\(\lambda\)
component forces \(\dim K_{d,\lambda}\ge2\).  For fixed \(\theta\),
\[
D_{d,\theta}=\{D:R_{t+1}(D;\Delta_\theta u)=
R_{t+1}(D;\Delta_\theta v)=0\}
\]
is the analogous first-difference recurrence space, and a positive-dimensional
constant-\(\theta\) component forces \(\dim D_{d,\theta}\ge2\).  Thus, after
charging high-dimensional fixed-slice recurrence spaces, any remaining
positive-dimensional primitive landing component has genuinely nonconstant
parameter.
Such a nonconstant component descends to a persistent one-parameter kernel.
With an indeterminate \(Z\), define
\[
M_d^K(Z)D=R_{t+2}(D;v-Zu),\qquad
M_d^D(Z)D=(R_{t+1}(D;\Delta_Zu),R_{t+1}(D;\Delta_Zv)).
\]
If a fixed-kernel component has nonconstant parameter, then
\(\ker M_d^K(Z)\ne0\) over \(F(Z)\); if a first-difference component has
nonconstant parameter, then \(\ker M_d^D(Z)\ne0\) over \(F(Z)\).  Conversely,
a nonzero generic kernel vector whose root-free, primitive, order-\(d\), and
base-free opens are not empty gives a positive-dimensional landing family.
Thus after constant fixed-slice charges, the moving branch is exactly a
persistent one-parameter short-recurrence kernel, not an arbitrary
positive-dimensional incidence.
In the row-count feasible range this persistent kernel already forces endpoint
rank failure.  For the fixed-kernel pencil \(M_d^K(Z)=V_d-ZU_d\), vanishing of
all full-column minors over \(F(Z)\) implies that the constant and top-degree
coefficients vanish, hence both \(V_d\) and \(U_d\) have nontrivial kernel.
For the first-difference pencil \(M_d^D(Z)=A_d-ZB_d\), the same argument forces
\(\ker A_d\ne0\) and \(\ker B_d\ne0\).  At the endpoint-short threshold
\(d<h\le t+2\), the row-count hypotheses hold for both pencils.  Therefore the
four short endpoint injectivity checks
\[
H_{t+2,h-1}(u),\quad H_{t+2,h-1}(v),\quad J_0^{(h)},\quad J_+^{(h)}
\]
rule out the persistent moving branch entirely.
The constant-parameter positive slices also have a finite/persistent
dichotomy.  For order \(1\le d<h\), the condition
\(\dim K_{d,\lambda}\ge2\) is equivalent to
\(\operatorname{rank}(V_d-\lambda U_d)\le d-1\), hence is cut out by
\(d\times d\) minors of degree at most \(d\) in \(\lambda\).  Thus either
there are at most \(d\) such \(\lambda\), or the pencil is persistently
rank-deficient and both endpoint maps \(U_d,V_d\) have two-dimensional kernel.
The same statement holds for the stacked first-difference pencil
\(A_d-\theta B_d\).  After charging endpoint maps with two-dimensional short
kernels, constant-parameter positive slices contribute at most
\[
\sum_{d=1}^{h-1}d=\frac{h(h-1)}2
\]
parameters in each family.
Combining these last alternatives gives a finite endpoint-short denominator
ledger under the same four short injectivity checks.  Nonconstant
positive-dimensional primitive landing components are impossible, because they
would be persistent one-parameter kernels and hence would force endpoint rank
failure.  Constant-parameter positive slices contribute at most
\(h(h-1)/2\) parameters per family, while the zero-dimensional primitive
landing layers contribute at most \(2^h-1\).  Thus the
denominator-geometric accounting left after fixed-root, base endpoint, and
collision charges is finite, with per-family bound
\[
  (2^h-1)+\frac{h(h-1)}2,
\]
for bad parameters.  The explicit multiplier ledger attached to each primitive
denominator is needed only when counting certificate witnesses: after base
endpoint collision charges, cancelling a multiplier leaves the same primitive
denominator with the same \(\lambda\) or \(\theta\), and a second parameter for
that primitive denominator would itself be a charged collision.  This packages
the geometry behind the endpoint-short route; it does not improve the direct
\(|\Lambda_{K,>b}|,|\Theta_{D,>b}|\le h\) closure bound that already follows
from short injectivity.

In the ruled part, the common-kernel branch has a fixed projective boundary slope,
while the common-image branch forces the shadow locator \(L_S\) to satisfy the
lower and upper row-cut equations obtained by contracting both Hankel windows
against the annihilator of the common image line.  Thus the boundary residual
is split into a unique-neighbor shadow problem, low-exchange active-codegree
ledgers, and a row-cut resonance problem.

The common-kernel branch sharpens further: if its kernel relation has finite
slope \(z\), then both shifted shadow locators \(\ell_S^0,\ell_S^+\) lie in the
same fixed-slope kernel of \(H(u)+zH(v)\), so the entire external-anchor pencil
is a fixed-slope boundary slice.  The remaining endpoint case is
\(H(v)\ell_S^0=H(v)\ell_S^+=0\), a boundary-contained pencil.

For the common-image row-cut side, full shadow stars are also not new
obstructions: a row cut on degree-\((j-1)\) shadow locators containing the full
star through \(\alpha\in D\) must be the evaluation cut \(L_S(\alpha)=0\).
After these full stars are moved to the fixed-root/root-slice ledger, a
star-free rank-one row-cut packet gains the slice factor
\((j-2)/(j-1)\) over the bare one-root-loss bound.

In the polynomial-field window \(q=|\F|\le n^{B_F}\), this gives a concrete
closure criterion.  After fixed-root/full-star boundary slices have been
charged, if the reduced shadow ledgers for the nondegenerate quadratic branch,
finite common-kernel fixed-slope branch, boundary-contained endpoint branch,
and common-image row-cut branch are polynomially bounded, then
\[
|\mathrm{Boundary}_{\mathrm{off}}^{\mathrm{red}}|
 \le 2|\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{quad}}|
  + q\bigl(|\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{ker}}|
          +|\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{cont}}|
          +|\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{img}}|\bigr)
\]
is polynomial.  This does not prove those shadow-ledger estimates; it records
that the remaining boundary-off multiplicity is at worst the field-size factor
over the three ruled shadow images and a factor \(2\) over the nondegenerate
shadow image.

The common-image shadow ledger is now rank-testable.  For each projective image
line \(I\subset \F^t\), the lower/upper Hankel contractions define an affine
row-cut system on monic degree-\((j-1)\) shadow locators.  If its direction
dimension is \(d_I\), then the number of squarefree \(D\)-split shadows in that
fixed image-line stratum is at most \(\binom{n}{d_I}\).  Hence
\[
|\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{img}}|
  \le \sum_I \binom{n}{d_I}.
\]
In bounded slack and polynomial field size, the common-image branch is therefore
closed by bounded direction dimension; the only remaining obstruction in this
branch is the low-rank image-line locus.

The finite common-kernel and endpoint-contained ruled ledgers admit the same
rank certificate.  For a finite slope \(z\), the conditions
\((H(u)+zH(v))\ell_S^0=(H(u)+zH(v))\ell_S^+=0\) cut out an affine row-cut
system on monic degree-\((j-1)\) shadow locators; if its direction dimension is
\(d_z\), then that fixed-slope shadow stratum has size at most
\(\binom{n}{d_z}\).  The endpoint branch is the same statement with \(H(v)\)
and dimension \(d_\infty\).  Thus the whole ruled part of the reduced
boundary-off ledger is bounded by
\[
q\left(\sum_z\binom{n}{d_z}+\binom{n}{d_\infty}
       +\sum_I\binom{n}{d_I}\right),
\]
so every ruled branch is now expressed as an explicit Hankel row-rank ledger.
For finite slopes this low-rank locus has a one-variable determinantal
dichotomy: fixing a threshold \(b<m=j-1\), either the set of finite slopes with
direction dimension \(d_z>b\) has size at most \(m-b\), or every
\((m-b)\times(m-b)\) minor of the row-cut matrix vanishes identically in \(z\).
Thus a large finite-slope low-rank obstruction must come from a persistent
low-rank Hankel pencil, not from many unrelated exceptional slopes.
Equivalently, at any fixed threshold \(b\), if the persistent alternative is
absent and the at most \(m-b\) exceptional slopes are charged to the
fixed-slope/root-slice ledger, then the bounded-rank finite common-kernel
residual contributes at most
\[
q^2\binom{n}{b}.
\]
For bounded \(b\) and \(q\le n^{B_F}\), this closes that residual
polynomially and leaves only the explicit exceptional fixed-slope charge.
The identically persistent alternative is also certificate-form: over the
rational function field \(\F(z)\), vanishing of every
\((m-b)\times(m-b)\) minor is equivalent to the existence of \(b+1\)
independent polynomial vectors \(Q_i(z,X)\), \(\deg_X Q_i<m\), with
\[
(H(u)+zH(v))\ell_{Q_i(z)}^0=(H(u)+zH(v))\ell_{Q_i(z)}^+=0
\]
as polynomial identities in \(z\).  The remaining persistent case is therefore
an explicit moving-kernel certificate to classify or rule out.
Moreover, persistence forces endpoint low rank: the constant and degree-\(r\)
coefficients of the \(r\times r\) determinantal minors show that the \(H(u)\)
and \(H(v)\) endpoint row-cut systems both have direction dimension \(>b\),
where \(r=m-b\).  Equivalently, if either endpoint has direction dimension at
most \(b\), the persistent alternative is impossible and the finite-exception
closure applies.  A moving kernel \(Q(z)=\sum q_i z^i\) also gives the ladder
\[
R_uq_0=0,\qquad R_uq_i+R_vq_{i-1}=0,\qquad R_vq_D=0,
\]
so persistent finite-slope degeneracy starts and ends in endpoint kernels.
The common-image low-rank locus has an analogous projective form.  For an
image line \(I=[y]\subset\F^t\), the direction space is the kernel of
\[
Q\longmapsto
\bigl(y\wedge H(u)\ell_Q^0,\ y\wedge H(u)\ell_Q^+,
      y\wedge H(v)\ell_Q^0,\ y\wedge H(v)\ell_Q^+\bigr).
\]
Thus the locus \(d_I>b\) is cut out in \(\mathbb P^{t-1}\) by homogeneous
\((m-b)\times(m-b)\) minors of degree \(m-b\).  Outside this projective
low-rank locus, the common-image contribution is bounded by
\[
q\,\frac{q^t-1}{q-1}\binom{n}{b},
\]
while the fully persistent case is an explicit moving-image wedge certificate.
Combining the finite-slope, endpoint, and common-image estimates gives the
ruled closure ledger: after charging finite slopes with \(d_z>b\), projective
image lines with \(d_I>b\), and assuming \(d_\infty\le b\), the remaining ruled
targets are bounded by
\[
\left(q^2+q+q\,\frac{q^t-1}{q-1}\right)\binom{n}{b}.
\]
Thus bounded \(b,t\) and polynomial field size close the bounded-rank ruled
residual; what remains is only the explicit fixed-slope, projective low-rank,
or moving-kernel certificate loci.

There is also a root-free sharpening of these row-rank ledgers.  Let
\(m=j-1\) and let \(P_0+V\) be one affine row-rank stratum with
\(\dim V=d\).  After common-root subledgers have been charged to the
fixed-root/root-slice ledger, incidence counting through roots gives
\[
|\mathrm{Sh}(P_0+V)|\le \frac{n}{m}\binom{n-1}{d-1}
  =\frac{d}{m}\binom{n}{d}
\]
for \(d\ge1\), with the old bound \(1\) kept when \(d=0\).  Thus in the
bounded-rank range \(1\le b\le n/2\), the root-free ruled closure replaces the
bare factor \(\binom{n}{b}\) above by \((b/m)\binom{n}{b}\).  This does not
remove the exceptional low-rank loci, but it shows that the bounded-rank
residual carries a genuine fixed-root saving once common-root pieces are
charged.

\textbf{Status.}  This is a proved local packet lemma under the Hankel-pencil
normal form and its non-fixed variable-line hypotheses.  It is not the final
all-line M1 theorem and does not add a leaderboard row.

\subsection{Challenge-map pullbacks and public status corrections}

The adjacent-ledger update adds the missing pullback rule from slope ledgers to
protocol challenge maps.  For a challenge-to-slope map \(\phi:K\to Z\), a bad
set \(B\subseteq Z\) pulls back with probability
\[
  \frac{|\phi^{-1}(B)|}{|K|}.
\]
If the nonempty fibers have sizes \(s_1\ge s_2\ge\cdots\), the adversarial
envelope for \(|B|=L\) is \((s_1+\cdots+s_L)/|K|\).  For an \(\F_q\)-linear map
of rank \(r\), the effective denominator is \(q^r\), not the ambient challenge
space size.  This prevents accidental multiplication of denominators in protocol
ledgers.

The public-board status corrections are:
\[
\begin{array}{c|c|c|c}
\text{row} & a & \text{tangent floor} & \text{status}\\
\hline
\text{Cycle116 gate} & 262 & 251 & \text{gate unconditional; exact count conditional}\\
\text{Cycle119 gate} & 263 & 250 & \text{gate unconditional; exact count conditional}\\
\text{reserve272} & 272 & 241 & \text{proved but subsumed}\\
\text{reserve288} & 288 & 225 & \text{proved but subsumed}\\
\text{reserve313} & 313 & 200 & \text{proved but subsumed}
\end{array}
\]
The exact Cycle84 numerator \(52{,}747{,}567{,}092\) remains a
computation-dependent record; only the weaker \(>2^{-128}\) gate follows from
the generic tangent floor without that finite census.
